\paragraph{\wtag\ A component lead from the review.} The review proposes to
keep the observation formula of Section~\ref{ptr:sec:calculus}, after exact
lane projection, as a shared Boolean DAG and to compile \emph{membership at
a supplied lane index} without expanding signed indicator products. Its
atoms are affine inequalities and fixed-modulus congruences; the
programme's five-witness congruence component
(\path{presburger_congruence_five.md} in
\path{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem/Papers/research-wip/native-stream-queue/},
whose standalone 21-operation sum of squares excludes evaluation of the
affine input, truth-output use and outer composition) supplies canonical
truth and falsehood for congruences. An inequality truth bit $\chi=[\Lambda\ge0]$
of an affine form $\Lambda$ takes two natural coordinates $\chi,\eta$
and the residuals
\[
 \chi(\chi-1),\qquad \Lambda-(2\chi-1)\eta-\chi+1,
\]
which force $\eta=\Lambda$ when $\chi=1$ and $\eta=-\Lambda-1$
otherwise; AND/OR/NOT gate values then have quadratic or affine residuals
with unique values. (The review writes $b=[L\ge0]$ with coordinates $b,s$;
the letters are changed here because $b$, $L$ and $s$ already have
meanings in this report.) This is an \emph{unimplemented} paid-component
lead, not a saving or a bound: a paid compiler must still charge every
affine form, atom, gate, truth-output use, square and final sum. It does
\emph{not} certify first-hit minimality (the global count and minimum of
Proposition~\ref{ptr:prop:min} would need a separate certificate); it does
not certify arbitrary supplied lane tables (the cycle, defect-ownership and
no-earlier-collision checks of Section~\ref{ptr:sec:algorithm} remain
necessary); and it is not an ordinary-input universal loader. The review's
suggested next task is a bounded literal DAG compiler for one nontrivial
clause of this report, with a full natural-fibre proof and a paid
finalizer, compared against its expanded-atom form.

\paragraph{\wtag\ Neighbouring reports.} No other report of the collection
treats turmites, Langton's ant or first revisits. Research Report~35
(batch-83 manuscript~07), placed as source~22 of Part~XX of the collection's
report \emph{canonical-diophantine-certificates}~\cite{cdcreport}, supplies
for abelian sandpiles what question~3 below asks for turmites: a literal
periodic background, a finite input loader and an acceptance event (finite
total activity) equivalent to halting of a fixed universal machine. It
shares no theorem with this report.

\bnote\ The first sentence remains true of the \emph{other} reports of the
collection, but this report now treats Langton's ant itself: Parts II--IV
(Reports 40, 42, 44, 47 and 48) give the literal periodic Langton ant for U15
and its Diophantine certificate. They are the turmite counterpart of
Report~35 and of the certificate built on it in the same Part~XX: in both, a
literal periodic substrate, a finite loader for the same fixed machine U15
and an acceptance event equivalent to its halting. The constructions share
no theorem.
\par}

Several focused next questions remain.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Observation complexity.} Bound term growth for restricted stencils or congruence families, or devise a more compact representation that avoids distributive blowup while preserving exact count and minimum. A polynomial observation result would require a separate proof.
\item \textbf{Sharper first revisit bounds.} Improve the deliberately loose $T_*$ and $O(N_{\mathrm{in}}^7)$ estimates, determine tight parameter dependencies, and separate decision from certificate construction complexity.
\item \textbf{A literal two visit interface.} Supply explicit periodic colour coordinates, a finite input loader, exhaustive gadget visit accounting, and an iff acceptance port in the stated observation class. This is needed before asserting a sharp threshold in a common formal model.
\item \textbf{Independently checkable certificates.} Formalize the cycle, truncation, intersection, and colour reconstruction claims in a small proof checker or proof assistant. The current standard library replay checks are strong finite evidence, not machine checked proofs of all inputs.
\item \textbf{Model changes.} Investigate succinct tiles, other lattices or turning rules, multiple heads, and restricted post-repeat dynamics separately. The permutation, fresh-cell, and bounded-defect arguments must be re-established rather than assumed to transfer.
\end{enumerate}

{\small\wtag\ All five questions are open at the write. For question~3, the
sandpile analogue of Report~35 is cited in the remark on neighbouring
reports above; it does not transfer to turmites. For question~4, the
repository formalizes none of the cycle, truncation, intersection or colour
reconstruction claims; the review's Boolean-DAG lead above concerns
Diophantine occurrence certificates, not first-hit minimality.

\bnote\ \textbf{Question~3 is answered} by Part~\ref{ptr:part:la}, for the
binary RL ant (Langton's ant) and the fixed machine U15:
Theorem~\ref{ptr:la:thm:interface} supplies explicit periodic colour
coordinates (periods $576000$ and $481238074400$, a total colour query), a
finite input loader with exactly $2812+4(\pop\ell+\pop r)$ overrides,
exhaustive gadget visit accounting (Lemma~\ref{ptr:la:lem:local},
Proposition~\ref{ptr:la:prop:physical}, Theorem~\ref{ptr:la:thm:global}: at
most two visits per cell over the whole infinite run) and an iff acceptance
port in this report's observation class, a heading and two coordinate
congruences (Theorem~\ref{ptr:la:thm:accept}). Its
Corollary~\ref{ptr:la:cor:boundary} then asserts the sharp threshold in the
common model, and Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:lower} checks that the models match.
The answer depends on the published U15 encoding of Neary and Woods for
undecidability, and it does not cover other rules, blank backgrounds or other
observation classes. Parts~\ref{ptr:part:cert} and~\ref{ptr:part:src} carry
it into arithmetic, with the scope of item~\ref{ptr:it:scope} of the preface
to the four Parts. Questions 1, 2, 4 and 5 remain open; Report~40 also names a
proof-assistant formalization of its finite-history composition as a next
step (Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:conclusion}).\par}

\section{Reader guide to the reproducible materials}\label{ptr:app:guide}
The report source and PDF are \code{Research_Report38.tex} and \code{Research_Report38.pdf}. The frozen packet's main scientific entry points are:
\begin{itemize}
\item \code{boundary-context/proof.md}: original total decision, Presburger formulation, eventual periodicity, and universality scope
\item \code{PROOF.md}: the complete specialized first hit set calculus and colour compiler
\item \code{complexity-review.md}: the numerical and intermediate bit bounds for first revisit
\item \code{review.md}: independent implementation and mathematics audit, including the resolved iterator hazard
\item \code{example.py} and \code{example-result.json}: the exact first hit at $2\cdot10^{100}$
\end{itemize}
Use the enclosing release's instructions and verifier rather than executing historical context scripts. Python 3.10 or later is required by the source syntax; a local pdfLaTeX installation and standard TeX packages are needed only to rebuild the PDF. The scientific algorithms themselves use the Python standard library. No online service is required for replay.

\section{Selected boundary cases and why they are covered}\label{ptr:app:cases}
\begin{longtable}{@{}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.26\textwidth}>{\raggedright\arraybackslash}p{0.69\textwidth}@{}}
\toprule
Case & Reason the algorithm remains exact\\
\midrule
\endhead
Repeated position with a new heading & Spatial equality is the only revisit criterion; headings are used for dynamics and optional query restrictions\\[4pt]
Defect at the starting point & Its first arrival has $F=0$ and is processed before its departure unless a genuine earlier collision exists, which is impossible at time zero\\[4pt]
Defect tied with a repeat & Collision wins at $C=F$; its repeated arrival is included, and the subsequent turn is not used\\[4pt]
Move from a defect directly to history & The next restart's local index zero is checked against all history\\[4pt]
Redundant or unreachable defect & Redundant overrides are harmless fresh events; unreachable defects generate no candidate and do not obstruct the final certificate\\[4pt]
Zero drift or stationary relation & Rank zero handles stationary matches; a zero drift cycle necessarily supplies a repeat\\[4pt]
Negative drift and parameter direction & Floor and ceiling bounds retain signs; negative parameter step reverses which endpoint gives the first projected index\\[4pt]
Equal-time lane matches & The integer minus one in strict chronology excludes the current arrival and future arrivals\\[4pt]
One colour palette & Increment is modulo one; the two initial colour alternatives coincide, and the Boolean visited split remains valid\\[4pt]
Empty or contradictory query & Empty clause union is false, an unrestricted empty clause is true, and contradictory stencil entries have empty intersection\\[4pt]
Huge first hit or large CRT product & Endpoints, periods and counts are exact integers; neither the trajectory nor the complete residue period is expanded\\[4pt]
A query beyond first revisit & The certified domain ends at the repeated arrival. No claim about a later board is supplied\\
\bottomrule
\end{longtable}

\part{A literal periodic Langton ant and the one/two-visit boundary (Report 40)}\label{ptr:part:la}
\ptrstart{40}{Part II: Report 40}
\phantomsection\label{ptr:la:sec:intro}
\noindent\wtag\ \textbf{Source.} \emph{A Literal Periodic Langton Ant Interface
and the One Visit Two Visit Boundary. Exact finite templates and a fixed U15
accepting port}, Research Report~40, dated 3 October 2026, author line
``Mathematical research report''; batch-88 manuscript~03, archive
\path{Literal_Periodic_Langton_Ant_and_Visit_Boundary_Package.zip}; pin
\texttt{5883b08b7} (its bibliography entry~\cite{ptr:la:arithmetic}), the
same as Part~I's. Its sections, statements and equations are numbered 40.$k$
here; its labels carry \texttt{ptr:la:} and its shipped files the prefix
\texttt{02-literal-ant-}. It answers question~3 of Part~I
(Section~\ref{ptr:sec:consequences}) and proves the threshold Report~38
declined to claim. Report~40's archive embeds Report~38 unchanged as its
lower-side companion; that companion is Part~I of this report, and its files
are shipped once, under Part~I's names. The programme reviewed the report in
commit \texttt{cfbe37de7}~\cite{ptr:w:rev4048} and found no defect in what it
read; it did not regenerate the physical templates or the atlas. The scope of
the arithmetic that later Parts build on this ant is item~\ref{ptr:it:scope}
of the preface to the four Parts.

\begin{quote}\small\noindent\textbf{Abstract (Report 40).} We specify a single doubly periodic binary Langton ant background, a fixed head start, and a finite input perturbation that simulates the fixed fifteen-state two-symbol machine U15. The background has rectangular periods $576000$ and $481238074400$. For finite nearest-head-first tape words $\ell,r$, the loader changes exactly $2812+4(\pop\ell+\pop r)$ cells, including a fixed 2806-cell startup patch. A fixed pre-departure residue-and-heading event occurs exactly when the simulated machine reaches its undefined transition J1. Every cell is visited at most twice over the entire infinite ant trajectory, including after simulated halting. We give the finite-history composition argument, a complete 862-NAND cellular rule, an exact arithmetic grammar for 601547591 physical instruction rounds, the growing-layer and long-wire proofs, and the accepting-port exclusivity argument. Together with the complete one-visit decision and observation theorem in Report 38, this yields a sharp one-versus-two-visit boundary for reachability in the precisely stated fixed-periodic-background model. The qualitative universality and two-visit principle are due to Maldonado and coauthors; the arbitrary-program-to-U15 encoding remains a published dependency. Neither a dense giant board nor the expanded instruction list is claimed to have been constructed. The accompanying archive includes all literal numerical maps, own-code generators, proofs, and receipts, with authenticated isolated replay and explicit limits on what those checks establish.
\end{quote}
\section{The result and its exact scope}\label{ptr:la:sec:result}
The object of this report is a complete interface between a fixed source computation and one fixed physical ant, rather than a collection of truth-table gadgets without a controller. The interface includes the initial head, a finite list of changed colours, an effective colour query at every lattice site, and a specified physical event whose occurrence has both a soundness and a completeness proof. The controller keeps running after the simulated halt.

\begin{theorem}[Literal periodic interface]\label{ptr:la:thm:interface}
There is a fixed binary colouring $b:\Z^2\to\{0,1\}$ with periods
\[
 S=576000,\qquad 2V=481238074400,
\]
and an explicit computable map $I$ from pairs of finite binary words to finite colour overrides, with the following properties. Start the colour-zero-right, colour-one-left ant at
\[
 p_0=(288650,75),\qquad h_0=E
\]
on $b$ modified by $I(\ell,r)$. The source machine starts in state A reading zero, with nearest-head-first words $\ell$ and $r$ on its two sides and zero elsewhere.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The override support has exactly $2812+4(\pop\ell+\pop r)$ sites
\item At every site there are at most two visits in the whole infinite ant run
\item U15 reaches J1 if and only if a pre-departure head state has heading S and residue, modulo $(S,2V)$, in
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:ports}
 \{(546702,240606225650),\ (258702,481225262850)\}
\end{equation}
\item On the valid primary-loaded runs just specified, the first residue clause never occurs; the second clause alone gives the same equivalence
\end{enumerate}
No colour stencil is part of the accepting event. The colouring, all overrides, and both residue clauses are independent of a simulated running time bound.
\end{theorem}
The proof is distributed across the construction, composition, initialization, and observation sections below. In particular, item 2 is an all-history theorem; it is not inferred from bounded simulation.

The distinction between the implemented U15 interface and its universality dependency matters. The included table is an exact fixed source machine. Its 30 table entries, one undefined, are fully simulated. The reduction from an arbitrary program to the appropriate finite U15 tape encoding is the published construction of Neary and Woods~\cite{ptr:la:neary}; it is not a second compiler implemented by this packet. Invoking that dependency gives undecidability of the indicated accepting-port problem. The literal pair-to-ant loader is implemented here.

\begin{corollary}[Precisely aligned visit boundary]\label{ptr:la:cor:boundary}
For the binary RL ant on fixed doubly periodic backgrounds with finite perturbations and a fixed residue-and-heading acceptance predicate, globally at-most-one-visit reachability is decidable. There is a fixed background, fixed start, fixed predicate, and computable family of finite perturbations whose trajectories are globally at-most-two-visit and whose reachability is halting-equivalent. Thus one visit cannot support a halting reduction with this observable, whereas two visits suffice, subject to the stated published U15 encoding dependency.
\end{corollary}
Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:lower} checks the model match explicitly. This is a sharp boundary for this decision model, not a statement that every two-visit run is universal, nor a threshold for every conceivable output interpretation.

\paragraph{Prior result and contribution.}
Maldonado, Gajardo, Hellouin de Menibus, and Moreira give periodic-background universality and the two-visit construction principle~\cite{ptr:la:maldonado}. These qualitative results are credited to them. This report makes one fixed implementation literal through numerical cell maps, history-aware interfaces, source-machine compilation, global geometry, input anchoring, and exclusive observation. No novelty or priority theorem is asserted. The construction uses an infinite periodic background; it gives no finite blank-background universality, physical stopping event, or return-to-a-fixed-absolute-site detector.

\section{Dynamics and evidence conventions}\label{ptr:la:sec:model}
Coordinates increase east and south. Headings $N,E,S,W$ have codes $0,1,2,3$ and unit vectors
\[
 d_0=(0,-1),\quad d_1=(1,0),\quad d_2=(0,1),\quad d_3=(-1,0).
\]
At time $t$, the snapshot $(p_t,h_t,c_t)$ is taken before departure. If $a=c_t(p_t)$, set
\[
 h_{t+1}=h_t+1-2a\pmod4,\qquad
 c_{t+1}(p_t)=1-a,\qquad p_{t+1}=p_t+d_{h_{t+1}},
\]
leaving all other colours unchanged. A visit means an arrival snapshot, including time zero. Because the physical dynamics never stop, every visit has one departure; an all-time departure bound is therefore an all-time visit bound.

An owned support is the finite set of cells that a template supplies, including supplied colour-zero cells. Unowned cells are not automatically forbidden terrain; the global default is zero. A segment ends on its specified terminal head state \emph{before processing that cell}. Consequently the terminal cell is owned and processed by the next segment when it starts. Counting both the upstream endpoint and the downstream entry as processed cells would be an error.

There are four kinds of evidence, with different logical force:
\begin{enumerate}
\item A finite cell map, explicit history, and step-by-step interpreter establish a finite template fact
\item Complete truth-table evaluation establishes equality of two finite Boolean functions
\item Arithmetic interval and ownership proofs cover all rows, coordinates, and histories, including infinite families
\item Hashes and replay receipts establish byte identity and repeatability of specified computations, not truth of a theorem by themselves
\end{enumerate}
The article supplies ordinary mathematical proofs with independently replayed finite certificates. It is not a proof-assistant formalization. Historical component status strings are retained byte-for-byte: a record saying ``Boolean cell only'' still has that local scope even though the enclosing construction now supplies the missing geometry.

\section{Literal primitive maps and legal histories}\label{ptr:la:sec:primitives}
Seven finite numerical maps form the source basis: a straight cable, two turns, one bit box, two ordered crossings, and a union. The maps were extracted as numerical unit-square fills from the seven named MetaPost assets of~\cite{ptr:la:maldonado}; no upstream PostScript, MetaPost, TeX, or other source program was executed. The complete extracted maps and their independent histories are retained as pure data. Appendix~\ref{ptr:la:app:maps} prints every primitive row, and the archive retains the exact coordinates, halo cells, intermediate boards, traces, and asset hashes.

\Needspace{18\baselineskip}
\subsection{Ports and storage state}
\input{data/02-literal-ant-figures-primitive-ports.tex}
The port coordinates are relative to each primitive's normalized origin. In the box, INIT0 is the printed map. INIT1 changes exactly $(4,4)$ and $(5,4)$ from colour one to zero. READ enters at $(3,9,N)$; its exit is $(-1,6,W)$ for zero and $(9,6,E)$ for one. WRITE enters at $(2,0,S)$ and exits at $(7,-1,N)$. WRITE is the operation that changes an INIT0 logical box to the written-one form. A logical zero output is realized by omitting WRITE on a fresh INIT0 box.

The legal box histories are
\[
 \epsilon,\quad \mathrm{WRITE},\quad \mathrm{READ0},\quad
 \mathrm{WRITE};\mathrm{READ1}
\]
from INIT0, and $\epsilon,\mathrm{READ1}$ from INIT1. The total departure counts in the four nonempty cases are respectively $18,19,41,23$. READ1 from INIT1 already has cells departed from twice. Thus the proof never relies on every individual primitive traversal being self-avoiding.

For each crossing the allowed history is $\epsilon$, FIRST, or FIRST followed by SECOND. The first and second ports are the exact ones in the table. A union takes exactly one of its two alternatives, not both sequentially. Straight and turning cable pieces are used once. The catalogue includes explicit failed histories: READ0 followed by WRITE does not yield the specified write exit, and SECOND before FIRST exits incorrectly. Those are actual interface restrictions, not interchangeable Boolean operations.

\begin{lemma}[Finite history verification]\label{ptr:la:lem:local}
For the supplied seven maps, each legal history reaches every indicated terminal head state, has the recorded intermediate board, and departs from no cell more than twice in aggregate. INIT0/INIT1 changes and each optional read are included in that aggregate. Cable pieces have maximum one.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For each history start with its explicit map and initialization, and apply the recurrence of Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:model} until the next specified terminal state. At each step the current coordinate must be in the owned support, its listed colour determines a unique new heading, and the colour is flipped once. Concatenate the operations on the \emph{same} resulting board. Compare each generated transition, endpoint, final board, and per-cell count with the catalogue. There are two histories for each cable or corner, six for the box, three for each crossing, and three for the union. These finite lists are exhaustive for the stated interface languages. Taking the maximum over each listed count map gives one for cables and corners and two for the other resources. This finite computation is fully specified by the displayed maps and recurrence; complete traces are included for independent inspection.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Arbitrary even cable lengths}
A horizontal cable of even longitudinal length $L$ uses two lanes, colours zero above and one below, translated so its entry heading is east. Its local two-column trajectory is a four-step zigzag. After those four steps the head has moved two cells east, regained heading E and the same lane, and used each of the four cells once. Induction on $L/2$ proves correct exit and one departure per cell. Rotation by a quarter-turn preserves the rule and gives vertical and westward cables. This lemma is used for enormous but exactly specified long strips; it does not require materializing each site.

\subsection{Shared box ownership}
An output box of one stage can coincide with an input box of the next. The allowed intersection is the \emph{whole 43-cell box with agreeing initial colours}. It has one physical owner and a single chronological history. Naming the box in two templates does not create two boxes. Its projected history is at most one optional WRITE and then one READ. A proof that added ``two visits in the upstream gadget'' to ``two visits in the downstream gadget'' would only give four and would lose the real invariant. Instead the merged box history is checked directly by Lemma~\ref{ptr:la:lem:local}. No triple painter or partial-box alias is admitted.

\section{A complete physical instruction basis}\label{ptr:la:sec:instructions}
Columns have pitch 600. A full physical round has old boxes near $y=50$, new boxes near $y=450$, an eastward control sweep at $y=75$, and a westward sweep at $y=305$. All output boxes begin as fresh INIT0 resources. The four adjacent-pair operations are
\begin{align}
 \NAND(p,q)&=(1-pq,0),&\DUP(p,q)&=(p,p),\label{ptr:la:eq:pair}\\
 \ML(p,q)&=(q,0),&\MR(p,q)&=(0,p).\nonumber
\end{align}
A delayed-COPY tile handles every unselected column and preserves its input. In DUP and the two moves, the ignored input may be either bit and may already have received a WRITE; it is not read. This is why unknown workspace contents do not invalidate the later word compiler.

The physical NAND template is a NAND macro followed by two normalized lower COPY pieces. NOT and ordinary COPY use a 200-unit half-round; delayed COPY and all pair instructions use a 400-unit complete round. The pair instruction replaces both consumed delayed-COPY slots exactly once. Its input/output ports and all translated primitive placements and routes are literal numerical data.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lrr}
\toprule
Resource & Owned cells & Height convention\\\midrule
NOT & 3064 & half-round\\
COPY & 3134 & half-round\\
Delayed COPY & 5664 & full round\\
DUP & 10604 & full round\\
MOVE LEFT & 10550 & full round\\
MOVE RIGHT & 10318 & full round\\
NAND with lower copies & 13197 & full round\\
Right marker joint & 6269 & exceptional joint\\
Left marker joint & 6193 & exceptional joint\\
Right and left row turns & 684 and 564 & full-round connectors\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
These are support sizes, not independent allowances that may be summed at shared cells.

\begin{proposition}[Physical instruction contract]\label{ptr:la:prop:physical}
With the declared chronology and fresh outputs, each complete round realizes~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:pair} on its selected pair and preserves all other active columns. Its horizontal and vertical neighbors agree on initial colours and intersect only at declared whole-box aliases. Every cell in the complete legal resource history has at most two departures.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
For each tile, expand its listed primitive placements and cable routes by exact translation and rotation. Their maps agree on every shared owner. Enumerate the input bit assignments, prior input WRITE possibilities, both crossing orders where relevant, and all optional later reads of outputs. Project each composite trace to each owner and check it against the legal histories of Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:primitives}. The resulting output state fields give~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:pair}; an untouched fresh output has value zero. The independent finite atlas then checks both horizontal neighbor halos, every vertically meeting translate, all pair types and both edge-turn continuations. Non-box supports are disjoint. Whole-box intersections are merged before counting. Hence local maxima apply to one real history at every physical cell. The archived independent interpreters reproduce the generated histories rather than trusting a generator's status field.
\end{proof}

Two partial cases are needed in addition to full rounds. A skipped upper NOT pass may be followed only by the lower westward COPY. Its intermediate box remains INIT0, so its output is zero without reading the old input. Also the modified left marker/header joint can receive a normal east-only zero input when its earlier outer COPY was never used. The certificate for this second case covers unused old-source INIT0, INIT1, or prior WRITE0, followed by stopping, an output READ1, or the next header COPY and READ1: nine histories, all bounded by two. These cases close the initial partial header and every newly exposed outside edge; they cannot be assumed from the ordinary full-round cases.

\section{The fixed machine and the total cellular rule}\label{ptr:la:sec:ca}
\subsection{The exact U15 table}
The fixed machine has states A through O, symbols 0 and 1, and the following transition table. An entry is written symbol, direction, next state. The undefined J1 transition is the simulated halting condition.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{ccc@{\qquad}ccc}
\toprule
State & On 0 & On 1 & State & On 0 & On 1\\\midrule
A & $0RB$ & $1RA$ & I & $0RA$ & $1LJ$\\
B & $1RC$ & $1RA$ & J & $1LK$ & halt\\
C & $0LG$ & $0LE$ & K & $0RL$ & $1RN$\\
D & $0LF$ & $1LE$ & L & $0RM$ & $1RL$\\
E & $1RA$ & $1LD$ & M & $0LB$ & $1RL$\\
F & $1LD$ & $1LD$ & N & $0LC$ & $0RO$\\
G & $0LH$ & $1LG$ & O & $0RN$ & $1RN$\\
H & $1LI$ & $1LG$ & & &\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The table's archived JSON hash is
\begin{center}\small\codeu{0c6d8ae506f503e2783f6ed2ee68db4cde2f501b9864f376ab3773f86b59428a}\end{center}
The interface uses the 32-state encoding below. A separate 34-state sandpile convention is not imported.
