\subsection{Radius one with 32 primary states}
A headless tape bit has code 0 or 1. If $q$ is the zero-based index of A through O and $a$ is the scanned symbol, the headed code is
\[
 H(q,a)=2+2q+a\in\{2,\ldots,31\}.
\]
Thus J1 has code 21. Define the total radius-one map $g(l,c,r)$ as follows. If $c$ is headed, write its transition's tape bit; if $c=21$, retain 21. If $c$ is headless, an incoming right-moving head from $l$ or left-moving head from $r$ gives the corresponding next state combined with the current bit $c$. If both arrive, give the left arrival priority. If neither arrives, retain $c$.

The center-headed case takes priority over arrivals. These rules define $g$ for all $32^3=32768$ triples, including malformed multi-head configurations. The priority on malformed double arrivals is a total extension; it is not an assertion that such configurations simulate a Turing machine.

\begin{lemma}[Valid primary simulation]\label{ptr:la:lem:primary}
On a configuration with one headed tape cell, one application of $G(X)_i=g(X_{i-1},X_i,X_{i+1})$ gives the next machine configuration when the current transition is defined. A configuration with the unique head J1 is fixed. In particular, code 21 occurs in the valid evolution exactly when the machine reaches J1.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
At the current head, the center-headed branch writes the prescribed tape bit. Exactly its destination neighbor sees an incoming head in the prescribed direction and constructs the prescribed next state over that neighbor's old symbol. The other neighbor sees no incoming head in its direction, and every other headless cell remains unchanged. Hence there is again exactly one head and the tape and state are correct. For J1 the center is retained, its undefined transition causes no arrival in a neighbor, and all other cells remain unchanged. Induction gives the assertion.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Radius half with 11-bit states}
Use codes $0,\ldots,2047$. Primary codes remain $0,\ldots,31$. Define
\[
 \Pair(a,b)=\begin{cases}0,&a=b=0,\\1024+32a+b,&\text{otherwise},\end{cases}
 \quad 0\le a,b<32.
\]
The valid pair codes are 0 and 1025 through 2047. Code zero serves both phases. Define the two-input rule $f$ in the following order:
\begin{enumerate}
\item If both inputs are primary, set $f(a,b)=\Pair(a,b)$
\item Otherwise, if they are valid and consistent pair codes $\Pair(a,b),\Pair(b,c)$, set the output to $g(a,b,c)$
\item In every remaining case set the output to zero
\end{enumerate}
There is no ambiguity at $(0,0)$ because both interpretations give zero. The complete table has $2048^2=4194304$ entries, stored as little-endian two-byte integers.

Let $F(X)_i=f(X_{i-1},X_i)$, and define $(\sigma X)_i=X_{i-1}$. For primary configurations,
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:shift}
 F^2(X)=\sigma G(X),\qquad F^{2t}(X)=\sigma^tG^t(X).
\end{equation}
Indeed $F(X)_i=\Pair(X_{i-1},X_i)$, so the next step at $i$ uses the consistent pair of pairs and returns $g(X_{i-2},X_{i-1},X_i)$. The second identity follows by induction and translation equivariance. Odd times contain only pair codes or zero, so cannot contain primary 21. Existence of primary 21 is invariant under the shift in~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:shift}.

\subsection{Markers and the Boolean cell}
The actual cell takes 24 bits, grouped as $(x_L,s_0,s_1,x_R)$ with 11 bits for each state, and computes
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:phi}
 \Phi(x_L,s_0,s_1,x_R)
 =\bigl(s_1,\ f((1-s_1)x_L,x_R),\ f((1-s_1)x_L,x_R),\ s_0(1-s_1)\bigr).
\end{equation}
The scalar multiplication in this notation means mask every bit of $x_L$ by $1-s_1$. In particular the left-edge branch $s_1=1$ ignores both $x_L$ and $s_0$ for the state computation. This validates skipping their initial physical header prefix. There are again 24 output bits: one left marker, two copies of the 11-bit state, and one right marker. The separate halt bit is
\[
 h=\ind\{f((1-s_1)x_L,x_R)=21\}.
\]

\section{The complete 862 NAND proof}\label{ptr:la:sec:boolean}
The Boolean DAG has 24 inputs and 862 NAND gates, hence wire indices 0 through 885. Each gate lists two strictly earlier wires. The physical header first complements the 24 real input bits, and the footer later complements the 24 circuit outputs. Accordingly, the DAG receives complemented inputs and emits complemented $\Phi$ outputs; its halt wire is deliberately \emph{uncomplemented}.

For clarity, the construction uses only identities reducible to NAND:
\[
 \neg a=\NAND(a,a),\quad
 a\wedge b=\neg\NAND(a,b),\quad
 a\vee b=\NAND(\neg a,\neg b),
\]
\[
 \operatorname{MUX}(c,a,b)=(c\wedge a)\vee(\neg c\wedge b).
\]
Equality to a fixed binary code is a conjunction of either each input bit or its negation. Finite conjunctions and disjunctions start from constructed constant-one and constant-zero wires. Identical unordered NAND pairs are memoized; no non-NAND primitive is left in the serialized circuit.

The circuit first negates each complemented input and masks the left state. A primary validity predicate tests that bits 5 through 10 are zero. Pair validity tests zero or bit 10 together with a nonzero lower ten-bit field. In the paired branch, the five low bits of the left pair equal the five middle bits of the right pair. The local radius-one circuit tests each machine-head code, constructs the incoming direction and next-state bits, and implements the center-head, halt, and left-arrival priorities exactly as in Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:ca}. The selected output bits combine the primary and consistent-pair branches; all invalid branch combinations vanish to zero. Duplicating those state bits and appending the markers gives~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:phi}. The 24 final negations supply the complemented footer inputs. A code-equality test produces $h$.

\begin{theorem}[Complete Boolean agreement]\label{ptr:la:thm:boolean}
For every one of the $2^{24}=16777216$ Boolean inputs, the 862-gate DAG on complemented inputs has complemented outputs $\Phi$ and halt wire exactly $h$. Its comparison is with the independently enumerated total tables, including malformed codes and all four marker assignments.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The NAND identities prove every helper expansion. Code comparisons and branch selection implement the preceding total definition; the encoding calculation establishes its valid-machine meaning. To eliminate any ambiguity in the concrete compiled DAG, the independent checker also enumerates all 32768 primary triples to build $g$, all 4194304 state pairs to build $f$, and all four assignments $(s_0,s_1)$. For each marker assignment it forms one 4194304-bit integer for every input bit: bit position $(l,r)$ stores that input on that state pair. A NAND is evaluated as bitwise complement of conjunction, masked to those 4194304 positions. Induction on the DAG rows proves that each live integer is the complete truth plane for that wire. The independent table-derived output truth planes are compared bit-for-bit with all 24 complemented outputs and $h$. Zero mismatch over four planes of assignments covers all $2^{24}$ inputs, not a sample of valid states. The serialized DAG and both complete table files are pinned in the receipt. Its peak of 63 live bitplanes is a memory implementation detail and does not reduce the exhaustive domain.
\end{proof}
The short circuit-builder's randomized checks are ancillary. They are not used in place of the independent complete-table proof. Conversely, exact Boolean agreement alone establishes no physical tiling or visit bound; those are supplied elsewhere in this report.

\section{An exact compressed physical program}\label{ptr:la:sec:program}
The physical columns do not move. The logical occupied word is a prefix of those columns, with arbitrary contents allowed in unused slack. A logical WORD operation is implemented through~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:pair}; compilation never assumes fresh zero workspace unless a physical instruction itself creates it.

\subsection{WORD insertion and contraction}
For a word of length $m$ and index $i$, put $r=m-i$.
\begin{itemize}
\item $\mathrm{WORD}(\DUP,m,i)$ moves the suffix one position right, using $\MR$ at indices $m-1,m-2,\ldots,i+1$, then applies $\DUP$ at $i$. Its length is $r$ and pair-left range $[i,m-1]$
\item $\mathrm{WORD}(\NAND,m,i)$ applies $\NAND$ at $i$, then uses $\ML$ at $i+1,\ldots,m-2$. Its length is $r-1$ and range $[i,m-2]$
\end{itemize}
Descending right moves preserve every suffix bit before overwriting its former cell; ascending left moves likewise preserve each source until used. Hence the first operation inserts a copy immediately after the chosen bit, and the second replaces adjacent bits by their NAND and contracts the suffix. These proofs hold for arbitrary preceding, following, and slack contents.

\subsection{SWAP and COPY to the end}
The explicit logical XOR macro has three duplications and four NANDs. Three XORs and three more duplications give the standard adjacent swap identity. The resulting archived SWAP grammar contains 24 WORD operations, exactly 12 DUP and 12 NAND. Its truth table on the two selected bits is $(a,b)\mapsto(b,a)$, with every other logical bit untouched. The logical width increases by at most four and returns to its original value.

For a SWAP at index $i$ in width $m$, the lengths of its 24 WORD terms are $r+c_j$, where
\[
(c_j)=\begin{gathered}[t]
(0,-1,1,0,1,1,2,0,0,0,2,1,\\
 2,2,3,1,1,0,-1,0,0,1,-1,-1).
\end{gathered}
\]
All terms are positive when $r\ge2$, and $\sum c_j=14$. Thus
\[
 L_{\SWAP}(r)=24r+14.
\]
Its physical counts are DUP $12$, NAND $12$, right moves $12r-7$, and left moves $12r-3$. Applying the WORD ranges gives exact pair-left range $[i,m+2]$ and exact peak logical width $m+4$.

To $\COPY(m,i)$ a source to the end, first insert a duplicate and then move that duplicate through the old suffix by $r-1$ adjacent SWAPs. The original word is restored and one copy is appended at position $m$. The prefix length of the first $t$ swaps is
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:copy-prefix}
 P_r(t)=12t(2r-t)+26t,\qquad 0\le t\le r-1.
\end{equation}
Its increment is $24(r-t)+14$, exactly the next SWAP length and strictly positive. Consequently
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:copy-counts}
\begin{split}
 L_{\COPY}(r)&=r+P_r(r-1)=12r^2+27r-38,\\
 n_{\DUP}&=12r-11,\qquad n_{\NAND}=12r-12,\\
 n_{\MR}&=6r^2-6,\qquad n_{\ML}=6r^2+3r-9.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
For $r=1$, COPY is the single final DUP; there is no empty-suffix exception to decode. For $r>1$ its exact pair-left range is $[i,m+3]$ and its exact peak width $m+5$.

\subsection{Appending a circuit gate}
At logical width $m=24+j$, the $j$th DAG gate references wires $u,v<m$. Apply $\COPY(m,u)$, then $\COPY(m+1,v)$, and finally $\NAND$ at pair index $m$. The first two operations preserve every old wire and append the referenced bits at $m,m+1$; the NAND stores their result at $m$ and zero at $m+1$. The new logical width is $m+1$. This remains valid when $u=v$. Induction over all 862 gates proves circuit evaluation. Each GATE has three contiguous nonempty row intervals; its exact peak width is $m+6$ and its exact pair-left range $[\min(u,v),m+4]$.

The program gathers its 24 uniformly spaced real inputs, evaluates the DAG, copies the 24 selected complemented outputs and then $h$ to the end, performs one dedicated observer DUP on $h$, gathers the outputs, and scatters them back to the uniform positions. The 958 positive-length top-level grammar records define exactly
\[
 R=601547591
\]
physical pair rounds. Their exact counts are
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lr}\toprule
Instruction & Rows\\\midrule
NAND & 1365370\\
DUP & 1366258\\
MOVE LEFT & 299589177\\
MOVE RIGHT & 299226786\\\midrule
Total & 601547591\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
No full 601-million-row list is included or needed.

\subsection{Gather and scatter correctness}
The selected active columns are $40j$, $0\le j<24$. Input zero already lies at column zero. For $j=1,\ldots,23$, left moves at descending pair indices $40j-1,\ldots,j$ move input $40j$ into $j$. Earlier gathered inputs lie below $j$ and future sources above $40j$, so neither is touched. There are $39(1+\cdots+23)=10764$ rows.

The completed DAG occupies 886 wire positions. Copying its 24 outputs and $h$ places them at 886 through 910. The observer DUP at 910 copies $h$ to 911. For output $j=0,\ldots,23$, descending left-move indices $886+j-1,\ldots,j$ move that source into $j$, requiring 886 rows each, or 21264 total. Earlier gathered outputs and later sources are untouched. Finally, for $j=23,\ldots,1$, ascending right-move indices $j,\ldots,40j-1$ move output $j$ to $40j$, for another 10764 rows. Descending order protects already scattered outputs. Any later overwrite of halt workspace happens after its dedicated observation and cannot alter the selected outputs.

\subsection{Random access for every row}
The top-level prefix array has 959 entries, starting at zero and ending at $R$, strictly increasing. For $0\le k<R$, binary search finds the unique interval containing $k$. The selected node's decoder chooses a WORD, SWAP, COPY, or GATE branch. WORD has affine row descriptions. SWAP sequentially subtracts the 24 positive WORD lengths. GATE subtracts the two COPY lengths before its final NAND.

For COPY, after excluding its first $r$ rows, use the invariant
\[
 P_r(\mathrm{low})\le k<P_r(\mathrm{high}),\quad
 0\le\mathrm{low}<\mathrm{high}\le r-1.
\]
Each binary branch preserves it and strictly shrinks the interval. On termination high equals low plus one; strict increase in~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:copy-prefix} identifies exactly the next SWAP and a valid residual row inside it. At $r=1$ this search is never entered. Thus the decoder terminates and returns the correct pair operation for \emph{every} legal row.

The independent audit reconstructs all 958 nodes and every prefix entry from the raw DAG and the gather/scatter definitions. Its interval certificate covers 958 top-level intervals, 2586 GATE intervals, 83783 COPY intervals over all occurring residual widths, 20112 SWAP WORD intervals, and 40220 affine WORD subintervals. This is not random-row sampling: within each half-open arithmetic interval the branch decisions are constant and the residual index is affine, so endpoint checks together with the algebra cover its interior. Translation of both $m$ and $i$ shifts emitted column indices without changing lengths, allowing normalization by residual width.

The exact maximum pair-left index is 919, and the right index is at most 920. Active columns 0 through 957 therefore suffice. The exact compiler prefix peak is 915; the earlier conservative bound 917 is valid but not the exact peak. Physical scattering, rather than compiler scratch, accounts for the larger used destination 920. There are two surrounding routing columns, giving 960 total column slots.

\section{The literal fixed colouring}\label{ptr:la:sec:colouring}
A computation block C has an all-NOT/header-COPY round, $R$ instruction rounds, and a NOT-footer/outer-COPY round. Put
\[
 K=958,\quad W=960,\quad S=600W=576000,
\]
\[
 F=400(R+1)=240619036800,\quad V=400(R+2)=240619037200.
\]
Layer $j\in\Z$ has vertical origin $jV$ and horizontal shift $(j\bmod2)S/2$. Its block $i$ has origin
\[
 (iS+(j\bmod2)S/2,\ jV).
\]
All such blocks, including unused negative-index blocks and layers, belong to the background. Finite input perturbations select a growing portion of this already fixed hardware.

The literal generator is a total colour query. Given $(x,y)$, let $j_0=\lfloor y/V\rfloor$ and inspect $j_0-1,j_0,j_0+1$. For each, translate to local $Y=y-jV$ and retain only $50\le Y\le V+259$. Let $i_0$ be the floor of its shifted $x/S$ and inspect $i_0-1,i_0,i_0+1$, retaining the block horizontal support. For header and body positions inspect the three possible rounds around $\lfloor(Y-50)/400\rfloor$; only the legal range $0\le r\le R$ survives. In round zero place the NOT and lower COPY resources, except for the specially supplied marker header. For $r\ge1$, decode instruction $r-1$, place that pair once, and place delayed COPY in all other active slots that can meet the coordinate. Add the exact left and right turns. A cell near a 600-wide column boundary needs only columns $k_0-1,k_0$.

The footer chooses the right-marker joint at absolute column 921 and ordinary NOT elsewhere. Its outer sweep includes all 960 storage columns; the left-marker joint is at absolute column 1. The normal C-to-C rising channel is supplied separately. Every nonempty painter list must agree in colour; an empty list returns zero.

\begin{proposition}[A well-defined periodic board]\label{ptr:la:prop:board}
The selector terminates at every integer coordinate, finds every possible painter, and gives a single colour. Its rectangular periods are $(S,0)$ and $(0,2V)$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Each resource lies within $0\le X\le S+599$ and $50\le Y\le V+259$, so its true layer and block lie among the three candidates just described, including at negative coordinates because integer floor is used. A full-round support reaches at most one neighboring 400-unit slab; a column support reaches at most its neighboring 600-unit slot. Thus the finite round/column candidates are exhaustive. Pair placements replace both selected delayed COPY slots, preventing duplicate unrelated owners. The independent finite compatibility atlas checks all template classes, boundary classes, marker exceptions, and both stagger parities; its 1974 multiply-painted groups are complete agreeing 43-cell boxes and contain no triple painter. The long pieces are covered by the uniform inequalities proved in Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:channel}. Hence no missed painter or conflicting colour remains. Finitely many comparisons, lookups, and terminating row decoders make the query total. Increasing $x$ by $S$ increments the block index, while increasing $y$ by $2V$ increments the layer by two without changing its horizontal phase. All local coordinates and maps are unchanged.
\end{proof}
The finite review evaluated 263525 template, box, and anchor cells. That is finite evidence for the finite cases. The selector-range argument and arbitrary-even-wire proof cover the infinite translations and long cable lengths; the finite count is not represented as an exhaustive enumeration of the huge period rectangle.

\section{Turnarounds and the long return channel}\label{ptr:la:sec:channel}
Each complete internal round has the verified east-to-west and west-to-east connectors. The exceptional footer and header controllers are chronological joints, rather than a Boolean marker wired onto a path of unknown history.

\subsection{The right marker}
The right marker is selected output slot 23, active column 920, absolute column 921. Its joint computes the marker before selecting a branch. On zero it continues the eastward footer and later accepts the ordinary westward COPY visit. On one it performs the output WRITE, joins its own outer COPY, and turns into the leftward sweep immediately. Its route never requires a crossover SECOND whose FIRST was skipped. This is why the marker gadget is verified as a combined NOT/COPY/branch resource, not as separate maxima.

\subsection{The left marker}
The left marker is selected output slot zero, absolute column one. Its joint either copies zero and continues west, or copies one and immediately enters the next layer's $s_1$ header NOT. The next layer is shifted by half a block. Since $S/2=12\cdot40\cdot600$, selected output slot $t$ aligns with selected input slot $t+12$ modulo 24:
\[
0\mapsto12,\quad1\ldots11\mapsto13\ldots23,
\quad12\ldots22\mapsto0\ldots10,\quad23\mapsto11.
\]
Vertically, ordinary outer-COPY output is at $F+450=V+50$, exactly the next header input. The left-marker header output is at $F+650=V+250$, exactly the next header COPY input. These identities match full box maps, not just representative center points.

For fixed physical block indices, even-to-odd outputs 0 through 11 go to block $i-1$ and 12 through 23 to block $i$. Odd-to-even they go to $i$ and $i+1$. The resulting phase-dependent spatial reindexing must be distinguished from the fixed logical index in~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:shift}. Both preserve the existence of state 21, which is the observation used here.

\subsection{Normal completion into the next block}
Let $e=575400$. A normally completed C rises through its far-right margin and enters the next C header. Its exact successive head states are
\begin{align*}
(e,F+75,E)&\longrightarrow(e+170,F+75,E)\\
&\longrightarrow(e+175,F+69,N)\\
&\longrightarrow(e+175,79,N)\\
&\longrightarrow(e+180,75,E)\\
&\longrightarrow(S+600,75,E).
\end{align*}
The pieces are an east strip of length 170, the 19-cell E-to-N corner, a north strip of even length $F-10$, the rotated 17-cell N-to-E corner, and an east strip of length 1020. Their owned rectangles are
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{lll}\toprule
Piece & $x$ interval & $y$ interval\\\midrule
Bottom east & $e\ldots e+169$ & $F+74\ldots F+75$\\
E-to-N corner & $e+170\ldots e+176$ & $F+70\ldots F+75$\\
North strip & $e+174\ldots e+175$ & $80\ldots F+69$\\
N-to-E corner & $e+174\ldots e+179$ & $74\ldots79$\\
Top east & $e+180\ldots S+599$ & $74\ldots75$\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
They are pairwise disjoint; shared head seams are terminal states excluded from the earlier piece. Their total support and exact departure count is
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:channelcount}
2\cdot170+19+2(F-10)+17+2\cdot1020
=2F+2396=481238075996.
\end{equation}

\begin{lemma}[Long-channel isolation]\label{ptr:la:lem:channel}
Every cell of this route has the specified cable or corner colour and is owned by no unrelated active resource. The route visits each owned cell once.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The two finite strips and corners have explicit map checks and exact ports. On the north strip, for every $80\le Y\le F+69$, the $x=e+174$ cell is colour zero and the $x=e+175$ cell is colour one. Substitution into the generator's rotation formula gives longitudinal coordinate $a=F+69-Y\in[0,F-11]$ and lane $b=x-(e+175)+1\in\{0,1\}$. This proves every long-wire cell, not selected sample heights.

The active body is at $x\le e$ and internal right turns at $x\le e+59$, strictly left of the north strip. The outer right-guard COPY starts at $F+250$, below its last owned row $F+69$, and the next layer starts at $V+50=F+450$. Prior ordinary outer-COPY and right-marker output footprints end at current height 59. The only previous-layer extension reaching current heights 80 through 259 is the left marker, confined modulo $S$ to $[288600,289200]$, far left of this channel. A previous same-layer block reaches at most current $x=600$, while the next starts at $S$; the north strip is strictly between them. The top strip ends at $S+599$ and excludes its next header entry $(S+600,75,E)$. These uniform inequalities exclude all other painters. The even-cable induction and finite corner traces give exactly one departure per owned cell.
\end{proof}

\section{The growing-layer controller and global visit proof}\label{ptr:la:sec:global}
At a layer boundary let the represented state word have length $m+j$. Its unique left marker is $s_1=1$ at block $L$ and its unique right marker is $s_0=1$ at block $R$, with $R-L=m+j$. All intermediate marker bits are zero. One layer computes the $m+j+1$ pairs spanning the word and a quiescent neighbor at each end.

\begin{lemma}[One complete layer]\label{ptr:la:lem:layer}
Starting in the marked left header, the ant computes the correct next radius-half layer, returns through the outer westward COPY sweep, and enters the next marked header. The next word has one additional state; its physical left and right edges expand by $S/2$ in the two directions. Every newly exposed input is quiescent.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The first C is entered at its $s_1$ header, skipping the $x_L,s_0$ prefix. Equation~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:phi} ignores the skipped left state under $s_1=1$, and the certified W-only header histories account for all skipped columns. Its complete instruction program is then executed. Subsequent blocks are entered normally by the isolated rising route of Lemma~\ref{ptr:la:lem:channel}. The right-marker joint at the unique $s_0=1$ computes the last meaningful output and diverts before escaping into uncomputed eastward hardware.

The outer westward sweep copies every computed output box in the interval. Zero-valued intermediate left-marker tests continue west. The unique copied $s_1=1$ enters the next layer. The marker equations in~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:phi} retain one left endpoint and propagate one right endpoint; the duplicated state and stagger identities of Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:channel} give the correct adjacent input pairs. The next interval has one extra state and edges shifted by half a block. Fresh outside boxes were never activated, so they are INIT0. The certified normal header E-only zero case covers the boundary whose preceding outer COPY never ran. Thus the same invariant is established for the next layer.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Infinite global composition]\label{ptr:la:thm:global}
For every loaded finite pair, the unique physical trajectory is an infinite concatenation of the certified resource histories. No lattice site is visited more than twice, for the whole trajectory rather than only through acceptance.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:loader} establishes the initial marker and board invariant at the actual fixed start. Assume it at one layer. Every selected instruction index is in range by Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:program}; each local operation has Proposition~\ref{ptr:la:prop:physical}'s history; Lemma~\ref{ptr:la:lem:layer} supplies the whole controller sequence and a next layer. Each layer is a finite number of finite instructions and finite cables, so its endpoint is reached in finite time. Induction produces arbitrarily many layers. The rule itself never lacks a transition.

It remains to bound all-time reuse rather than merely to validate endpoints. Nonalias resources have disjoint owned supports by the finite neighbor atlas, selector proof, exceptional-marker bounds, and long-channel inequalities. Each such resource is scheduled once. The only temporal support overlaps are complete storage-box aliases across stages. Projecting the global schedule onto one such box gives exactly its optional-WRITE-before-READ legal history. Every crossing receives FIRST before SECOND and every union receives one alternative, as checked within the controller's local schedule. Thus each physical cell belongs either to one two-bounded complete resource history or to one merged two-bounded storage history. No separate macro maxima are summed at aliases.

Any purported third departure has a finite time and therefore belongs to a finite number of completed layers plus one finite prefix. The finite-prefix induction just proved excludes it. Hence all infinite-history departure counts are at most two. Every arrival, including the starting site, is followed by departure in this infinite run, so the same bound holds for visits. A frozen simulated J1 still undergoes new layer computation on fresh hardware; no later post-halt reuse is omitted from the argument.
\end{proof}

\section{The exact finite loader and actual startup}\label{ptr:la:sec:loader}
The input consists of finite binary lists $\ell,r$, each with its first element nearest the machine head. Form the primary state word
\[
 (a_0,\ldots,a_{m-1})=(\operatorname{reverse}(\ell),H(A,0),r),
 \qquad m=|\ell|+|r|+1\ge1.
\]
The head code $H(A,0)=2$ has exactly one set bit. For each set bit $b$ of state $a_i$, set two source fields: block $i$, selected slot $13+b$, and block $i+1$, selected slot $b$. A slot $k$ in block $i$ has source-box origin
\[
 (iS+600+24000k+100,\ 50).
\]
A logical one sets its two state cells $(x+4,54)$ and $(x+5,54)$ to colour zero; their background colour is one. State zero changes no state field. Set the left marker at block 0, slot 12, and the right marker at block $m$, slot 11.

\subsection{The fixed 2806-cell anchor}
The startup patch is the exact net board change left by a certified virtual COPY1-to-header prefix, translated by $(288600,-400)$. The virtual prefix is a way of deriving a finite initial state, not an actual uncounted physical run. Its unprocessed endpoint is
\[
 (288650,75,E),
\]
which is the real fixed starting head. The ordinary unused slot entry $(288600,75,E)$ is not the start. The two virtual INIT1 setup flips cancel in the virtual read and are not applied a second time.

All 6193 cells in the translated marker's baseline agree with the global colour query, and all 2806 expected old colours in the net patch agree. The real sparse patch plus the global background reproduces the exact certified 1276-departure suffix to $(289200,75,E)$, with maximum two departures at a cell. More generally the real startup is a suffix of a legal complete history, so deleting its earlier virtual prefix cannot increase a physical count. The anchor lies in
\[
 [288617,289200]\times[-144,76].
\]
It meets raw input fields only at the left marker's two state cells, already set to the same zero colour.

\begin{proposition}[Exact support and initial invariant]\label{ptr:la:prop:loader}
The loader supplies a finite perturbation of the fixed board, establishes the initial valid layer invariant at the actual start, and changes exactly
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:support}
 |I(\ell,r)|=2812+4(\pop\ell+\pop r)
\end{equation}
cells. Its support is contained in
\[
 [288617,576000m+264705]\times[-144,76].
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Every primary set bit is copied into two different selected source fields, each changing two cells. Different state copies lie in disjoint fields. The head contributes one set bit. The right marker is at block $m\ge1$, disjoint from the anchor and state fields; it changes two cells. The left marker is already part of the anchor and contributes no additional support. Therefore
\[
2806+4(\pop\ell+\pop r+1)+2
=2812+4(\pop\ell+\pop r).
\]
Every assigned colour differs from the background, as checked by the literal query; no redundant override is counted. The anchor's sole overlapping marker assignment agrees in colour. Its suffix places the controller at the active left header, while the duplicated source bits and two markers give exactly the initial state word and boundaries. The source-field formula and the anchor bounds give the displayed support rectangle.
\end{proof}

Zero-only words retain their lengths. For example $(\epsilon,\epsilon)$ has $m=1$, while $(000,00)$ has $m=6$. Both change exactly 2812 cells, but the right marker is in a different block. Removing zero prefixes or suffixes as if the loader depended only on popcount would silently alter its declared finite input representation.

The public loader accepts the two words, not caller-chosen hardware, anchor, or program. A concrete output contains the fixed start, fixed periods, accepting clauses, and sorted changed-cell list. Its defensive-return checks ensure that a caller's mutation of returned metadata does not alter future compilations; such API isolation is separate from the physical simulation theorem.

\section{An exclusive accepting port with the correct polarity}\label{ptr:la:sec:observer}
The accepting instruction is zero-based program row
\[
 k_*=601515562,
\]
and is a DUP on active column 910. At its entry, that input is the \emph{uncomplemented} bit $h=[f=21]$. The associated first output box's WRITE entry is the physical observation cell. An incoming heading S there means that this DUP is conditionally writing one.

\subsection{Why the bit at that row is really h}
The DAG halt wire is 885, the output of zero-based gate 861, namely $\NAND(884,884)$. After all 862 gates and 24 output copies, the final copy-to-end has width 910 and source 885. Here $r=25$, so~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:copy-counts} gives
\[
12\cdot25^2+27\cdot25-38=8137
\]
physical rows, transporting $h$ to column 910. The independent polarity checker eagerly expands these 8137 rows, compares each with the random-access decoder, verifies that no unused workspace bit is read, and checks the resulting transported value on all 16777216 Boolean cell inputs. No footer NOT lies between this copy and the observer DUP. Thus ``the DAG computes a halt bit'' is strengthened to the exact polarity at the actual physical observation instruction.

\begin{lemma}[Local observer exclusivity]\label{ptr:la:lem:port}
In every legal history of the observer DUP, its first output WRITE-entry cell is visited with heading S exactly once when $h=1$, and never when $h=0$. The eastward pass, later output READ, and next-row input alias cannot produce this head state.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The DUP input read branches on the selected bit. For zero it omits both output writes, leaving fresh zero boxes; for one it follows the certified write routes. The first box's WRITE entry is a specified port, so the one branch visits it in heading S exactly once. Enumeration of all prior input and optional output-read histories verifies that the eastward pass and read route do not enter that cell with the claimed heading. The next row can only share the same whole box as an input; its chronological projection is the already included subsequent READ, not a second WRITE. Therefore it cannot supply a false or duplicate observation.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Why no other part of the plane can imitate the port}
The observation residues in~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:ports} are the two translations of that resource under the staggered layers. Apart from the just-accounted next-row shared box, all other body rows have a different vertical phase modulo $2V$. Header, footer, marker turn, and partial-header paths also have different phases. The only normal route rising through old instruction heights is the far-right channel, whose $x$ interval is disjoint from the observer column. The input state changes lie at $y=54$, the anchor is in $[-144,76]$, and the actual start has $y=75$, so initialization cannot satisfy either residue clause.

A partially entered C still executes the full program; its skipped prefix is ignored through $s_1=1$. Thus no uncomputed workspace can lead to the observation row. A sound event does not depend on the later completion of the DUP or footer: $h$ was already computed and exactly transported before its WRITE-entry visit.

\begin{theorem}[Acceptance equivalence and simplification]\label{ptr:la:thm:accept}
The event~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:ports} with incoming heading S occurs if and only if the source U15 configuration reaches J1. For every valid primary-loaded input, it is equivalent to the single clause
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:single}
 h_t=S,\qquad x_t\equiv258702\pmod{576000},\qquad
 y_t\equiv481225262850\pmod{481238074400}.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The geometric exclusion and Lemma~\ref{ptr:la:lem:port} show that an observed state occurs exactly at a computed $h=1$. The polarity argument and Theorem~\ref{ptr:la:thm:boolean} identify this with a computed CA output 21. The layer invariant and~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:shift} identify some such output with the valid source machine reaching J1. Conversely, on reaching J1 the corresponding primary CA step computes 21 in the finite active interval, and its complete program reaches the observer's one branch in finite time.

The first clause is the even physical computation layer's observer. Such a layer computes an odd CA output from primary inputs, hence only pair codes or zero. None is 21. The second clause detects primary outputs and is the only reachable accepting clause on the loader's valid runs. Retaining both clauses is a uniform two-phase description with no false event; deleting the unreachable first clause preserves the equivalence.
\end{proof}
This proves the observation portion of Theorem~\ref{ptr:la:thm:interface}. Neither clause denotes a single absolute lattice site. The ant may accept in any translated occurrence of this fixed residue port, and still continues forever.

\section{Exact dimensions and conservative physical costs}\label{ptr:la:sec:bounds}
The global board is specified by finite templates and 958 arithmetic program records. A rectangular period contains
\[
576000\cdot481238074400=277193130854400000
\]
lattice positions. No claim is made to enumerate this dense bitmap. The random-access query uses boundedly many local resource candidates, a binary search among the finite program records, and the finite SWAP/COPY arithmetic grammar.

For $T$ computed CA rounds, the successive numbers of C evaluations are $m+1,m+2,\ldots,m+T$. Therefore
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:evaluations}
 N_C(T,m)=T(m+1)+\frac{T(T-1)}2.
\end{equation}
Every finite resource has at most 13197 owned cells and at most two aggregate departures per cell. Set $M=26394$. Including the explicit long route, a deliberately loose control bound per C is
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:microsteps}
\begin{split}
 B_C&=(R+1)(K+4)M+(K+W+8)M+2F+2396\\
 &=15274393040715416.
\end{split}
\end{equation}
This intentionally overcounts several shared and partial resource histories. It is a valid upper bound, not an optimized timing claim. Consequently, if U15 reaches J1 after $t\ge1$ steps, an accepting event is reached within
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:halttime}
 \bigl[2t(m+1)+t(2t-1)\bigr]B_C
\end{equation}
ant departures. Acceptance can precede completion of its last C.

Through $T$ complete CA rounds, safe coordinate bounds are
\[
 |x|\le576000(m+T+2),\qquad
 -144\le y\le240619037200(T+1)+659.
\]
Their signed encoding needs $O(\log(m+T+2)+39)$ bits. Input-coordinate magnitudes grow linearly in $m$ even though support cardinality depends on popcount. These distinctions matter when an external arithmetic representation encodes the initial board as an integer.

\section{Why one visit cannot realize the same acceptance task}\label{ptr:la:sec:lower}
Report 38~\cite{ptr:la:report38}, included unchanged in the archive, proves the total first-revisit algorithm and exact pattern-query implementation. We give the decisive arguments here and spell out why its input conventions cover this report.

{\small\wtag\ \emph{A re-proof, in summary, of Part~I.} Sections 40.14.1--40.14.2
restate and sketch the proofs of Part~I's Lemma~\ref{ptr:lem:shadow} (static
shadow), Lemma~\ref{ptr:lem:permutation} (projected permutation),
Proposition~\ref{ptr:prop:primitive} (the rank-2/1/0 intersection primitive),
Proposition~\ref{ptr:prop:termination} and Theorem~\ref{ptr:thm:main} (the
total first-revisit algorithm) and Theorem~\ref{ptr:thm:query} (exact
queries). They are printed here as Report~40 wrote them, as a second,
abbreviated route; Part~I has the complete proofs and the bit-complexity
bounds. Two pieces are new in Report~40: the elementary generalized-CRT
decision of heading-and-congruence clauses on a lane (the special case that
the acceptance port needs, without Part~I's signed indicator calculus), and
the model-match argument of Section~40.14.3 (the reflection between Part~I's
north-positive and this Part's south-positive $y$, and the observation that a
fixed succinctly specified tile is a constant).\par}

\subsection{Fresh walks have a finite affine description}
Before the first repeated arrival, every departure is from an unmodified cell. The moving-and-flipping ant therefore follows the static walk that always reads the initial periodic board plus finite defects. The two paths agree through the first repeated arrival, inclusive. The next departure is outside this assertion.

On a periodic tile of width $u$ and height $v$, the static background transition on $(x\bmod u,y\bmod v,h)$ is a permutation of $4uv$ states: given the successor and its heading, subtract the last unit move to recover the predecessor residue; its colour identifies and reverses the turn. Every projected orbit is thus a pure cycle. A defect-free prospective excursion has a finite description
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:lanes}
 p_{r+i+nL}=a_i+n\delta,\quad h_{r+i+nL}=g_i,
 \quad 0\le i<L,\quad n\ge0,
\end{equation}
with $L\le4uv$ and $\delta\in u\Z\times v\Z$. Each phase is an affine lane; a finite prefix is represented by bounding $n$, without expansion.

To intersect a new lane and an old lane, solve
\[
 a+n d=b+k e,\quad n,k\ge0,
\]
with their finite bounds when present and strict chronology
\[
 t_B+k\ell_B<t_A+n\ell_A.
\]
The two-by-two spatial system has rank two, one, or zero. In rank two, Cramer's rule and divisibility yield at most one candidate. In rank one, extended Euclid yields all integer solutions
\[
 n=n_0+sz,\qquad k=k_0+rz;
\]
all domain and chronological inequalities intersect to one integer interval in $z$, and a suitable endpoint minimizes $n$. In rank zero, equal spatial bases permit the earliest old index $k=0$, imposing only a threshold on $n$; unequal bases are disjoint. These exhaust all cases, including negative drifts, zero components, and different headings at the repeated site.

At a restart, compute the prospective background excursion, its earliest collision with itself or history, and its earliest encounter with an initial defect. If a collision is no later than the defect, return it. If neither exists, certify a fresh infinite tail. Otherwise the defect arrival is fresh; append the excursion through it, perform its one actual defect departure, and restart. Every processed defect is a new site, so there are at most $K$ such departures and $K+1$ excursions when $K$ defects are listed. There are at most $(K+1)4uv$ lanes. Report 38 supplies the polynomial bit bounds on this arithmetic, not merely a bound on the number of large-integer operations. The algorithm requires no one-visit promise and recognizes global freshness itself.

\subsection{Fixed residue-and-heading events are decidable}
On a certified globally fresh run, each time belongs to one of finitely many lanes. A heading condition is constant on a lane. Each coordinate congruence becomes
\[
 an\equiv b\pmod q,
\]
which is either empty or a single residue class after dividing by $\gcd(a,q)$. Intersect the finitely many such classes by generalized CRT and with the lane's integer interval. Check whether any admissible $n$ remains and take the smallest if desired. A finite union of clauses is handled by taking the minimum over lanes and clauses. This elementary special case already decides~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:single} and both clauses of~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:ports}.

The companion proves a stronger exact query theorem including finite head-relative colour stencils. Before a first repeated departure, the colour at a site $z$ is its initial colour plus the indicator of an earlier visit, modulo the palette size. Earlier visits are lane relations with strict chronology. Their projection onto the new lane index is an interval congruence atom. Boolean combinations are represented by finite signed sums of atom indicators, using
\[
 A\wedge B=AB,\quad \neg A=1-A,\quad A\vee B=A+B-AB.
\]
Generalized CRT gives atom intersections and floor division gives exact counts on an interval. Every resulting Boolean indicator is eventually periodic; a finite exceptional prefix plus one eventual period gives an exact emptiness bracket and binary search gives its minimum. This is a terminating implemented procedure, but distributive growth prevents any general polynomial observation bound from being claimed. The polynomial theorem is specifically for first revisit detection.

\subsection{The model match is literal}
Report 38 permits any explicitly listed finite periodic tile, a finite defect map, the same turn/flip/move rule, and pre-departure queries. Its picture uses positive $y$ north; reflection of coordinate notation identifies its north/south vectors with the present south-positive coordinates without changing the physical RL rule. The input override colours and fixed residue classes transform correspondingly. Fixing the rule, tile, start, and query only restricts its decidable input family.

The upper board is given by a fixed finite colour-query program rather than a supplied dense tile. That does not evade the lower result: its periods and query are constants, and its total colour query computes every one of the finitely many tile cells. The enormous tile can be enumerated once as a mathematical constant. No efficient uniform theorem for arbitrary succinctly specified tiles is inferred. The fixed-background decidability argument needs only effective access to this one fixed finite tile, and is unaffected by its impractical size.

On the one-visit side the certified domain is the whole run. The accepting predicate uses only heading and fixed coordinate congruences, a subset of the companion's query language, with no halt signal, hidden unbounded property, or absolute-site-return interpretation. The upper loader produces a globally two-bounded trajectory on every input, not just on halting ones. These are exactly the hypotheses needed for Corollary~\ref{ptr:la:cor:boundary}.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Corollary~\ref{ptr:la:cor:boundary}]
Suppose an undecidable language reduced computably to the globally one-visit class with this fixed kind of observation. Apply the total first-revisit procedure and then the exact all-time query procedure. The reduction's promised one-visit outputs have no revisit, so the observation answers the language, a contradiction. Conversely Theorem~\ref{ptr:la:thm:interface}, composed with the published U15 encoding, gives the required halting-equivalent two-visit family. Its predicate may be the single clause~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:single}. Every premise concerns the same physical rule, periodic-plus-finite initialization, and pre-departure observation model.
\end{proof}
Report 38 correctly declined to assert a sharp boundary while its literal upper interface was absent. This report adds that interface and the model comparison; it does not rewrite the earlier document's historical scope statement.

{\small\wtag\ Report~38's historical scope statement is kept, with a dated
note, in Part~I (Section~\ref{ptr:sec:scope}).\par}

\section{Arithmetic alignment and what remains unpaid}\label{ptr:la:sec:arithmetic}
A north-facing coordinate convention used by an existing bounded-board history encoding is obtained by the orientation-preserving rotation and translation
\[
 X=y-75,\qquad Y=288650-x,\qquad E\mapsto N.
\]
The actual start becomes $(0,0,N)$ with even checkerboard parity. The periods become $(481238074400,576000)$ and the accepting residues become
\[
(240606225575,317948,E),\qquad(481225262775,29948,E),
\]
each on odd parity. Period-multiple translations preserve these residues and place any finite run prefix inside a sufficiently large odd-width, even-height board.

This alignment is geometric. It does not supply an arithmetic initial word for free. The existing 174-operation complete bounded-board history relation takes an encoded initial board as an external parameter. Composing it with raw U15 words would have to pay for raw-input dilation, periodic phase, finite anchor and background, a sufficient board, and the actual accepting observable. Appendix~\ref{ptr:la:app:endpoint} supplies a separately paid residue-and-heading endpoint selector, conditional on the existing history ports and theorem. Its exact 42-operation fixed-numeral and 101-operation literal-1/3 ledgers cover that endpoint only. They do not pay the initialization or yield a combined universal count. No 174-operation universal construction, improved universal operation bound, or physical ant halt is claimed.

\section{Reproducibility and release assurance}\label{ptr:la:sec:repro}
The archive contains the literal source packet, the separately paid endpoint packet, all finite numerical maps and complete local histories, the 32-state and 11-bit tables, the 862-gate DAG, the exact compressed physical program, initialization and observer receipts, and the full unchanged Report 38 companion. Third-party complete articles, PDFs, source archives, and article screenshots are excluded. Their official links and source hashes are recorded instead.

{\small\wtag\ \emph{In this repository.} The archive is shipped flattened
with the prefix \texttt{02-literal-ant-}: \path{evidence/X/Y} became
\path{02-literal-ant-X-Y} (the segment \path{evidence/} dropped), Markdown at
the report root, Python in \path{code/}, everything else in \path{data/}.
The Report~38 companion is Part~I and is not shipped twice; the 24 largest
regenerable files of \path{literal-interface/} (156\,MB in all, including
\path{ca/radius_half_11_table_le.bin}, \path{copy/pair_dup.json},
\path{copy/pair_move_left.json} and \path{copy/pair_move_right.json}) are not
shipped and are rebuilt byte for byte by the shipped generators; the report
README lists them and gives the commands. The delivered verifiers check POSIX
file modes and the delivered layout, so they do not run on the shipped tree;
the README gives a rerun route on a copy. At placement the 28-command
literal-interface driver regenerated its 39 outputs byte for byte and the
endpoint driver its 10, on a copy.\par}

\subsection{Authentication before execution}
The enclosing release inventories every relative path, byte size, mode, and SHA256, and pins its manifest. It rejects unknown files, missing files, symlinks, special files, duplicate JSON keys, nonfinite numeric values, and noncanonical paths. It also pins the active source inventory and replay plan. A normalized self-pin convention avoids a recursive hash cycle: only the verifier's designated manifest-pin line is replaced by 64 zeroes when that verifier is hashed; all other bytes are exact. The independently supplied final archive digest is the external trust anchor. A self-reported PASS or a newly regenerated manifest is not authentication of a received package.

Scientific scripts execute only after this identity gate. The source driver writes \codeu{atlas/fresh_replay.json} under its own root, even though it performs its scientific regeneration in a second temporary tree. Therefore the enclosing replay invokes it only from an authenticated \emph{mutable copy} in an external temporary directory. It never runs that driver in the frozen evidence or original source directory. The source copy's bytes can match again while its receipt's modification time has changed; the release does not claim that mutable worktree metadata stays unchanged. The frozen enclosing release and original source bytes, modes, and modification times are checked separately for preservation.

The driver runs 28 own-code commands and compares 39 regenerated files byte-for-byte with its source data. Its complete receipt is compared with the authenticated expected receipt using exact JSON types and keys; in particular a Boolean cannot masquerade as integer zero or one. No network, third-party source program, dense period bitmap, or complete 601-million-row expansion is needed. The companion has its own pinned active-program allowlist and normal/optimized replay contract.

\subsection{Optimization is deliberately not a scientific mode}
The literal scientific checks use assertions. The 27 explicitly guarded entry points, including the driver, reject optimized Python as required by their contract. Two standalone commands, \codeu{copy/translate_anchor.py} and \codeu{copy/program_audit/verify_program_index_bounds.py}, lack an explicit optimization guard and are marked unsupported under \codeu{-O}; they are not claimed to pass optimized scientific execution. Their supported execution is through the unoptimized guarded driver. The enclosing identity and strict-receipt tools use explicit exceptions and can be tested under optimization, but that is not a claim that optimized source science passes. The different Report 38 code has explicit nonremovable checks and does support its normal/optimized comparison.

\subsection{Deterministic artifacts and hostile inputs}
PDF rebuilding occurs in a new external directory with fixed source date, fixed metadata conventions, and shell escape disabled. Compiler auxiliary files and visual-review images stay outside the sealed release. ZIP construction fixes member order, timestamps, regular-file modes, and compression settings. A second build is compared byte-for-byte before delivery.

Archive checking validates the independently supplied SHA256, exact member inventory and sizes, canonical paths, regular-file modes, permitted compression, and all payload bytes \emph{before} any extraction. It rejects traversal, absolute paths, backslashes, duplicate names, symlinks, special files, encrypted members, and additions. Extraction occurs only in an external temporary directory and is followed by the same identity gate. Regression cases exercise these boundaries as well as modified sources, metadata, receipts, and output destinations. External QA records the actually executed checks and final hashes; the article does not silently substitute a proposed test plan for passing results.

\section{Conclusions}\label{ptr:la:sec:conclusion}
The upper construction is now a fixed colour generator and finite perturbation compiler with a fixed actual start and a proved exclusive accepting port. Its small-state logical computation, enormous but finite physical instruction grammar, and infinite growing trajectory are linked by separate exact arguments. The history language, shared ownership, partial headers, marker turns, and long return channel are necessary parts of that link.

The one-visit lower theorem and two-visit upper construction match in their rule, initialization class, global visit quantifier, and finite pre-departure observable. This supplies the stated sharp boundary without changing physical halting into a head-port event by convention. The qualitative universality and two-visit principle retain their published credit, and the arbitrary-program U15 compiler retains its published dependency.

Natural next steps are a proof-assistant formalization of the finite-history composition and arithmetic selector proofs, a materially smaller literal layout, and a complete raw-input arithmetic composition with all initialization costs included. None is required for the present literal U15-to-ant equivalence, and none is claimed as already supplied by it.

\section{Failure modes explicitly excluded}\label{ptr:la:app:failures}
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{A true Boolean table without a physical route.} The full-round and global controller proofs supply the missing trajectory and ports
\item \textbf{A crossing read in the wrong history.} FIRST-before-SECOND is part of the resource language, and marker branches avoid missing FIRST events
\item \textbf{Four visits hidden at an alias.} Complete storage boxes have one merged optional-WRITE/READ history, never a sum of separate maxima
\item \textbf{Uninitialized skipped inputs.} The left-edge mask ignores the skipped state, and W-only plus header-E-only histories handle the untouched physical resources
\item \textbf{A malformed CA transition called valid simulation.} The total extension is explicit; unique-head preservation is a separate theorem
\item \textbf{A complemented accepting bit.} The exact wire 885 and 8137-row transport are checked at all Boolean inputs before the observer DUP
\item \textbf{A false later visit to the accepting cell.} Optional output reads and the next-row alias are included in local exclusivity, while global phase and margin arguments exclude other routes
\item \textbf{An anchor run charged to neither past nor future.} The anchor is an explicit 2806-cell net initial patch; the virtual prefix never physically runs, and its setup flips are not duplicated
\item \textbf{Zero-only inputs silently shortened.} The right marker's position depends on the actual word lengths even when popcount is zero
\item \textbf{An enormous sampled circuit described as exhaustive.} The row decoder has an all-interval arithmetic proof; only the 8137-row observer transfer and bounded local cases are eagerly expanded
\item \textbf{A full replay that changes the frozen packet.} The scientific driver is staged in a mutable external copy because its root receipt write changes metadata
\item \textbf{Free arithmetic preprocessing.} The raw input, initial board, phase, anchor, bounds, and accepting endpoint are not included in an unproved 174-operation composition
\end{enumerate}

\section{A separately paid arithmetic endpoint}\label{ptr:la:app:endpoint}
This appendix attaches the single reachable accepting clause to an existing bounded-board history relation. It is a separate endpoint component, not a construction of the arithmetic initial board or a universal combined operation bound. The complete own-code endpoint source, predecessor forms, independent audits, six arithmetic DAGs, and exact ledgers are included in \codeu{evidence/endpoint/}.

\subsection{Inherited interface and counting convention}
The parent history relation supplies existing integer ports $W$, $H=\mathrm{FinalHead}$, and $Z=\mathrm{FinalSignPlus}$. Its theorem guarantees integers $w,h,j$ satisfying
\[
 w\ge3\text{ odd},\quad h\ge2\text{ even},\quad
 W=3^w,\quad Q=W^h,\quad H=3^j<Q,\quad0\le j<wh.
\]
The canonical board coordinates are $x=j\bmod w$ and $y=\lfloor j/w\rfloor$, with east/south-positive coordinates. The final snapshot is after a certified positive number of steps and before the next turn/flip/move. There is no edge wrapping or reflection. The inherited sign is $(Z-1)/H\in\{0,1\}$: on a white site $x+y$ odd, zero means east; on a black site it means north. Checkerboard parity is unrelated to the mutable binary ant colour.

The pinned bounded-board component has five positive parameter ports, 61 positive unknowns, and 48 equations at a recorded cost of 174 operations. Those counts and its power/head geometry theorem are inherited assumptions here; this appendix neither re-proves them nor executes upstream source. $W$ is reused from an existing witness, and $H,Z$ are existing parameter ports. A multiplication costs M, an addition or subtraction costs A, and fixed literals and aliases cost no operation. Asserting equality of already built expressions adds no arithmetic gate. Arithmetic gate outputs are expressions, not separately quantified witnesses, and intermediates may be zero or negative unless positivity is explicitly required.

For the normalized single port set
\[
 u=481238074400,\quad x_0=481225262775,\quad
 v=576000,\quad y_0=29948.
\]
Both periods are even, $x_0$ is odd, and $y_0$ is even. Use prescribed fixed integers
\[
 K=3^{x_0},\qquad C=3^u-1,\qquad D=C-1,
\]
and computed variable powers $A=W^v$, $T=W^{y_0}$. These constant recipes specify exact integers without expanding their enormous digits. In the free-fixed-numeral model $K,C,D$ are fixed coefficients, never existentially chosen values. In the stricter source they are computed from literal 3 and literal 1 with all operations charged.

\subsection{Three witnesses and three equations}
Supply exactly three positive witnesses $X_+,Y_+,B$. Compute
\[
 U=CX_+-D=1+C(X_+-1),\qquad
 V_*=1+(A-1)(Y_+-1),\qquad P=KU.
\]
Here $V_*$ is an arithmetic factor, distinct from the physical block height $V$ elsewhere in this report. Assert
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:endpoint-three}
 P+B=W,\qquad (PT)V_*=H,\qquad Z=1.
\end{equation}
The positive adapters permit zero underlying quotients: $X_+=1$ gives $U=1$, and $Y_+=1$ gives $V_*=1$.

\begin{theorem}[Endpoint equivalence]\label{ptr:la:thm:endpoint}
Under the inherited history assumptions, positive witnesses for~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:endpoint-three} exist if and only if the canonical final coordinate satisfies
\[
 x\equiv x_0\pmod u,\qquad y\equiv y_0\pmod v,
\]
and its incoming heading is east. No divisibility relation between the board width and either period is assumed.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Because $X_+,Y_+\ge1$, both $U,V_*\ge1$. The head identity
\[
3^j=KT V_* U
\]
forces each positive integer factor $U,V_*$ to be a power of 3. Write $U=3^a$, $V_*=3^b$ with $a,b\ge0$. Their defining equations imply
\[
3^u-1\mid3^a-1,\qquad 3^{wv}-1\mid3^b-1.
\]
For integers $d>0,k\ge0$,
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:divisor}
3^d-1\mid3^k-1\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad d\mid k.
\end{equation}
For the forward direction write $k=qd+r$, $0\le r<d$. Reduction modulo $3^d-1$ leaves $3^r-1$, which lies between zero and $3^d-2$, and vanishes only at $r=0$. The reverse direction is the geometric-sum factorization, including $k=0$.

Hence $a=um$ and $b=wvn$ for $m,n\ge0$. Put $x^*=x_0+um$ and $y^*=y_0+vn$. Equality of powers in the head equation gives $j=x^*+wy^*$. The strictly positive column slack gives $P<W$, hence $3^{x^*}<3^w$ and $0\le x^*<w$. Thus this is the unique canonical row-major decomposition: $x=x^*$ and $y=y^*$. The inherited $j<wh$ then yields $y<h$, so no extra row slack is needed. Since $x^*$ is odd and $y^*$ even, the site is white; $Z=1$ selects the inherited zero sign, therefore east.

Conversely suppose the stated residues and east heading hold. Canonical nonnegative coordinates give $x=x_0+um$, $y=y_0+vn$ with $m,n\ge0$. Choose
\[
 X_+=1+\frac{3^{um}-1}{3^u-1},\qquad
 Y_+=1+\frac{W^{vn}-1}{W^v-1},\qquad
 B=W-3^x.
\]
The geometric-sum identities make the quotients nonnegative integers, including zero. Since $x<w$, $B>0$. These witnesses make $U=3^{um}$ and $V_*=W^{vn}$, so all of~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:endpoint-three} follow from $H=3^{x+wy}$ and the white/east sign convention. The witnesses are uniquely determined by the inherited history and endpoint.
\end{proof}

Prime factorization in this proof is a mathematical inference, not an uncharged factorization instruction. The column slack is essential: with smaller illustrative parameters $u=v=2$, $x_0=1$, $y_0=0$, $w=3$, and underlying quotients $X_+-1=10$, $Y_+-1=0$, the unconstrained exponent is $j=5$. Its canonical coordinate is $(2,1)$, outside the intended residues, because the factor representing the column carried into the row. Positive $B$ rules this out.

\subsection{Fully explicit shared power chains}
The joint variable-power chain starts at $P_0=W$ and squares to $P_{19}$, costing 19M with $P_i=W^{2^i}$. The binary support of $y_0$ is
\[
\{2,3,4,5,6,7,10,12,13,14\},
\]
whose product costs 9M; the support of $v$ is $\{9,11,14,15,19\}$, costing 4M. Thus both powers $T,A$ cost exactly $32$M.

For fixed powers, square literal 3 through level 38, costing 38M. The binary supports of $x_0,u$ are
\begin{align*}
\operatorname{bits}(x_0)&=\{0,1,2,4,5,7,9,10,14,15,17,22,24,25,27,36,37,38\},\\
\operatorname{bits}(u)&=\{5,10,14,17,18,26,27,36,37,38\}.
\end{align*}
The common eight positions are $\{5,10,14,17,27,36,37,38\}$. Build their product in 7M and reuse it: multiply by the ten $x_0$-only powers in 10M for $K$, and by the two $u$-only powers in 2M for $3^u$. Both fixed powers cost $38+7+10+2=57$M. Computing $C=3^u-1$ and $D=C-1$ adds 2A. No giant power is materialized during verification; integer exponent propagation proves the exact targets at every multiplication gate.

The folded non-power source has exactly the following ten gates, with $H_y=Y_+-1$:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{ll@{\qquad}ll}\toprule
Expression & Cost & Expression & Cost\\\midrule
$H_y=Y_+-1$ & A & $V_*=1+J$ & A\\
$E=CX_+$ & M & $P=KU$ & M\\
$U=E-D$ & A & $P+B$ & A\\
$A_-=A-1$ & A & $PT$ & M\\
$J=A_-H_y$ & M & $(PT)V_*$ & M\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Therefore the exact displayed-source ledgers are
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:la:eq:ledger-endpoint}
\begin{aligned}
\text{Prescribed }K,C,D:&\quad32\mathrm M+(5\mathrm M+5\mathrm A)
=37\mathrm M+5\mathrm A=42,\\
\text{Only literals }1,3:&\quad(57\mathrm M+2\mathrm A)+32\mathrm M+(5\mathrm M+5\mathrm A)
=94\mathrm M+7\mathrm A=101.
\end{aligned}
\end{equation}
These are construction costs, not optimality statements. The historical unfurled fixed-numeral source uses 43 operations. Folding the horizontal expression to $CX_+-D$ saves one A in the fixed-numeral model; constructing the additional prescribed constant $D$ consumes that saving in the strict model, which remains 101.

\subsection{Five witnesses with a smaller endpoint degree}
Alternatively supply positive $U,V_*,U_q,V_q,B$ and assert
\begin{align}
 U+D&=CU_q,\label{ptr:la:eq:endpoint-five}\\
 (V_*-1)+(A-1)&=(A-1)V_q,\nonumber\\
 KU+B&=W,\nonumber\\
 ((KU)T)V_*&=H,\nonumber\\
 Z&=1.\nonumber
\end{align}
The first two equations force $U=1+C(U_q-1)$ and $V_*=1+(A-1)(V_q-1)$. Thus the correspondence $U_q=X_+$, $V_q=Y_+$, with these forced values of $U,V_*$ and unchanged $B$, is a bijection with the three-witness solutions. In particular it includes both zero-quotient boundaries and introduces no spurious accepting endpoints.

The non-power gates are $U+D$ (A), $CU_q$ (M), $A-1$ (A), $V_*-1$ (A), their sum (A), $(A-1)V_q$ (M), $KU$ (M), $KU+B$ (A), $(KU)T$ (M), and its product with $V_*$ (M). This again is 5M+5A, so both ledgers in~\eqref{ptr:la:eq:ledger-endpoint} remain unchanged. Writing the second equality in the displayed form uses only literal 1, reuses $A-1$, and does not hide a free literal 2.

After expanding the gate expressions, treat the fixed numeral recipes as coefficients and the inherited ports and supplied witnesses as variables. The three endpoint residuals have exact total degrees
\[
 (1,605950,1).
\]
The head residual's highest-degree monomial is $W^{v+y_0}X_+Y_+$ with nonzero coefficient $KC>0$, so its degree is exactly $v+y_0+2$, not merely an upper bound. For the five-witness form the degrees are
\[
 (1,576001,1,29950,1).
\]
The vertical residual has the nonzero term $-W^vV_q$, degree $v+1$; the head residual has $KUW^{y_0}V_*$, degree $y_0+2$. These are endpoint-only expanded degrees before substituting any parent expression or combining equations into one polynomial.

\subsection{Verification and remaining obligations}
The endpoint verifier checks every source input, dependency, gate opcode, live gate, shared exponent, equation, and fixed-numeral recipe. An independent whole-DAG sparse polynomial expansion uses a formal symbol for base 3 and compares the complete residual polynomials with the stated equations. It verifies nonzero leading coefficients after specializing that symbol to 3, avoiding a false exact-degree claim from an untested symbolic cancellation. Four own commands regenerate six DAGs and four receipts exactly. Supporting regressions cover 17280 small candidate endpoints, horizontal-fold positivity, and 192 witness-correspondence cases. The arbitrary-integer theorem is the proof above, not extrapolation from those finite examples.

This attachment applies to the normalized literal port proved in Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:observer}, and only because the first physical clause is unreachable on valid primary loads. It recognizes an endpoint of an already valid bounded-board history. Constructing the correctly translated periodic board, choosing and encoding its finite rectangle, preparing the initial integer from raw input, and paying any dilation or final single-polynomial combination remain separate tasks. Thus neither $174+42$ nor $174+101$ is asserted here as a complete universal operation count.

{\small\wtag\ Report~44 attaches this selector in its folded three-witness
form (Section~\ref{ptr:pc:sec:splice}). In Report~48's source the programme
later replaced the selector's own power $W^{576000}$ by the initializer's
already paid one, deleting nine multiplications
