\part{The arithmetic certificate: paid initialization and the complete positive polynomial (Reports 42 and 44)}\label{ptr:part:cert}
\ptrstart{42}{Part III: Report 42}
\phantomsection\label{ptr:ai:sec:intro}
\noindent\wtag\ \textbf{This Part} merges two reports. Report~42 builds a
fixed polynomial system that turns two raw positive integers into the exact
initialized board of Part~II's ant (Theorem~\ref{ptr:ai:thm:main}); Report~44
joins it with the inherited bounded-history relation and Part~II's endpoint
into one polynomial, the Part's main theorem (Theorem~\ref{ptr:pc:thm:complete}).
Report~44's archive is a \emph{recovered edition}: a filesystem reset before
delivery lost the originally verified files, and the edition was rebuilt
from retained work (its own edition note, below). Only this edition exists;
there is no earlier Report~44 in the repository to compare. At placement its
two complete arithmetic streams were regenerated on a copy and reproduced the
SHA-256 identities that the earlier audit had recorded
(Appendix~\ref{ptr:pc:app:pins}). The scope of the recognized language is
item~\ref{ptr:it:scope} of the preface to the four Parts: the fixed U15
sentinel-pair halting language, not an ordinary-input loader for the
programme's input convention. The programme reviewed both reports in commit
\texttt{cfbe37de7}~\cite{ptr:w:rev4048}: no defect in the read algebra and
composition; it independently checked the 1055-row recoder's 228 residuals
and the 174-row history DAG's closure, count and degree, but did not re-prove
the history theorem or regenerate the complete streams.

\section*{Report 42: the paid arithmetic initialization}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Report 42: the paid arithmetic initialization}
\noindent\wtag\ \emph{A Paid Arithmetic Initialization for a
Fixed Periodic Langton Ant. Uniform binary word recoding with an explicit Pell
macro}, Research Report~42, dated 3 October 2026, author line ``Mathematical
research report''; batch-88 manuscript~04, archive
\path{Arithmetic_Initialization_for_a_Periodic_Langton_Ant_Package.zip}. Pins:
ProveIt \texttt{5883b08b7} for the parent history note, Mathlib
\texttt{ac77769f} for the Pell theorems, and Report~40's delivered PDF
(Appendix~\ref{ptr:ai:app:provenance}). Numbered 42.$k$; labels
\texttt{ptr:ai:}; files \texttt{03-ant-init-}.

\begin{quote}\small\noindent\textbf{Abstract (Report 42).} We give a fixed finite conjunction of polynomial equations that turns two ordinary positive binary-sentinel integers into the exact initialized board and north-facing head required by a bounded-history encoding of one fixed periodic Langton ant. The initialization component has 2306387 arithmetic operations, 234 equations, and 396 new strictly positive internal witnesses, independent of the input word lengths. A fully streamed single-equation conversion uses 2307088 operations and has exact total degree 2304000. Its five inherited expression ports remain explicit. The construction combines a paid periodic-rectangle and finite-patch formula with a fixed all-length binary digit recoder. The latter uses binomial-coefficient parity, coefficient extraction, two residue identities, and fourteen calls to a 70-operation polynomial power macro specialized from pinned constructive Pell theorems. Forward and reversed word values are unique; the existential witness tuple is not. Prescribed enormous background coefficients are specified by a fixed source recipe rather than expanded. A separate literal-1-and-3 ledger charges their astronomical construction. We also give the fixed-length predecessor, an elementary witness-free polynomial obstruction, source-level accounting, and precisely delimited reproducibility checks. This is an initialization theorem conditional on the earlier literal-ant and bounded-history interfaces, not a new global universal arithmetic bound or a re-proof of the inherited Pell theorem.
\end{quote}
\section{What the construction establishes}\label{ptr:ai:sec:result}
The gap addressed here is arithmetic initialization. An already encoded board word is not the same input interface as an ordinary binary integer representing a source tape. A periodic colour query is not a free arithmetic instruction either. We therefore pay for the raw word conversion, its orientation and zero padding, the periodic rectangle, the finite anchor and source-field corrections, the board dimensions, and the initial head placement.

The geometric input comes from Research Report 40~\cite{ptr:ai:report40}: a fixed binary periodic colouring, a fixed 2806-cell startup anchor, and a literal finite input loader for a fixed U15 source machine. The finite-history relation comes from the pinned 174-operation note~\cite{ptr:ai:history}. Its hypotheses, not new assertions of this report, provide
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:parentports}
 W=3^w,\qquad W_p=3^{w-1},\qquad Q=W^h,
 \qquad \IH,\quad \IM,
\end{equation}
with $w\ge3$ odd and $h\ge2$ even. Here $\IM-1$ encodes an initially Boolean ternary board, and $\IH$ is a north-facing head at an even flattened index. The five displayed quantities are inherited expression ports. They are neither free unconstrained replacements for the parent theorem nor new witnesses counted in this component.

Put
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:periods}
 u=481238074400,\qquad v=576000.
\end{equation}
Physical coordinates $(a,b)$ increase east and south. The frozen start is $(288650,75,E)$. Normalize and translate by
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:coordinates}
 X=b-75,\quad Y=288650-a,\qquad x=X+u,\quad y=Y+h/2.
\end{equation}
The rotation preserves orientation and makes the initial heading north. The normalized periods are $(u,v)$.

\begin{theorem}[Fixed all-length initialization]\label{ptr:ai:thm:main}
Under the inherited contracts in~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:parentports} and the literal interface theorem of Report 40, there is a fixed arithmetic DAG using only $+,-,\times$, prescribed fixed integer numerals, and strictly positive existential witnesses with the following property. For every two positive raw integers, its equations hold exactly when the inherited initial memory and head are the literal initialized board and start associated with the two sentinel-decoded binary words, placed on a permitted rectangle. If the decoded word lengths are $L,R$ and $m=L+R+1$, the permitted rectangles are exactly
\[
 w=1+au\quad(a\ge2),\qquad h=2kv\quad(k\ge m).
\]
The component uses $1153192$ multiplications and $1153195$ additions or subtractions, hence $2306387$ operations, 234 equations, and 396 new positive internal witnesses. It has no gate family indexed by $L$ or $R$. Every finite prefix of the infinite literal trajectory fits in some permitted rectangle.
\end{theorem}
The theorem is proved by the word recoder in Sections~\ref{ptr:ai:sec:recoder}--\ref{ptr:ai:sec:pair}, the geometry and memory formula in Sections~\ref{ptr:ai:sec:geometry}--\ref{ptr:ai:sec:patch}, and the literal splice in Section~\ref{ptr:ai:sec:ledger}. ``Exactly when'' describes the initialization relation within the parent's valid geometry. Without that inherited geometry, the displayed polynomial equations are not claimed to characterize powers of three or a valid bounded history on their own.

The numerals in the headline ledger are fixed independently of both raw inputs. Some are enormous. Section~\ref{ptr:ai:sec:strict} replaces them by an explicitly counted literal source prefix from 1 and 3 only. We distinguish a finite, exactly specified source from its practical expansion, and a finite regression check from a theorem covering all lengths.

\section{Arithmetic and raw input conventions}\label{ptr:ai:sec:conventions}
An arithmetic gate evaluates one addition, subtraction, or multiplication of earlier expressions or fixed numerals. Gate outputs are expressions, not automatically quantified unknowns. Equality assertions have zero gate cost. A multiplication by a fixed numeral still costs one multiplication. No simplifier removes gates with zero or repeated operands in the source counted here.

All quantified unknowns are strictly positive integers. For a mathematical quantity $z\ge0$, introduce $z^+>0$ and use the expression $z=z^+-1$, charging its subtraction. Intermediate expressions may be zero or negative. In particular, a nonnegative output expression must not silently be reclassified as a positive unknown.

For a nearest-head-first word $d_0,\ldots,d_{n-1}$, the ordinary most-significant-bit-first sentinel convention is
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:sentinel}
 \Raw=2^n+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}d_j2^{n-1-j},\qquad d_j\in\{0,1\}.
\end{equation}
Every positive integer has exactly one such word. The empty word has code 1. A length-$n$ all-zero word has code $2^n$, so zero padding is retained. The two raw parameters are $\Raw_L,\Raw_R$.

\subsection{The fixed-length predecessor}
If $L$ and $R$ are fixed before constructing a circuit, introduce $L+R$ positive bit adapters. For each bit $b=b^+-1$, assert $b(b-1)=0$. Reconstruct each raw integer by Horner evaluation in radix 2, starting at 1. This is a correct family indexed by two lengths. It does not, by itself, give a single relation for all raw integers. The fixed-length family is useful both as a direct construction and as a source-polynomial cross-check of the fixed outer formula.

\begin{proposition}[Witness-free polynomial obstruction]\label{ptr:ai:prop:obstruction}
Fix $G=3^{wv}>1$ for any valid board width. No finite witness-free circuit over fixed constants using only $+,-,\times$ computes the sentinel-preserving dilation $f(\Raw)=G^n+\sum_{j<n}d_jG^{n-1-j}$ for every positive sentinel input.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Such a circuit computes a polynomial $f$. For the all-zero words, $f(2^n)=G^n$. A nonzero polynomial of degree $d$ has ratio $f(2^{n+1})/f(2^n)\to2^d$; for a nonzero constant the ratio is 1. The required ratio is the odd power of three $G>1$, which is neither possibility. Enormous fixed coefficients do not change this asymptotic argument.
\end{proof}
This obstruction concerns witness-free polynomial evaluation only. The existential construction below has additional witnesses and is fully consistent with it.

\section{One fixed all-length digit recoder}\label{ptr:ai:sec:recoder}
Fix input ports $C\ge2$ and $\Raw>0$. We seek the uniquely determined length $n$, power $P=C^n$, and either the forward value $\sum_jd_jC^{n-1-j}$ or the reverse value $\sum_jd_jC^j$. The variable length is an existential expression $n=n^+-1$; it never controls the number of source gates.

Introduce seven powers
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:sevenpowers}
 \begin{gathered}
 P=C^n,\quad E=2^n,\quad B=2^{CP+2},\quad F=B^n,\\
 \Lambda=2^{U+1},\qquad Y=\Lambda^D,\qquad Z=(\Lambda+1)^U.
 \end{gathered}
\end{equation}
Every displayed power will be replaced by the fixed polynomial macro in Section~\ref{ptr:ai:sec:pell}. In this section $U,D,x,V,q_2,q_C,q,o,r$ are nonnegative; $s_x,s_c,s_r,s_V$ are strictly positive. With $c=2o+1$, impose the following nine equations:
\begin{align}
 F-1&=(B-1)U,& x&=\Raw-E,\label{ptr:ai:eq:recode1}\\
 x+s_x&=E,&D&=q_2(B-2)+x,\label{ptr:ai:eq:recode2}\\
 Z&=(q\Lambda+c)Y+r,& c+s_c&=\Lambda,\label{ptr:ai:eq:recode3}\\
 r+s_r&=Y,&V+s_V&=P,\label{ptr:ai:eq:recode4}\\
 D&=q_C(B-C)+V &&\text{(forward)},\label{ptr:ai:eq:forward}
\end{align}
with the last equation replaced, for reverse output, by
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:reverse}
 PD=q_C(BC-1)+CV.
\end{equation}
There are nine equations in either choice, not ten. The separate positive adapter for $x$ matters: the expression $\Raw-E$ alone does not establish its nonnegativity.

\begin{lemma}[Auxiliary radix margins]\label{ptr:ai:lem:radix}
The deterministic choice $B=2^{CP+2}$ satisfies
\[
 B>C+P,\qquad B>2^n+2.
\]
Consequently $B-2>2^n-1$, $B-C>P-1$, and $CP<BC-1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Write $t=CP$. Because $C\ge2$, $P=C^n\ge1$, we have $t\ge2$, $t\ge C+P-1$, and $t\ge2P\ge2^{n+1}$. The elementary inequality $2^{t+2}>2t+2$ proves both margins. The last conclusion follows from $B>P+1$ and $C\ge2$.
\end{proof}

\begin{lemma}[Binary support from coefficient parity]\label{ptr:ai:lem:parity}
For $B$ a power of two and $U=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}B^i$, the coefficient $\binom UD$ is odd exactly when
\[
 D=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}b_iB^i,\qquad b_i\in\{0,1\}.
\]
For $D>U$, the coefficient is interpreted as zero.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
In $\mathbb F_2[z]$, repeated squaring gives $(1+z)^{2^a}=1+z^{2^a}$. Since every $B^i$ is a power of two,
\[
 (1+z)^U=\prod_{i=0}^{n-1}(1+z^{B^i}).
\]
The subset sums of the distinct place values $B^i$ are distinct for $B\ge2$. Their coefficients are exactly one, and all other coefficients vanish. This is the needed binary instance of Lucas parity, proved directly rather than used as an unexpanded black box.
\end{proof}

\begin{theorem}[Dilation graph]\label{ptr:ai:thm:dilation}
After replacing the seven powers by their polynomial macros, equations~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:recode1}--\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:forward}, or their reverse variant, have positive witnesses exactly for the power $P=C^n$ and the appropriate value $V$ of the unique sentinel word of $\Raw$. The forward graph uses 524 operations, 196 positive internal witnesses, and 114 equations; the reverse graph uses 527 operations and the same witness and equation counts.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}[Soundness]
The first equation gives $U=1+B+\cdots+B^{n-1}$, including $U=0$ when $n=0$. Each coefficient of $(1+z)^U$ is at most $2^U<\Lambda$. Thus the base-$\Lambda$ digit at position $D$ in $Z=(1+\Lambda)^U$ is $\binom UD$. Equations~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:recode3}--\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:recode4} assert $0<c<\Lambda$, $0\le r<Y=\Lambda^D$ and
\[
 Z=(q\Lambda+c)\Lambda^D+r.
\]
They therefore extract that digit exactly. As $c=2o+1$, the digit is odd. Lemma~\ref{ptr:ai:lem:parity} forces $D=\sum_i b_iB^i$ with $b_i\in\{0,1\}$, and in particular excludes $D>U$ without a separate upper-bound equation.

Modulo $B-2$, this $D$ equals $\sum_i b_i2^i$. Both the latter number and $x$ belong to $[0,2^n)$, which lies below the modulus by Lemma~\ref{ptr:ai:lem:radix}. Hence $x=\sum_i b_i2^i$. The two equations involving $x$ force $2^n\le\Raw<2^{n+1}$, so $n$ is the sentinel length and $b_i=d_{n-1-i}$.

For forward output, reduction modulo $B-C$ gives $D\equiv\sum_i b_iC^i$. Both the candidate and $V$ lie in $[0,P)$, below the modulus, so they agree. For reverse output, $BC\equiv1\pmod{BC-1}$ gives, for $i<n$,
\[
 C^nB^i\equiv C^{n-i}\pmod{BC-1}.
\]
Thus $PD\equiv C\sum_i b_iC^{n-1-i}$. Both this candidate remainder and $CV$ lie in $[0,CP)$, below $BC-1$. Equation~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:reverse} forces the reverse value. No negative exponent is used when $n=0$.
\end{proof}
\begin{proof}[Completeness]
For a given word, set the seven powers as displayed and set $D=\sum_i b_iB^i$. Its extracted coefficient is odd by Lemma~\ref{ptr:ai:lem:parity}; take $c=\binom UD$ and $o=(c-1)/2$. Ordinary Euclidean division gives $q,r$. The raw and forward quotient differences are nonnegative since $B>C\ge2$. For the reverse quotient, termwise $C^nB^i\ge C^{n-i}$ for $i<n$ makes the divisible difference nonnegative. Thus all $q_2,q_C$ also have the required signs. The strict remainder and value margins give positive slacks. Positive adapters then represent every quantity that vanishes. For the empty word,
\[
 \Raw=P=E=F=1,\quad U=D=V=0,\quad
 \Lambda=2,\quad Y=Z=c=1.
\]
These values satisfy all equations and positive-slack conditions. Completeness of the power macro supplies its witnesses. The operation counts are checked in Section~\ref{ptr:ai:sec:ledger}.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Functional outputs and nonunique witnesses.}
The output $P,V$ is unique. The complete existential fiber is not: paired-quotient congruence witnesses in the Pell macro may be simultaneously increased. The relation is not claimed to be single-fold or finite-fold.

\section{The explicit polynomial power macro}\label{ptr:ai:sec:pell}
This section supplies the promised fixed source for exponentiation. It specializes the constructive Pell theorems \codeu{Pell.matiyasevic} and \codeu{Pell.eq_pow_of_pell} in the pinned mathlib source~\cite{ptr:ai:pell}; it does not derive exponentiation from a general undecidability theorem. The source is archived for reading and attribution, not executed or recompiled by this work.

{\small\wtag\ The archive's copy of the cited Mathlib file
(\path{Mathlib/NumberTheory/PellMatiyasevic.lean}, 39,986 bytes, SHA-256
\path{993760c797ad0ff66fa77064a616fce779550bc745cbf6c44e04f392fd0bed0a}, at
Mathlib commit \texttt{ac77769fabe23cb237559e7f56578dbead91499f}) and its
Apache-2.0 licence file are not shipped in this report; they are third-party
files, available at that commit, and the shipped pin record is
\path{data/03-ant-init-science-dependencies-mathlib-primary_source_pin.json}.
The repository's own Lean development uses \path{Pell.matiyasevic} as well
(\path{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem/Lean/Diophantine/Paper1976/Cor26.lean});
the specialization below is not formalized.\par}

For $a\ge2$, define Pell coordinates by
\[
 X_0(a)=1,\quad Y_0(a)=0,
 \qquad
 \begin{aligned}
 X_{j+1}(a)&=aX_j(a)+(a^2-1)Y_j(a),\\
 Y_{j+1}(a)&=X_j(a)+aY_j(a).
 \end{aligned}
\]
The first cited theorem characterizes an exact indexed pair $(X_k(a),Y_k(a))$ by three Pell equations, divisibility and congruences, with a separate zero-index branch. The second characterizes $b^k$ through that indexed pair and an additional growth Pell equation. We give every polynomial equation of the specialization so that its accounting and domain restrictions are inspectable.

To assert $O=b^e$ for $b\ge2$, $e\ge0$, $O>0$, construct $k=e+1$ and $m=bO$. Introduce 25 positive unknowns. Eleven are
\[
 w,A_0,\Theta,g,x,y,p,q,s,t,B_0,
 \qquad a=A_0+1,\quad \beta=B_0+1.
\]
Three more are positive adapters for nonnegative gaps $\delta_{wb},\delta_{wk},\delta_{yk}$. A fourth is a positive strict slack $S$. Two are positive $q_b,q_v$. The last eight are positive adapters for the nonnegative variables
\[
 \alpha_1,\alpha_2,\sigma_1,\sigma_2,
 \tau_1,\tau_2,\rho_1,\rho_2.
\]
Impose exactly the following fifteen equations:
\begin{align}
 x^2&=1+(a^2-1)y^2,\label{ptr:ai:eq:pell1}\\
 p^2&=1+(a^2-1)q^2,\\
 s^2&=1+(\beta^2-1)t^2,\\
 \beta&=1+4yq_b,\\
 \beta+p\alpha_1&=a+p\alpha_2,\\
 q&=y^2q_v,\\
 s+p\sigma_1&=x+p\sigma_2,\\
 t+4y\tau_1&=k+4y\tau_2,\\
 y&=k+\delta_{yk},\label{ptr:ai:eq:pell9}\\
 w&=b+\delta_{wb},\\
 w&=k+\delta_{wk},\\
 \Theta&=m+S,\\
 a^2&=1+\bigl((w+1)^2-1\bigr)(wg)^2,\label{ptr:ai:eq:growthpell}\\
 2ab&=\Theta+(b^2+1),\\
 x+\Theta\rho_1&=y(a-b)+m+\Theta\rho_2.\label{ptr:ai:eq:pell15}
\end{align}
The paired quotients represent unrestricted signed congruence quotients as differences of two nonnegative integers. No positivity assumption on a signed quotient has been smuggled into these equations.

\begin{lemma}[Polynomial power macro]\label{ptr:ai:lem:exp}
The fifteen equations above hold with positive witnesses exactly when $O=b^e$. Their literal source uses $31$ multiplications and $39$ additions or subtractions, for 70 operations, with 25 positive internal witnesses.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}[Soundness from the pinned constructive theorem]
Equations~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:pell1}--\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:pell9} meet the positive-index, nonzero branch of \codeu{Pell.matiyasevic}: $a,\beta\ge2$, $y\ge k\ge1$, $q>0$, and the displayed divisibilities and congruences hold. The omitted branch would require $y=0$, so $(x,y)=(X_k(a),Y_k(a))$.

The source power theorem uses natural subtraction $a-b$, whereas our arithmetic uses integer subtraction. They coincide here for a proved reason. Equation~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:growthpell}, with $w\ge b\ge2$ and $g\ge1$, gives the elementary bound
\[
 a^2=1+(w^2+2w)w^2g^2>w^2.
\]
As $a,w$ are positive, $a>w\ge b$, so $a-b$ is nonnegative.
The remaining equations now meet \codeu{Pell.eq_pow_of_pell}: $w\ge b,k$, $\Theta=2ab-b^2-1$, $m<\Theta$, the growth equation, and $x\equiv y(a-b)+m\pmod\Theta$. That theorem gives $m=b^k$. Cancelling $b>0$ from $m=bO$ proves $O=b^e$.
\end{proof}
\begin{proof}[Completeness and positivity]
The cited construction takes $w=\max(b,k)$, $a=X_w(w+1)$, and $g=Y_w(w+1)/w$, all satisfying the required positive domains. It takes $x=X_k(a)$ and $y=Y_k(a)$, then uses a larger index for $p,q$, and a CRT-compatible $\beta\ge2$, followed by $s=X_k(\beta)$ and $t=Y_k(\beta)$. All these coordinates are positive because $k\ge1$. Since $\beta\equiv1\pmod{4y}$ and $\beta>1$, its quotient $q_b$ is at least one. Since $q>0$ and $y^2\mid q$, $q_v\ge1$. The source's strict bound $m<\Theta$ provides $S>0$. All weak gaps and paired congruence quotients admit their stated nonnegative representatives and hence positive adapters. Thus the stricter domains lose no valid power.
\end{proof}
The small Pell examples in the executable checks are regression tests, not a proof that spurious outputs are excluded. The all-input exclusion is supplied by the two cited constructive theorems and their explicit specialization above.

\section{The pair of word outputs}\label{ptr:ai:sec:pair}
Take $C=G=W^v$. Apply the reverse recoder to $\Raw_L$ and the forward recoder to $\Raw_R$. Denote their outputs by
\[
 P_L=G^L,\quad V_L=\sum_{j<L}\ell_jG^j,
 \qquad P_R=G^R,\quad V_R=\sum_{j<R}r_jG^{R-1-j}.
\]
Build
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:pair}
 A=P_LP_R,\qquad B_{\rm out}=P_R,\qquad
 T=(GP_R)V_L+V_R.
\end{equation}
This uses three multiplications and one addition. Thus
\[
 A=G^{L+R},\quad B_{\rm out}=G^R,\quad
 T=G^{R+1}\sum_{j<L}\ell_jG^j+\sum_{j<R}r_jG^{R-1-j}.
\]
Equivalently, $T$ is the base-$G$ Horner value of
\[
 \operatorname{reverse}(\ell),\ 0,\ r.
\]
The central zero is deliberate: the A0 primary head's nonzero state bit is added separately by the physical initialization formula. $B_{\rm out}$ is not either recoder's auxiliary power-of-two radix $B$.

There are three distinct interface conventions, whose ledgers must not be mixed:
\begin{enumerate}
\item In the inlined interface used here, $A,T$ are expressions and $B_{\rm out}$ aliases $P_R$. The pair has 1055 operations, 392 positive internal witnesses, and 228 equations
\item Exposing separate ports $A,B_{\rm out},T$ adds three equality assertions, giving 231 equations with the same operation and internal-witness counts. The last port is nonnegative, not necessarily positive
\item Exposing positive $T^+=T+1$ instead adds one operation, giving 1056. If all three ports are separately quantified, count three further positive unknowns outside the 392 internal ones. A consumer needing $T$ must also pay for $T^+-1$ unless that expression is already present
\end{enumerate}
The fixed splice uses the first convention; it never turns those three outputs into free raw parameters.

\section{A paid rectangle and initial head}\label{ptr:ai:sec:geometry}
Set $n=L+R$, $m=n+1$. The recoder provides the expressions $A=G^n$, $B_{\rm out}=G^R$, and $T$. Four additional unknowns are positive:
\[
 X_{\rm extra},\quad H,\quad K,\quad P_{\rm pad}.
\]
The fixed numerals $C_u=3^u$, $C_x=C_u-1$ are specified independently of the input. Compute $G=W^v$ once, with its charged binary multiplication chain, and impose
\begin{align}
 W_p-1&=C_x(X_{\rm extra}+1),\label{ptr:ai:eq:width}\\
 H-1&=(G-1)K,\\
 Q&=H^2,\\
 H&=P_{\rm pad}(AG),\label{ptr:ai:eq:padding}\\
 \IH&=C_uH.\label{ptr:ai:eq:head}
\end{align}
The expression $AG=G^{n+1}$ costs one multiplication. Reusing the parent's $W_p$ is an alias, not an uncharged division. A variant exposing only $W$ could introduce a new positive $W_p$ and assert $3W_p=W$, but that would add one witness, equation, and multiplication; it is not the displayed interface.

\begin{lemma}[Exact geometry]\label{ptr:ai:lem:geometry}
Within the parent geometry, these five equations are equivalent to
\[
 w=1+au\ (a\ge2),\quad h=2kv\ (k\ge m),\quad
 H=W^{kv},\quad P_{\rm pad}=G^{k-m},\quad \IH=3^uW^{kv}.
\]
The remaining $X_{\rm extra}$ and $K$ are uniquely determined positive integers.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
For $q\ge2$ and $d,e\ge1$, $q^d-1$ divides $q^e-1$ exactly when $d\mid e$: reduce $e$ modulo $d$ and use $0<q^r-1<q^d-1$ for $0<r<d$. Apply this first to~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:width}. It gives $u\mid w-1$. The quotient is one for $a=1$, whereas $X_{\rm extra}+1\ge2$, so $a\ge2$; every $a\ge2$ has a valid positive extra quotient.

Since the inherited $h$ is even and $H>0$, $H^2=W^h$ forces $H=W^{h/2}$. Divisibility by $G-1$ then gives $v\mid h/2$, say $h=2kv$, with $K=(G^k-1)/(G-1)>0$. Equation~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:padding} forces $k\ge m$ and $P_{\rm pad}=G^{k-m}$. The zero exponent gives the permitted value 1. Equation~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:head} places the initial head at $(u,kv)$. Its flattened index $u+wkv$ is even, since $u,v$ are even. All converse implications follow by substitution.
\end{proof}

\subsection{Support containment and arbitrary finite prefixes}
The anchor's physical ranges are
\[
 288617\le a\le289200,\qquad -144\le b\le76.
\]
After~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:coordinates}, these become $u-219\le x\le u+1$ and $kv-550\le y\le kv+33$. All variable source and marker changes have $x=u-21$, with least row $kv-vm+23945\ge23945$ and greatest row below $kv+33$. Thus all initialized cells and the head belong to every permitted rectangle: $w\ge2u+1$, $k\ge m\ge1$, and $kv+33<2kv$.

The global literal-interface proof bounds the physical coordinate $b$ below by $-144$ for every prefix. Hence $X\ge-219$ throughout the infinite trajectory. The fixed shift by $u$ suffices on that side. For any finite prefix the upper $X$ and both extremes of $Y$ are finite. Choosing $a$ and $k$ large enough places it inside a permitted rectangle. Conversely a bounded history that never leaves the rectangle evolves identically to the infinite literal board over its entire duration. Enlarging the rectangle neither creates nor removes an accepting event already inside that prefix.

\section{The exact periodic background}\label{ptr:ai:sec:background}
Let $c(a,b)$ be the frozen total binary colour function before all finite overrides. In normalized coordinates define
\[
 \mathcal B(i,j)=c(288650-j,i+75),
\]
with periods $(u,v)$. For $0\le j<v$, prescribe
\[
 \alpha_j=\sum_{i=0}^{u-1}\mathcal B(i,j)3^i,
 \qquad \beta_j=\mathcal B(0,j),
\]
and the fixed-coefficient polynomials
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:tilepolys}
 \mathcal A(W)=\sum_{j=0}^{v-1}\alpha_jW^j,
 \qquad \mathcal B_0(W)=\sum_{j=0}^{v-1}\beta_jW^j.
\end{equation}
These coefficients are fixed by the pinned colour-function recipe. They are neither existential colours nor input-dependent coefficient ports. A complete numerical expansion of an $\alpha_j$ would have roughly $10^{11}$ decimal digits; none is claimed to have been materialized.

Set $H_x=X_{\rm extra}+1$ and $H_y=K(H+1)$. Construct
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:background}
 \mathrm{Background}=H_y\bigl(H_x\mathcal A(W)+W_p\mathcal B_0(W)\bigr).
\end{equation}
\begin{lemma}[Rectangle identity]\label{ptr:ai:lem:background}
Equation~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:background} is exactly the row-major ternary encoding of the periodic binary background on the chosen $w\times h$ rectangle.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
By the width equation,
\[
 H_x=\frac{3^{au}-1}{3^u-1}=\sum_{s=0}^{a-1}3^{su}.
\]
This repeats each complete $u$-column row tile $a$ times. The $W_p=3^{au}$ term supplies the first column once more, giving width $au+1$. The supports are disjoint, so there is no ternary carry. Likewise
\[
 H_y=K(H+1)=\frac{Q-1}{G-1}=\sum_{t=0}^{2k-1}G^t
\]
repeats the $v$-row tile exactly $2k$ times. With $W=3^w$, the row supports are disjoint. This explains the extra boundary column: the even horizontal period is not incorrectly required to divide the odd board width.
\end{proof}

\section{The anchor and input corrections}\label{ptr:ai:sec:patch}
For each frozen anchor record $(a,b,c_{\rm old},c_{\rm new})$, put $\delta=c_{\rm new}-c_{\rm old}\in\{-1,1\}$. Define the degree-at-most-583 fixed polynomial
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:J}
 J(W)=\sum_{\rm anchor}\delta\,3^{b+144}W^{289200-a}.
\end{equation}
The exponents satisfy $0\le b+144\le220$ and $0\le289200-a\le583$. Its 584 signed coefficients are prescribed numerals. The anchor has 2806 distinct cells and already includes the left marker.

Let $C_{\rm base}=3^{u-219}$ and $C_{198}=3^{198}$. Using the recoder expressions, construct
\begin{align}
 E_{\rm tape}&=W^{24000}T+B_{\rm out},\label{ptr:ai:eq:Etape}\\
 Z_{\rm patch}&=W^{23945}+W^{263945}(W^{264000}+1)E_{\rm tape},\label{ptr:ai:eq:Zpatch}\\
 D_{\rm patch}&=W^{v-550}A J(W)-C_{198}(W+1)Z_{\rm patch},\label{ptr:ai:eq:Dpatch}\\
 \IM&=\mathrm{Background}+C_{\rm base}P_{\rm pad}D_{\rm patch}+1.\label{ptr:ai:eq:memory}
\end{align}
All variable-base fixed powers are charged by literal multiplication chains. The final $+1$ is the positive memory adapter.

\begin{theorem}[Exact initialization identity]\label{ptr:ai:thm:memory}
The right side of~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:memory}, minus one, is precisely the fixed literal background after its frozen anchor and the source input changes, translated as in~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:coordinates}. Its ternary digits are Boolean.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
An anchor cell contributes
\[
 \delta\,3^{u+b-75}W^{kv+288650-a}
 =C_{\rm base}P_{\rm pad}\delta\,3^{b+144}W^{vm+288650-a}.
\]
Since $vm+288650-a=(v-550)+vn+(289200-a)$, the full anchor contribution is the first term in $C_{\rm base}P_{\rm pad}D_{\rm patch}$.

A source field at slot $q$ of module $i$ changes exactly the two physical cells
\[
 (vi+704+24000q,54),\qquad(vi+705+24000q,54)
\]
from one to zero. After division by $C_{\rm base}P_{\rm pad}$ their negative contribution is
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:field}
 -C_{198}(W+1)W^{vm+287945-vi-24000q}.
\end{equation}
The right marker has $i=m,q=11$, giving exponent 23945. The left marker is omitted from this correction because its two changed cells already belong to the anchor.

At primary index $i$, a tape bit one uses fields $(i,13)$ and $(i+1,0)$. In~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:field} their exponents are
\[
 v(m-1-i)+551945,\qquad v(m-1-i)+287945.
\]
These are exactly the contribution of that digit to
\[
 W^{24000}W^{263945}(W^{264000}+1)T.
\]
The primary A0 head has value 2 at index $i=L$, using bit 1 rather than bit 0. Its fields $(L,14)$ and $(L+1,1)$ lower both exponents by 24000; their shared power of $G$ is $G^{m-1-L}=G^R=B_{\rm out}$. Thus~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:Etape} includes exactly the head contribution as well.

The frozen interface proves all variable source fields have old colour one. Their only overlap with the anchor is the agreeing left marker, already omitted, and all remaining fields are disjoint. Adding the signed changes to the exact background therefore leaves every ternary digit zero or one. The changed support has cardinality
\[
 2806+4(\pop\ell+\pop r+1)+2
 =2812+4(\pop\ell+\pop r).
\]
Leading zero symbols still alter $m$, the endpoint marker and the geometry; they are not erased.
\end{proof}

\section{Source splice and exact arithmetic ledgers}\label{ptr:ai:sec:ledger}
The source first computes $G=W^v$ once and uses that exact expression in both recoders and the board wrapper. The two recoders have disjoint witness prefixes. Their expressions $A,B_{\rm out},T$ are substituted into the wrapper before later gates are emitted, with no free dilation ports, no duplicate $G$ chain, and no extra output unknowns. The combined stream validates each opcode, each earlier-wire reference and every equality endpoint.

\begin{table}[ht]

\begin{tabular}{lrrrrr}
\toprule
Component & $M$ & $A$ & Total & Equations & Positive witnesses\\
\midrule
Power macro &31&39&70&15&25\\
Forward recoder &224&300&524&114&196\\
Reverse recoder &227&300&527&114&196\\
Pair with inlined outputs &454&601&1055&228&392\\

\caption{Free fixed numeral convention. Counts concern this initialization component and its stated ports. Equality assertions and expression aliases cost no arithmetic gate.}

\end{table}
Each recoder contains seven power calls, contributing $217M+273A=490$ operations, 175 witnesses and 105 equations. Its remaining forward part contributes $7M+27A$, 21 witnesses and nine equations. The reverse formula adds three multiplications. Pair assembly adds $3M+1A$. There are no coefficients or numbers of gates depending on a raw word length.

\subsection{Fixed-length family ledger}
For $e\ge0$, let
\[
 \lambda(e)=\begin{cases}0,&e=0,1,\\
 \lfloor\log_2e\rfloor+\pop(e)-1,&e\ge2.
 \end{cases}
\]
This is the count of the left-to-right binary multiplication chain used in the source. Exponent zero is the constant 1; exponent one is a wire alias. For $n=L+R$, set
\begin{align*}
 C_{\rm pow}={}&\lambda(v)+\lambda(n)+\lambda(n+1)+\lambda(R)\\
 &+\lambda(575450)+\lambda(23945)+\lambda(263945)
  +\lambda(264000)+\lambda(24000).
\end{align*}
The original fixed-length source has
\begin{align}
 M&=2(v-1)+583+3n+C_{\rm pow}+18,\label{ptr:ai:eq:fixedledger}\\
 A&=2(v-1)+583+4n+13,
\end{align}
with $n+8$ equations and $n+4$ positive witnesses. The two $2(v-1)$ terms are the two background Horner chains. The 583 terms evaluate $J$. Bit typing, binary sentinel reconstruction and the tape Horner chain contribute respectively $nM+2nA$, $nM+nA$ and $nM+nA$. No leading-zero coefficient gate is suppressed.

For empty words, the actual streamed source has $1152737M+1152594A=2305331$ operations, eight equations and four positive witnesses. The fixed outer wrapper replaces the length-specific $G^n,G^R,T$ by certified expression outputs and computes $G^{n+1}=AG$; its total is one operation larger, 2305332, with six equations. This component comparison is also checked by equality of off-solution residual polynomials after exact substitution.

\subsection{Literal constants from one and three}\label{ptr:ai:sec:strict}
The free fixed-numeral ledger does not claim that enormous coefficients appear at zero physical cost. Here is an exact alternative source prefix from literals 1 and 3. First construct $0=1-1$, $-1=0-1$, $2=1+1$. Construct $C_u=3^u$, $C_{\rm base}=3^{u-219}$, and $C_{198}=3^{198}$ by independent binary chains, and $C_x=C_u-1$.

For each of the $v$ coefficients $\alpha_j$, evaluate its prescribed $u$-digit ternary Horner string, beginning at the first fixed zero or one. This costs $u-1$ multiplications and $u-1$ additions even for zero digits. Each $\beta_j$ is an alias of 0 or 1. For each of the 584 coefficients of $J$, use its prescribed 221-symbol signed ternary string over $\{-1,0,1\}$, costing exactly 220 multiplications and 220 additions. The sign at each position is fixed by the anchor map, never existential.

The bridge-only prefix therefore costs
\begin{align*}
 M_{\rm const}&=v(u-1)+584\cdot220+\lambda(u)+\lambda(u-219)+\lambda(198),\\
 A_{\rm const}&=v(u-1)+584\cdot220+4,
\end{align*}
namely
\[
 \begin{aligned}
 M_{\rm const}&=277193130853952587,\\
 A_{\rm const}&=277193130853952484.
 \end{aligned}
\]
The recoder uses only literals $1,2,4$. The prefix already supplies 2; build $4=2+2$ once, adding one further addition. Hence the combined literal-1-and-3 prefix costs $554386261707905072$ operations, and prefix plus combined initialization costs
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:ai:eq:strictotal}
 554386261710211459.
\end{equation}
This enormous finite straight-line source is specified and counted, not expanded or run. The colour recipe is used to generate fixed source digits at compile time, never to answer a variable colour query during arithmetic evaluation. This strict ledger belongs to the initialization alone; it does not retrospectively pay for constants or adapters in any inherited component.

\subsection{Exact degrees and a paid single equation}\label{ptr:ai:sec:degree}
For this subsection, all independent ports and positive witnesses have degree one; fixed numerals have degree zero. Let $R_i$ be each equality's polynomial residual.

\begin{proposition}[Standalone recoder degree]
Before substituting $G=W^v$, every one of the six recoder interface variants has maximum residual degree 6, and its direct sum of squared residuals has exact degree 12. For the inlined pair this single polynomial uses $682M+1056A=1738$ operations and the same 392 positive witnesses.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Every power macro's growth-Pell residual expands as
\[
 a^2-1-\bigl((w+1)^2-1\bigr)(wg)^2
 =a^2-1-w^4g^2-2w^3g^2.
\]
Its unique top-degree term is $-w^4g^2$. All other macro residuals have degree at most four; the possible exponent argument $CP+2$ is quadratic and occurs linearly where it enters. Coefficient extraction, reverse residue extraction and pair assembly have degree at most three. Thus the maximum is exactly six. On squaring and summing, the degree-twelve part consists of fourteen distinct terms $w^8g^4$, each of coefficient one, for a pair. There can be no cancellation.

For $E$ equalities, form every residual with one subtraction, every square with one multiplication and the sum with $E-1$ additions. This adds $EM+(2E-1)A=3E-1$ operations and no witnesses. The inline pair has $E=228$, hence 683 additional operations. The separate-output and positive-output variants have $E=231$, hence 692 extra operations and totals 1747 and 1748 respectively, with their earlier port conventions unchanged.
\end{proof}
This degree-12 statement treats $G$ as an independent degree-one input. It does not survive substitution of the board radix without recalculation.

\begin{proposition}[Initializer degree and single polynomial]\label{ptr:ai:prop:initdegree}
For the 234 initializer residuals, with the inherited initializer ports still independent, the maximum total degree is $2v=1152000$. Their direct sum of squares
\[
 F=\sum_{i=1}^{234}R_i^2
\]
has exact degree $4v=2304000$, and $F=0$ is equivalent over integers to the original conjunction. Its fully streamed source uses $1153426M+1153662A=2307088$ operations, one equation, and the same 396 positive witnesses.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The exact radix expression $G=W^v$ has degree $v$. Two residuals, one in each call $\EXP(G,\text{length},\text{radixPower})$, have the form
\[
 2aG-(\Theta+G^2+1).
\]
Each has unique highest homogeneous term $-W^{2v}$. Every other residual has degree strictly less than $2v$. This is checked by propagating an upper degree along each literal gate: add degrees at multiplication and take the maximum at addition or subtraction. More explicitly, the final memory residual has upper degree 1127949, and all remaining residuals have smaller bounds. The memory bound follows from $\deg T=v+2$, the displayed fixed powers in~\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:Etape}--\eqref{ptr:ai:eq:memory}, and the degree-583 anchor polynomial. Fixed huge coefficients do not raise any of these degrees.

Consequently the top homogeneous term of $F$ is exactly $2W^{4v}$, proving the stated degree without relying on a cancellation-blind upper bound alone. A sum of integer squares vanishes exactly when each residual vanishes. With 234 residuals, the naive conversion adds $234M+467A=701$ operations. The emitted single-polynomial source retains the 234 equalities as expression pairs, asserts only $F=0$, and introduces no extra witnesses.
\end{proof}
The exact degree here is measured before replacing the inherited ports by any parent-history or endpoint expressions. Thus $F$ alone is not a universal halting polynomial. The literal-1-and-3 constant prefix changes neither its witness count nor its degree, but its enormous operation cost remains separately chargeable. No minimal-degree, sparse-optimal or operation-optimal claim is made.

\section{Dependencies and the remaining global composition}\label{ptr:ai:sec:scope}
The initialized board and head can be attached to the bounded-history relation by literal wire identifications. The normalized accepting event retained by the literal interface has residue and heading
\[
 (481225262775,29948,E)\pmod{(u,v)}.
\]
Both translations preserve those residues because the horizontal shift is $u$ and the vertical shift is $kv$. The separately frozen endpoint selector thus retains its meaning after this initialization. This report does not re-prove its arithmetic equations.

There are three distinct dependencies:
\begin{enumerate}
\item The pinned bounded-history theorem supplies the geometry and finite ant evolution. Its published local ledger is 174 operations, 61 positive unknowns, 48 equations and five positive parameters
\item Report 40 supplies the literal background, anchor, loader geometry and global finite-prefix facts, together with its exact U15 accepting interface
\item The pinned constructive Pell theorems supply the polynomial power macro's soundness and completeness
\end{enumerate}
Report 38~\cite{ptr:ai:report38} supplies the one-visit boundary context; it is not used to infer a new arithmetic initialization count. The arbitrary-program-to-U15 input compiler remains the separate published dependency identified in Report 40.

\begin{corollary}[Qualitative joined raw input relation]\label{ptr:ai:cor:joined}
Assume also the separately proved normalized endpoint selector. Existentially bind the parent history's five non-raw parameter ports
\[
 \IM,\quad\mathrm{FinalMemoryPlus},\quad\IH,
 \quad\mathrm{FinalHead},\quad\mathrm{FinalSignPlus}.
\]
Use the parent's existing $W,W_p,Q$ expressions in the initializer. The resulting one fixed positive Diophantine relation on $(\Raw_L,\Raw_R)$ holds exactly when the pinned U15 machine, started in state A scanning zero with those sentinel-decoded side words, reaches J1.
\end{corollary}

{\small\wtag\ Report~44 makes this relation numerical, with every port bound
and the equation combination paid: Theorem~\ref{ptr:pc:thm:complete}.\par}
\begin{proof}
For soundness, the parent relation gives a positive number of ordinary no-escape ant steps. The initializer identifies the exact translated restriction of the infinite literal board, so induction identifies its finite trajectory with the literal trajectory. The endpoint predicate has unchanged residues under translations by $u$ and $kv$. The literal accepting-interface theorem therefore gives U15 halting. Existentially choosing the final memory does not weaken this statement: the parent theorem makes it the actual final Boolean board.

Conversely, a U15 halt yields the accepting physical event after a positive finite number of ant steps. Choose $a,k$ large enough to contain its entire prefix. The initializer supplies its 396 positive witnesses, the parent converse supplies the history witnesses and its genuinely positive parameter ports, and the endpoint converse supplies its own positive witnesses. In particular, both memory ports are one greater than a nonnegative board word, both head ports are positive powers of three, and the white/east accepting endpoint has $\mathrm{FinalSignPlus}=1$. The literal interface excludes the predicate at initialization, so no zero-time history is required.
\end{proof}
This is a qualitative composition with every non-raw parent port bound. It does not supply an arbitrary-program-to-U15-word compiler or an uncharged numerical sum of all component ledgers.

A new global universal bound would require a new global ledger. In particular, the parent relation's original five external parameters, internal aliases $W,W_p,Q$, final memory and head/sign ports, endpoint selection, and equation combination must all be accounted for at one consistent boundary. Simply printing $174+2306387$ would not do this. Neither the headline theorem nor the archive asserts a minimized universal polynomial or a new record.

The initializer-only single polynomial in Section~\ref{ptr:ai:sec:degree} pays for its own equation combination. No corresponding combined history-and-endpoint source is counted here. Gate outputs remain expressions under all these ledgers; turning every gate into a separately quantified unknown would be a different witness convention.

\section{Verification and reproducibility}\label{ptr:ai:sec:verification}
The accompanying archive contains the full article source, the own arithmetic builders and finite checkers, compact read-only dependency snapshots, reference receipts, a deterministic PDF builder, and an authenticated release inventory. It deliberately does not duplicate the complete large finite-template packet of Report 40 or execute that packet. The colour recipe is included through its compact read closure: unchanged generator and decoder source, unchanged small program data, and exact colour-map projections of the larger template files. The asset manifest records both original and projection hashes. No coordinate or colour is changed; proof histories and unused metadata are omitted. The complete global ant simulation remains a pinned theorem dependency, not something re-certified by replaying an old schedule.

\subsection{What the arithmetic checks establish}
The periodic bridge checker compares exact geometric repetition with dense small periodic boards; compares the compressed patch polynomial against independently assembled literal coordinates; checks sentinel recovery, rejection boundaries, signed off-solution residuals, and structural gate counts; and streams all gates of the full empty-word family and outer wrapper. The raw recoder checker tests 1016 word/radix residue cases, 38 full binomial-extraction cases, 5766 pair-orientation cases and two tiny explicitly constructed Pell examples. Most residue tests use smaller power-of-two radices meeting the proved margins rather than materializing the deterministic enormous $B$.

The all-length conjunction and its single-polynomial conversion are streamed as actual source DAGs, not inferred only by adding printed counts. An exact sparse-polynomial expansion independently checks all six standalone recoder degree and sum-of-squares claims; for the much larger initializer, full-stream degree propagation plus its two exact leading monomials establishes the degree claim. Separate independent reviews compare the equations, source pins, domains and theorem specialization. These checks are evidence of implementation agreement with the mathematical construction. They do not replace the universal proofs, instantiate the giant parent history witnesses, simulate the giant physical ant controller, expand the strict constant prefix, or formally verify the Lean source in a proof assistant.

with the prefix \texttt{03-ant-init-} (Markdown at the report root, Python in

parent history note (\path{HISTORY174.md}, byte-identical to
\path{Computability/HilbertTenthProblem/Papers/1980/EXPLORATION_ANT_CHECKERBOARD_HISTORY.md},
unchanged since the pin), the Mathlib file and licence (note in
Section~\ref{ptr:ai:sec:pell}), and the files that duplicate Part~II's
(shipped once, under \texttt{02-literal-ant-}). The colour-map projections of

are not copies. The delivered verifier refuses NTFS file modes; the report
README gives a rerun route on a copy. At placement the whole replay passed on
a copy, including the byte-identical regeneration of the dilation evidence.\par}

\subsection{Replay discipline}
An external standard-library inventory check authenticates the release before its own verifier is invoked. The verifier again authenticates the complete inventory before executing any scientific script. Scientific replay takes place in new isolated copies; source bytes, modes and timestamps are checked for preservation. Normal Python replay must reproduce the reference scientific outputs. The recoder checker uses explicit guarded exceptions and also passes optimized Python with all checks active. The six assertion-dependent bridge, splice, degree and folding programs explicitly reject optimized Python before scientific work or outputs; those invocations are checked for that rejection rather than counted as scientific passes. All outputs, logs and TeX caches belong in external QA directories, outside the release tree.

The PDF builder uses three no-shell-escape pdfLaTeX passes with a fixed source epoch. Rebuilding in a fresh external directory must reproduce the packaged PDF byte for byte on the recorded TeX toolchain. The ZIP builder uses a fixed member order, timestamps and modes; its external SHA256 authenticates the archive. The inventory hash itself must be obtained from the enclosing delivery or another trusted channel: a self-consistent replacement of both payload and its checksum is not prevented by an unsigned manifest.

\subsection{Scope of the completed result}
The fixed all-length raw initialization obligation has been closed at the stated component boundary. The resulting bound is intentionally literal and unoptimized. Practical evaluation is not promised, source constants are not materialized, full witness uniqueness is false, and complete universal-certificate bookkeeping remains separate. These distinctions preserve the useful conclusion: ordinary binary sentinel inputs can now be connected to this exact fixed periodic ant initialization by one paid finite polynomial system.

\section{Concrete further research}\label{ptr:ai:sec:future}
The largest present cost is the pair of 576000-row background Horner chains. The literal colour generator has considerable repeated geometry, so a useful next target is a smaller fixed arithmetic description of its coefficient polynomial, with every compression operation and any extra witness charged. A recipe alone does not constitute such a compression.

A second target is a completely merged history, initialization and endpoint source with one consistent port convention, a total positive-witness ledger, fixed-numeral accounting and a paid final equation-combination step. The qualitative corollary already identifies the intended projection, but a numerical bound needs that additional source work.

The raw recoder offers a third concrete optimization problem: reduce its fourteen power calls, witness burden or residue machinery while retaining both orientations, zero padding and all converse bounds. Any such improvement needs its own exact source and independent review; no easy route to single-fold exponentiation is assumed. Finally, formalizing the newly proved recoder and splice in a proof assistant would narrow the gap between source-level arithmetic audits and a machine-checked end-to-end theorem.

\section*{Report 44: the complete positive certificate}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Report 44: the complete positive certificate}
\noindent\wtag\ \emph{A Complete Positive Certificate for the Literal Periodic
Ant. A composition addendum to Research Reports 40 and 42}, Research
Report~44, recovered edition, dated 4 October 2026, author line ``Mathematical
research report'' (its README title: ``Research Report 44: complete positive
literal-ant certificate, recovered edition''); batch-88 manuscript~07, archive
\path{Complete_Positive_Certificate_for_the_Literal_Periodic_Ant_Package.zip}.
Pins: ProveIt \texttt{5883b08b7} (the history note and its base-three and Pell
dependencies), Mathlib \texttt{ac77769f}, the Neary--Woods PDF and the
endpoint source (Appendix~\ref{ptr:pc:app:pins}). Numbered 44.$k$; labels
\texttt{ptr:pc:}; files \texttt{04-ant-cert-}. Its edition note, after the
abstract, is the manuscript's own.

\begin{quote}\small\noindent\textbf{Abstract (Report 44).} We close the arithmetic composition left open in Research Report 42. One fixed integer polynomial recognizes the exact raw-pair halting language of the pinned Neary--Woods U15 machine through the initialized literal periodic ant. Its source quantifies all five formerly external positive history parameters, retains a reconstructed 174-operation bounded-history component, includes the 2306387-operation initializer and 42-operation endpoint, and pays for the final sum of squares. The result has 465 strictly positive witnesses, 2307457 operations, and exact total degree 2304000. A separately paid positive Cantor pairing gives one ordinary positive input, 467 witnesses, and 2307467 operations. The leading homogeneous part is exactly $2W^{2304000}$. We specify the complete literal source, domains, port identifications, coefficients and reproducible checks. The unexpanded literal-1-and-3 variant costs 554386261710212588 operations for two inputs, ten more for one. These conservative counts are not operation records. The positive-history, base-three/Pell, recoder and literal-interface theorems remain explicit dependencies; finite checks do not replace them. No empty-right universality, physical ant cessation or single-fold representation is asserted.
\end{quote}
\paragraph{Edition note.} The pre-delivery filesystem reset lost the previously verified Report 44 files. This edition reconstructs the manuscript and package from retained work and authenticated earlier reports. It has a new release identity and fresh checks; an earlier PASS receipt is historical evidence, not authentication of reconstructed bytes. The corrected geometric-repunit identity for $h_x$ is retained.

\section{The exact relation and theorem}\label{ptr:pc:sec:result}
For a nearest-head-first finite binary word $d=(d_0,\ldots,d_{n-1})$, put
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:pc:eq:sentinel}
 \operatorname{sent}(d)=2^n+\sum_{j=0}^{n-1}d_j2^{n-1-j}.
\end{equation}
Every positive integer has a unique decoding. The empty word has code 1; explicit zero padding is retained. For $x,y\in\Zp$, let $H(x,y)$ mean that the pinned U15 machine starts in state A scanning blank 0, with the words decoded from $x,y$ on its left and right respectively, blank elsewhere, and eventually reaches the undefined pair J1. Both side words are nearest-head-first. The table's J0 instruction is defined; we use that table rather than the contradictory closing sentence of the original simulation discussion~\cite{ptr:pc:nw}.

{\small\wtag\ The ``contradictory closing sentence'' is the last sentence of
Section~3.5 of~\cite{ptr:pc:nw}, which names $c$ (here 0) in the halting
transition, whereas Table~16 leaves $(u_{10},b)$, here J1, undefined. Report~40
and the programme's review make the same observation (note in
Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:ca}).\par}

\needspace{12\baselineskip}
\begin{theorem}[Complete fixed polynomial]\label{ptr:pc:thm:complete}
Subject to the inherited theorems identified in Section~\ref{ptr:pc:sec:dependencies}, the accompanying literal source defines a fixed polynomial
\[
 F_2\in\Z[x,y,z_1,\ldots,z_{465}]
\]
such that, for every $x,y>0$,
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:pc:eq:iff}
 H(x,y)\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad
 \exists z_1,\ldots,z_{465}\in\Zp\quad F_2(x,y,z)=0.
\end{equation}
Its displayed arithmetic source has one asserted equation, exactly 1153586 multiplications and 1153871 additions/subtractions, hence 2307457 operations. The polynomial has exact total degree 2304000. There are no external geometry, board, final-memory, head or sign parameters.

There is also a fixed polynomial $F_1\in\Z[N,z_1,\ldots,z_{467}]$ whose displayed source has 2307467 operations and whose exact degree is the same. For every $N>0$, it has a positive zero exactly when $H(x,y)$ holds for the unique positive pair satisfying
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:pc:eq:pair}
 2N=(x+y-2)(x+y-1)+2y.
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
An operation is a binary addition, subtraction or multiplication of available expressions. Fixed integer coefficients have degree zero; multiplication by one is still charged. Gate values are expressions, not new quantified coordinates. Storing an equality pair costs no arithmetic, but forming its residual and the final sum of squares is paid. The theorem recognizes an accepting ant observable representing U15 halting. The physical ant continues moving after that event.

\paragraph{\wtag\ What the theorem recognizes.} The predicate $H$ is the
halting language of one fixed machine on sentinel-pair inputs. It is
recursively enumerable and complete only through the published Neary--Woods
encoding of arbitrary programs as U15 tapes, which no Part implements. In the
words of the programme's review~\cite{ptr:w:rev4048}, the certificate is ``an
ordinary-integer Diophantine recognizer of a fixed recursively enumerable
complete language'', ``not, by itself, an emitted per-program map from the
research tree's ordinary input variable into the published U15 tape
encoding''. It is therefore not an ordinary-input loader for the programme's
input convention, and it is not called a universal polynomial here except
with this qualification.

The new contribution is the complete source-level join. It does not repeat Report 42's long recoder proof~\cite{ptr:pc:report42}, replace Report 40's literal simulation theorem~\cite{ptr:pc:report40}, or establish a new qualitative turmite universality theorem. The arbitrary-program-to-U15 reduction remains the published dependency~\cite{ptr:pc:nw}.

\section{Inherited contracts and proof boundary}\label{ptr:pc:sec:dependencies}
\subsection{Positive bounded histories}
The reconstructed history has 174 explicit operations, 48 equations, five positive parameters and 61 positive witnesses. For arbitrary positive parameters, the equations are solvable exactly when there exist odd $w\ge3$, even $h\ge2$ and $t\ge1$ describing an ordinary no-escape ant history on a $w$-by-$h$ rectangle. On solutions,
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:pc:eq:geometry}
 W=3^w,\qquad W_p=W/3,\qquad Q=W^h,\qquad q=Q^t.
\end{equation}
The two memory parameters minus one are the exact initial/final ternary Boolean board words. Sites flatten as $x+wy$, east and south positive. The initial head is $3^j$ at an even index $j$ in the rectangle, with pre-departure heading north. The final head is its actual last head. The value $\FS-1$ is zero or $\FH$ and encodes incoming heading: at an even-index black site zero/one means north/south; at a white site it means east/west. Booleanity, power, range and actual endpoint status are conclusions, not external promises.

The all-integer theorem is inherited from the pinned history and base-three/Pell sources~\cite{ptr:pc:history}. The new reconstruction compares the actual DAG with separately stated residual polynomials. At zero-based residual 46,
\[
 R_{46}=S_{46}+S_{45}\bigl((2r+1+jc)^2-y_{\rm aux}^2\bigr),
 \qquad R_{45}=S_{45}.
\]
This checked unit-triangular replacement preserves the simultaneous zero set over the integers and does not alter witness domains. It is not silently identified with literal equality of the unmodified residuals.

{\small\wtag\ The programme's note states residual 46 literally; this
reconstruction replaces it by a unit-triangular combination with the same
zero set, and says so. The programme's review checked the 174-row DAG's
closure, count and degree, not the history theorem~\cite{ptr:w:rev4048}.\par}

\subsection{No circular power or positivity premise}
The inherited proof first uses positive geometry quotients and strict slacks to bound the packed fields, before interpreting digits. The fields are nonnegative; fifteen Horner steps pack sixteen fields with $0\le P<q^{16}$. The base-three kernel is applied to kernel input $q^4$, whose fourth-power bound is $q^{16}$. Four squarings construct the latter, and $D_0=9q^{16}$, $r=D_0-3P-1$ are charged expressions.

The kernel derives the power-of-three property. Unique factorization and divisibility/repunit equations then give~\eqref{ptr:pc:eq:geometry}. Its positive converse requires even packed $P$: the local parity identity, including the repeated terminal D field, establishes that condition before invoking the Pell construction. Negative intermediate Pell values are permitted. There is no freely imposed upper bound on Pell intermediates.

The precise dependencies are standard integer Pell classification, recurrence and divisibility; the pinned relaxed auxiliary Pell proof; the half-parameter and signed step-down arguments with their required bounds; the base-three kernel's own ratio, congruence and rounding lemmas; and the ternary carry criterion from Kummer's theorem. Their proof sources and exact scope are recorded in \codeu{history/CONTRACT.md}. The recoder separately uses the constructive \codeu{Pell.matiyasevic} and \codeu{Pell.eq_pow_of_pell} specialization~\cite{ptr:pc:pell}. These remain inherited mathematics, not newly mechanized proofs.

\subsection{Literal ant and initialization}
Report 40 supplies the periodic color recipe, 2806-cell startup anchor, literal two-sided loader and accepting-port equivalence. Report 42 supplies the paid all-length recoder, exact board formula and finite-prefix padding lemma. The initializer's normalized local arithmetic/equation hash is the frozen Report 42 hash; its scientific equations are unchanged by this composition.

\section{Initialized board and endpoint}\label{ptr:pc:sec:splice}
Set
\[
 u=481238074400,\quad v=576000,\quad
 x_0=481225262775,\quad y_0=29948.
\]
Physical coordinates $(a,b)$ increase east and south. The frozen start is $(288650,75,E)$. Normalize and translate by
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:pc:eq:rotation}
 X=b-75,\quad Y=288650-a,\qquad x=X+u,\quad y=Y+kv.
\end{equation}
The rotation preserves orientation and sends the initial east heading to north. Permitted rectangles are
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:pc:eq:rect}
 w=1+au\quad(a\ge2),\qquad h=2kv\quad(k\ge L+R+1),
\end{equation}
where $L,R$ are the unique decoded word lengths. Since $u$ is even, $w$ is odd. The initial head is $3^uW^{kv}$ with even flattened index. The padding theorem covers both vertical extremes and uses the literal interface's global lower horizontal-support bound.

The recoder takes $G=W^v$ and provides
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:pc:eq:recoder}
 A=G^{L+R},\quad B=G^R,\quad
 T=G^{R+1}\sum_{j<L}\ell_jG^j+\sum_{j<R}r_jG^{R-1-j}.
\end{equation}
Only after history establishes $W=3^w$ is the recoder premise $G\ge2$ used. Its forward/reverse outputs preserve orientation and zero padding. Six remaining initializer equations enforce~\eqref{ptr:pc:eq:rect}, the head and the periodic background plus anchor and input patch. This is an actual board equality, not a promise that the raw input already represents a suitable board. Tight padding permits \codeu{PaddingPower}=1.

In the compact outer formula the positive geometry witnesses are $X_g,H,K,P$. It computes $h_x=X_g+1$ and imposes
\[
 W_p-1=(3^u-1)h_x,\quad H-1=(G-1)K,\quad Q=H^2,
 \quad H=PAG,\quad \IH=3^uH.
\]
The sixth equation equates $\IM$ with one plus the exact background-and-patch polynomial. All literal Horner and patch expressions are retained; no coefficient oracle is free. On solutions $H=W^{kv}$ and
\[
 h_x=\frac{3^{au}-1}{3^u-1}=1+3^u+\cdots+3^{(a-1)u}.
\]
Thus $h_x$ is the geometric repunit for $a$ repeats, not their count. Also $K=(G^k-1)/(G-1)$ and $P=G^{k-L-R-1}$. The source's background factor $K(H+1)=(Q-1)/(G-1)$ repeats the $v$-row tile $2k$ times. The recoder contributes 228 equations and the outer formula six, totaling 234.

The folded endpoint~\cite{ptr:pc:endpoint} uses prescribed constants

{\small\wtag\ This is the folded form of Report~40's endpoint selector,
Theorem~\ref{ptr:la:thm:endpoint} in Appendix~\ref{ptr:la:app:endpoint}
(Part~II), with $H_x^+,H_y^+,B_c,K_e,V_e,A_c$ for that appendix's
$X_+,Y_+,B,K,V_*,P$.\par}
\[
 C=3^u-1,\qquad D=C-1,\qquad K_e=3^{x_0}
\]
and positive witnesses $H_x^+,H_y^+,B_c$. Its expressions and equations are
\begin{gather}
 U=CH_x^+-D=C(H_x^+-1)+1\ge1,\notag\\
 V_e=1+(W^v-1)(H_y^+-1)\ge1,\label{ptr:pc:eq:positive-end}\\
 A_c=K_eU,\quad A_c+B_c=W,\quad
 \FH=(A_cW^{y_0})V_e,\quad\FS=1.\label{ptr:pc:eq:end}
\end{gather}
The strict column bound prevents a carry into the row. With the history's actual final-head power, the endpoint theorem selects exactly residues $(x_0,y_0)\bmod(u,v)$ and white/east heading. The zero quotients are retained by $H_x^+=1$ or $H_y^+=1$. Translation~\eqref{ptr:pc:eq:rotation} preserves these residues.

\section{Proof of the complete equivalence}
\begin{proof}[Soundness of Theorem~\ref{ptr:pc:thm:complete}]
A zero of the final polynomial makes every integer square in the sum of squares zero. All three component systems therefore hold at the same positive coordinates. Apply history first: it gives a real positive-length trajectory, powers~\eqref{ptr:pc:eq:geometry}, a north-facing even-index initial head, exact initial/final memories, and no escape.

Now $G=W^v\ge2$, so the recoder identifies the unique words and outputs~\eqref{ptr:pc:eq:recoder}. Initialization identifies the exact translated restriction of the infinite literal ant board, including anchor and finite input patch. Induction identifies the no-escape trajectory with the same infinite literal trajectory prefix: cell color and heading agree, and the turn/write/move rule uniquely fixes each step.

Equations~\eqref{ptr:pc:eq:positive-end}--\eqref{ptr:pc:eq:end}, with history's actual final head and sign, give the accepting residue and orientation, unchanged by translation by $(u,kv)$. The literal-interface theorem yields U15's J1 event. An existential final memory is not a loophole: history already forces it to be the actual final Boolean board. Thus $\exists z>0\ F_2(x,y,z)=0\Rightarrow H(x,y)$.
\end{proof}
\begin{proof}[Completeness]
Suppose the decoded U15 input reaches J1. The literal-interface theorem supplies an accepting event after a positive finite number of ant steps and excludes it at initialization. Its finite prefix obeys the global lower horizontal-support bound. Choose $a\ge2$ and $k\ge L+R+1$ large enough to contain the entire translated prefix in~\eqref{ptr:pc:eq:rect}. This is Report 42's two-sided padding statement, not an extra containment assumption.

Initialization supplies its 392 recoder and four positive geometry coordinates. Take $\IM,\FM$ to be one plus the actual nonnegative initial/final Boolean board words, the two head parameters to be their positive powers of three, and $\FS=1$ at the accepting endpoint. These five parameters are positive. The all-positive history converse supplies 61 witnesses, including the Pell tuple and its required packed-word parity. The endpoint converse supplies three positive witnesses, including quotient-zero cases.

Every shared port has the same numerical value in all components. Thus the 285 equations hold simultaneously without a hidden compatibility variable, raw coordinate or uncounted nonnegative adapter. Their paid sum of squares vanishes. Section~\ref{ptr:pc:sec:pairing} gives the one-input assertion.
\end{proof}

\section{Literal arithmetic source and complete ledger}\label{ptr:pc:sec:source}
The source is a topological stream. External inputs are declared; each positive witness is declared before use. Arithmetic records have an integer output ID, an opcode in $\{+,-,*\}$ and two operands. Integer operands are earlier gate IDs. Strings are positive-coordinate names or prescribed \codeu{C:} coefficient labels. Fixed integers in component JSON are translated to coefficient labels before insertion, avoiding confusion with gate IDs.

Residual pairs are metadata, not additional assertions. For every stored pair $(a_i,b_i)$, the final source computes
\[
 r_i=a_i-b_i,\qquad s_i=r_i r_i,\qquad F_2=\sum_{i=0}^{284}s_i,
\]
and asserts only $F_2=0$. Every subtraction is paid, even if a simplifier could remove it; each square costs one multiplication and summing the 285 squares costs 284 additions.
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrrrr@{}}\toprule
Source section&M&A&Total&Pairs&New witnesses\\\midrule
History&72&102&174&48&61\\
Initialization&1153192&1153195&2306387&234&396\\
Endpoint&37&5&42&3&3\\\midrule
Conjunction&1153301&1153302&2306603&285&460\\
Final SOS&285&569&854&---&0\\\midrule
Complete $F_2$&1153586&1153871&2307457&1 asserted&465\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}\end{center}
The final witness count includes five parent coordinates in addition to the 460 new component witnesses. The zero-based half-open gate intervals are $[0,174)$ for history, $[174,2306561)$ for initialization, $[2306561,2306603)$ for endpoint, and $[2306603,2307457)$ for SOS. Gate 2307456 is the final output. These are streamed intervals, not simply sums of printed component totals.

The initializer's normalized local gate/equation SHA256 is
\begin{center}\small\codeu{503215b20d317cfb9e6e40ec82eb26044afbdedf26f37e03029bcd7dc0a04130}.\end{center}
It reproduces the frozen Report 42 component. Complete stream hashes include ordered declarations, gates, stored residual pairs and the sole final equation. They differ from the implementing Python file hash. An optional emitter writes every ordinary free-coefficient gate as JSON lines; default replay generates, validates, counts, hashes and degree-checks the same gates without saving the large stream.

\section{Exact total degree}\label{ptr:pc:sec:degree}
Degree means total degree in raw inputs and positive quantified coordinates, with prescribed coefficients degree zero. In particular \codeu{History:W} remains an independent degree-one variable; it is not replaced by a power expression.

Literal propagation gives history residual degree at most 104 and endpoint degree at most $v+y_0+2=605950$. The pairing residual has degree two. Exactly two initializer residuals attain degree bound $2v=1152000$: local zero-based indices 16 and 130, globally 64 and 178 after the 48 history pairs. Every other residual has bound at most 1127949.
\begin{proposition}
The leading homogeneous part of each final polynomial is exactly
\[
 2W^{4v}=2W^{2304000}.
\]
Both therefore have exact total degree 2304000.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The maximal residuals are the first left/right recoder power-modulus equations. Each has form
\[
 2aG-(\Theta+G^2+1),\qquad G=W^v,
\]
where $a$ is a positive witness plus one and $\Theta$ an independent positive witness. The $aG$ term has degree $v+1<2v$, so the unique top homogeneous term is $-W^{2v}$. Each square contributes $W^{4v}$; no other squared residual can reach that degree. Their sum has the nonzero leading coefficient 2.
\end{proof}
This is a surviving-term proof of exactness, not only cancellation-blind propagation. Sharing elsewhere cannot remove those terms. The coefficient-only prefix and degree-two pairing cannot change the leader. No minimum-degree claim follows.

\section{One ordinary input, with pairing paid}\label{ptr:pc:sec:pairing}
Let $n=x+y-2\ge0$. Equation~\eqref{ptr:pc:eq:pair} is equivalent to
\[
 N=\frac{n(n+1)}2+y,\qquad 1\le y\le n+1.
\]
For each $n$ it covers exactly the interval
\[
 \frac{n(n+1)}2+1\le N\le\frac{(n+1)(n+2)}2.
\]
These intervals are disjoint, consecutive and exhaust $\Zp$. The interval containing $N$ determines $n$, then $y$, then $x=n+2-y\ge1$. This proves a bijection without a square root, floor, division gate or pairing oracle.

The source internalizes \codeu{RawLeft}, \codeu{RawRight} as positive witnesses, leaving only \codeu{RawInput} external. Its seven gates compute
\[
 s=x+y,\ s_1=s-1,\ s_2=s_1-1,\ p=s_2s_1,\ y_2=2y,\ b=p+y_2,\ a=2N
\]
and store pair $(a,b)$. This is $3M+4A$, one residual and two additional witnesses. The enlarged 286-residual SOS costs $286M+571A$, an extra $1M+2A$. Hence the entire arity reduction costs $4M+6A=10$ operations. The totals are
\[
 1153590M+1153877A=2307467,\qquad467\text{ positive witnesses}.
\]

\subsection{Why an empty-right shortcut is unsupported}
Neary--Woods' actual initialization puts the dataword to the right of the scanned symbol. Their tape has form
\[
 \cdots cc\,\langle B\rangle S^k b[c]D_B(w)\,cc\cdots,
\]
where brackets mark the scanned cell, initial state is $u_1$, and the U15 guard is $G=bc$; see Definition 3.1, equations (3)--(4), Table 1 and Section 3.5/Table 16~\cite{ptr:pc:nw}. Renaming $c\mapsto0$, $b\mapsto1$, $u_1\mapsto A$ yields
\[
 \ell=\operatorname{reverse}(\beta(\langle B\rangle S^k b)),\qquad r=\beta(D_B(w)).
\]
The data encodings begin $cb$, so primary right words begin 01. Their second cell right of the head is 1, whereas an empty-right tape is blank there. The rightmost symbol of $G$ is not the rightmost symbol of the whole finite input.

Therefore \codeu{RawRight}=1 does not establish a universal restricted family. We do not assert that family is non-universal; the required argument is absent here. Fixing a universal program permits a fixed-left, varying-right inference, but a numerical certificate would still need its literal fixed data and an explicit paid construction. The pairing avoids both unsupported shortcuts. \codeu{one-input/AUDIT.md} records the exact orientation and the J1/table-versus-prose issue.

\section{Every coefficient from literals 1 and 3}\label{ptr:pc:sec:strict}
The economical ledger admits prescribed fixed integer coefficients. A separate deliberately unoptimized ledger constructs all coefficient labels by one finite topological prefix whose only initial literals are 1 and 3. Its enormous expansion is specified, never materialized or executed. Fixed loop endpoints are source-generation parameters, not run-time arithmetic inputs.

For $e\ge2$, define $\lambda(e)=\lfloor\log_2e\rfloor+\operatorname{popcount}(e)-1$, with $\lambda(0)=\lambda(1)=0$. A power $3^e$ uses the left-to-right binary multiplication chain: one square per remaining digit, one multiplication by 3 per remaining one bit. The full grammar is:
\begin{enumerate}
\item Compute $0=1-1$, $-1=0-1$, $2=1+1$ (three additions/subtractions).
\item Independently construct $3^u$, $3^{u-219}$ and $3^{198}$ by binary chains, then $C=3^u-1$.
\item For $0\le j<v$ prescribe $b(i,j)=c(288650-j,i+75)$, $0\le i<u$, from the pinned total literal color recipe. Construct each row by ternary Horner evaluation of $b(u-1,j),\ldots,b(0,j)$, retaining every multiplication and addition, including zero bits. Each row costs $(u-1)M+(u-1)A$. The first-column coefficient aliases $b(0,j)$, hence 0 or 1.
\item For $0\le j\le583$, prescribe signed anchor corrections $\delta(i,j)\in\{-1,0,1\}$ at physical $(289200-j,i-144)$ for $0\le i\le220$. Absent cells have zero correction. Every 221-symbol Horner chain costs $220M+220A$; signed symbols alias available $-1,0,1$.
\item Compute $4=2+2$, $6=3+3$, $8=4+4$, $9=3\cdot3$. Independently construct $K_e=3^{x_0}$ and then $D=C-1$.
\end{enumerate}
The bits are fixed data, not existential coefficients or a variable-dependent color oracle. The compact recipe read closure is hash-pinned under \codeu{dependencies/recipe_assets} and never executed by replay. The 2806-cell anchor remains fixed data. Different coefficient labels may denote equal integers; their number is not a count of distinct numerical values.

Steps 1--4 are the Report 42 prefix:
\begin{align*}
 M_0&=v(u-1)+584\cdot220+\lambda(u)+\lambda(u-219)+\lambda(198)\\
 &=277193130853952587,\\
 A_0&=v(u-1)+584\cdot220+4=277193130853952484.
\end{align*}
Step 5 adds 56 multiplications and four additions/subtractions. Thus
\begin{align*}
 M_{\rm prefix}&=277193130853952643,\\
 A_{\rm prefix}&=277193130853952488,\\
 C_{\rm prefix}&=554386261707905131.
\end{align*}
The history literal 6 and final equation's zero are paid. Initializer and endpoint share $C$ rather than constructing it twice. Main-source multiplications by these constants remain charged. Complete strict totals are
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lr@{}}\toprule
Two-input $F_2$&554386261710212588\\
One-input $F_1$&554386261710212598\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}\end{center}
The prefix adds no witnesses, equations or variable degree. These totals describe this exact finite grammar, not a practical expansion or optimized literal-source bound.

\section{Verification, recovery and limits}\label{ptr:pc:sec:verification}
The reconstructed packet carries the exact arithmetic source, small component specifications, positive-name receipts, dependency contracts, fixed coefficient read closure and fresh verification evidence. Authenticated Reports 40 and 42 remain unchanged. Upstream Python/Lean, physical ant programs, recipe code and saved upstream schedules are read-only provenance; only inspected newly authored reconstruction/audit programs and normal build/archive utilities are executed.

History checks compare all 48 residuals with separately stated integer polynomials, validate each operand and declaration, check the triangular correction and exact residual degree 104. A fresh finite regression examines 768 combinations of Boolean $3$-by-$2$ boards, even starts and one through four requested steps. Exactly 264 prefixes remain on the board. It checks 38 outer-plus-mask equations, positive outer witnesses, Boolean/even packing, strict bounds and the exact ternary central-binomial valuation. Ten mutations are rejected. It does not instantiate an enormous Pell tuple or re-prove the arbitrary-integer theorem.

Complete replay checks every generated gate, declarations, all 285/286 residual pairs, the sole final assertion, hashes, operation counts and degree bounds. Fresh source reviews and the independent checks recorded in the recovered packet are limited to their stated evidence. In particular a recovered old PASS record does not certify a newly generated program merely because its counts agree. Exact stream identity, when established by a matching full-stream hash, is reported separately from implementation-file identity. Implementation, manuscript, PDF and archive hashes are freshly recorded.

\subsection{Authenticated and isolated reproduction}
The delivery supplies the new \codeu{MANIFEST.json} SHA256. Before executing bundled code, a standard-library bootstrap must compare that trusted value, authenticate every entry and reject extra files/directories, symlinks, unsafe paths and duplicate keys. The bundled verifier repeats this authentication. A self-consistent replacement of payload and manifest is not prevented by an unsigned manifest without an external trusted hash.

Scientific replay occurs in fresh external copies. The complete generator requires fresh outputs outside its resolved source tree and validates both receipt/stream destinations before opening either. Duplicate destinations, symlinked parents, existing files and dangling symlinks are checked. Original release bytes, modes and timestamps are checked for preservation.

The complete generator and packet verifier reject optimized Python before scientific work because they contain assertions; the boundary checker imports that same guarded generator. These early rejections are guard tests, not optimized scientific passes. History reconstruction and finite-history checking use explicit exceptions and reproduce their reference outputs under both normal Python and \codeu{-O}. Main replay uses Python 3.11+ and standard library; exact history checks also need SymPy. No inherited source or recipe directory is executed.

The PDF uses three external no-shell-escape pdfLaTeX passes, fixed UTC source epoch and suppressed dates/trailer ID. Fresh builds must agree byte for byte on the recorded toolchain. Every final page is rendered and visually reviewed. ZIP member order, timestamp, mode and compression settings are fixed; fresh archive builds must agree. All logs and caches stay outside the sealed release. The README gives exact commands and the final binding identifies the freshly checked recovered bytes.

\subsection{Scope of the completed result}
This is one fixed positive-witness Diophantine certificate for the specified raw U15/initialized-ant interface, with aggregation paid. It is not a universal operation record, efficient decision procedure, physical ant cessation, witness uniqueness or a finite-fold theorem. Report 42's Pell fibers need not be unique. The enormous coefficient prefix is unexpanded. End-to-end formalization and reductions in background Horner cost, witness burden or endpoint sharing would require separate proofs, exact sources and audits.
