\begin{quote}\small\noindent\textbf{Abstract (Report 47).} We replace the dense, deliberately unoptimized fixed-coefficient prefix of Research Report 44 by an exact structured arithmetic construction. All 1,152,598 coefficient labels retain their prescribed integer values. The new prefix has only literal 1 and literal 3 leaves and exactly 29,081,374 paid additions, subtractions and multiplications. Its construction combines row-occurrence polynomials compiled by signed events, finite black-set ownership, division-free geometric sums, and the exact rotated periodic cut. Appending the unchanged variable-dependent source gives 31,388,831 operations for two positive raw inputs or 31,388,841 for one, with the same 465 or 467 positive witnesses, one equation and exact variable degree 2,304,000. \section{The result and its scope}\label{ptr:sc:sec:result}
Report 44 fixes a polynomial source for the raw-pair halting relation of the pinned Neary--Woods U15 machine and its literal periodic-ant realization~\cite{ptr:sc:report44}. Its variable-dependent source has 2,307,457 arithmetic gates. The separate literal-$1/3$ prefix in its strict grammar constructs all fixed coefficients by ternary Horner chains and costs
\[
 N_{\rm old}=554\,386\,261\,707\,905\,131.
\]
That count is a valid count of the displayed unoptimized grammar. This report supplies a different prefix for the same coefficients; it does not correct an erroneous old count.

An arithmetic gate is a binary $+$, $-$ or $\times$ of available expressions. We write $M$ for multiplication count and $A$ for addition/subtraction count. The word ``addition'' in a ledger includes subtraction. Initial constant leaves are exactly 1 and 3. All loop endpoints, template coordinates and fixed JSON bits are compilation data, not free run-time integer leaves. A gate output is an expression, not a quantified witness. Aliasing an available expression costs no gate; computing that expression and every later multiplication by it is charged. This is a unit-cost arithmetic-source model. Operands may have enormous bit length; generating or hashing the source does not evaluate those full integers or imply a comparable numerical running time or memory bound.

\begin{theorem}[Exact coefficient refinement]\label{ptr:sc:thm:prefix}
There is an explicit finite topological arithmetic prefix $P$, using only the constant leaves $1,3$, with
\[
 M_P=13\,182\,377,\qquad A_P=15\,898\,997,\qquad N_P=29\,081\,374,
\]
and a total alias map to every one of Report 44's 1,152,598 coefficient labels. Each alias has exactly the prescribed old integer value. The prefix introduces no variable, witness or equation.

Binding the old coefficient operands to these aliases and retaining every old main gate gives the following strict sources:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrr@{}}\toprule
Raw positive inputs & $M$ & $A$ & Total\\\midrule
Two &14,335,963&17,052,868&31,388,831\\
One &14,335,967&17,052,874&31,388,841\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}\end{center}
The resulting polynomials are pointwise identical to the old polynomials on all integer assignments. Their positive witness counts are respectively 465 and 467; each has one asserted equation and exact total variable degree 2,304,000.
\end{theorem}
The prefix claim is proved in Sections~\ref{ptr:sc:sec:occurrence}--\ref{ptr:sc:sec:labels}. The splice and degree claim is proved in Section~\ref{ptr:sc:sec:splice}. Counts describe these particular sources, including gates whose values happen to be zero or one. No lower bound or optimality theorem is implicit.

\wtag\ \emph{The recognition statement.} At this point Report~47 recalls,
``for completeness'', the inherited recognition theorem: with the sentinel
decoding $\operatorname{sent}(d)$ of nearest-head-first binary words and
$H(x,y)$ the halting of the pinned U15 machine on the decoded side words,
$H(x,y)\iff\exists z\in\Zp^{465}\ F_2(x,y,z)=0$ for every positive $x,y$,
relative to Report~44's inherited history, Pell, recoder, initialization,
endpoint and literal-simulation theorems; the one-input version quantifies
$x,y$ as well and pays for the Cantor relation $2N=(x+y-2)(x+y-1)+2y$. This is
Theorem~\ref{ptr:pc:thm:complete} with \eqref{ptr:pc:eq:sentinel}--\eqref{ptr:pc:eq:pair},
printed only there (Part~\ref{ptr:part:cert}). The recall's own remarks are
kept: the one-input version ``recognizes the same relation through the unique
positive pair associated with $N>0$''; ``This is ordinary paid arity
reduction, not a claim about empty-right machine inputs. The accepting ant
observable represents halting; the ant continues moving.''

\section{The fixed data and coordinate conventions}\label{ptr:sc:sec:data}
Use the following exact constants throughout:
\begin{align*}
 R&=601\,547\,591,& S&=576\,000,& K&=957,\\
 V&=400(R+2)=240\,619\,037\,200,& u&=2V=481\,238\,074\,400,& Z&=3^{400}.
\end{align*}
There are 960 width-600 storage slots in a macro, of which slots 1 through 958 are active. A pair index $s\in\{0,\ldots,956\}$ selects physical slots $s+1,s+2$. Horizontal coordinates are read modulo $S$. The next vertical macro is shifted horizontally by $S/2=288\,000$. Thus two macros give rectangular period $u$ vertically.

The fixed program JSON contains 958 compressed top-level nodes and specifies exactly $R$ operation rows. Its node forms are \codem{RANGE}, \codem{ROW}, \codem{COPY} and \codem{GATE}. These nodes describe a fixed sequence of adjacent-pair template placements. The new compiler never applies that sequence to a Boolean tape or an ant state. It lowers the finite description algebraically; in particular, it does not read the saved \codem{prefix_rows} field. The old random-access decoder and physical program are retained only as text specifications, not imported or run.

Finite colored maps are functions from finite subsets of $\Z^2$ to $\{0,1\}$. A colored union is valid when overlapping assignments agree. Its black set contains the coordinates assigned 1; unspecified coordinates have default color 0 in the atlas. It is essential to check agreement of white assignments as well as black union counts. The frozen JSON maps, anchor patch and compressed program are authenticated copies of the old atlas and interface data~\cite{ptr:sc:report40}. Their hashes, exact locations and read closure are recorded in the accompanying provenance and science manifests.

The proof concerns these concrete finite data, not arbitrary template families. Every asserted finite-map equality can be checked by set equality on the supplied cells. The general arguments then extend those exact finite equalities to the full height $R$, without generating $R$ physical rows or an astronomical bitmap.

\section{Exact occurrence polynomials from signed events}\label{ptr:sc:sec:occurrence}
For $a\in\{\sDUP,\sNAND,\sML,\sMR\}$ and $0\le s<K$, define the integer polynomial in an indeterminate $z$
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:sc:eq:occurrence}
 T_{a,s}(z)=\sum_{\substack{0\le r<R\\\text{row }r\text{ has kind }a\text{ and pair }s}}z^r.
\end{equation}
We construct $T_{a,s}=T_{a,s}(Z)$ without expanding the row sequence. These are fixed constants. Their definition does not add a witness, oracle or input-dependent operation.

\subsection{One monotone run}
A run starts at row time $n$, pair $s_0$, direction $d\in\{-1,1\}$ and length $L>0$. Its rows have slots $s_0+dk$ for $0\le k<L$. At those slots it contributes $z^{n+k}$. Create events
\[
 E(s_0)\mathrel{+}=z^n,\qquad E(s_0+dL)\mathrel{-}=z^{n+L}.
\]
Sweep the slots in direction $d$, with zero just outside the entering boundary, by
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:sc:eq:event}
 v(s)=zv(s-d)+E(s).
\end{equation}
\begin{lemma}[Run identity]\label{ptr:sc:lem:event}
The sweep of these two events is $z^{n+k}$ at $s=s_0+dk$ for $0\le k<L$, and zero at every other owned slot. The sum of events from any finite collection of runs gives exactly the sum of their occurrence contributions.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Before the start event the recurrence is zero. At $s_0$ it becomes $z^n$. Induction multiplies by $z$ once at each next slot, giving $z^{n+k}$. At the stop position the recurrence produces $z^{n+L}-z^{n+L}=0$, and stays zero afterward. This argument works for either sweep direction. Equation~\eqref{ptr:sc:eq:event} is linear in the event array, so superposition proves the finite-collection assertion over $\Z[z]$, not merely at tested numerical values. A stop event at exterior position $-1$ or $K$ affects no owned slot. The compiler still pays for creating that event.
\end{proof}
There are separate arrays for each of $\sML,\sMR$ and each direction. After four sweeps, the two directions for each kind are added. Singleton $\sDUP$ and $\sNAND$ occurrences accumulate directly at their pair indices.

\subsection{Exact lowering of the finite row specification}
The following are word-description identities, not assertions about machine execution. At logical width $m$ and index $i$, a \codem{DUP} word is a decreasing $\sMR$ run
\[
 (m-1,m-2,\ldots,i+1),
\]
followed by the singleton $(\sDUP,i)$. Its total row length is $m-i$. A \codem{NAND} word is the singleton $(\sNAND,i)$ followed by an increasing $\sML$ run $(i+1,\ldots,m-2)$, of total length $m-i-1$. Empty runs contribute no rows or gates.

The interchange word at $(m,i)$ consists of the following 24 pairs $(\text{kind},j)$, used at index $i+j$, updating the compile-time width by $+1$ after a \codem{DUP} word and $-1$ after a \codem{NAND} word:
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}llllll@{}}
$(D,0)$&$(D,2)$&$(D,1)$&$(D,3)$&$(N,2)$&$(D,2)$\\
$(N,1)$&$(N,2)$&$(N,1)$&$(D,1)$&$(D,0)$&$(D,2)$\\
$(N,1)$&$(D,1)$&$(N,0)$&$(N,1)$&$(N,0)$&$(D,1)$\\
$(D,3)$&$(N,2)$&$(D,2)$&$(N,1)$&$(N,2)$&$(N,1)$
\end{tabular}\end{center}
Here $D$ abbreviates \codem{DUP} and $N$ abbreviates \codem{NAND}. The final width is again $m$. Summing the 24 word lengths with those updates gives exactly $24(m-i)+14$ rows. This is the same displayed fixed word specification as in the pinned physical-program text.

A \codem{COPY} node $(m,i)$ is one \codem{DUP} word at $(m,i)$ followed by interchanges at
\[
 (m+1,i+1+t),\qquad 0\le t<m-i-1.
\]
A \codem{GATE} node $(m,p,q)$ is \codem{COPY}$(m,p)$, then \codem{COPY}$(m+1,q)$, then the singleton $(\sNAND,m)$. A \codem{RANGE} node is exactly its stated monotone run; a \codem{ROW} node is its singleton. Induction over these finite descriptions proves that the lowering has exactly the row-kind and pair-index sequence in~\eqref{ptr:sc:eq:occurrence}. No semantic simulation is required for this syntactic equality.

\subsection{Paid powers and the occurrence ledger}
Maintain a wire $P=Z^n$, initially the literal 1. For each nonempty run, construct
\[
 P_{\rm next}=PZ^L
\]
with one multiplication. Each distinct needed $Z^L$ is built once by a left-to-right binary chain and cached. For exponent $e\ge1$ the chain length is
\[
 \lambda(e)=\lfloor\log_2 e\rfloor+\operatorname{popcount}(e)-1;
\]
$e=0$ uses the literal 1. An exponent is fixed compilation data, never an exponentiation gate. The invariant $P=Z^n$ follows by induction on runs.

The exact finite lowering gives 5,456,442 nonempty runs: 2,724,814 directional and 2,731,628 singleton. It contains 2,730,765 word lowerings, 113,709 interchanges and 1,749 copies. The accumulated time is exactly $R$. Its power cache has 845 lengths including 0 and 1; additional cached powers cost 9,705 multiplications. Computing $Z=3^{400}$ costs 10. Hence
\begin{align*}
 M_T&=5\,456\,442+9\,705+10+4K=5\,469\,985,\\
 A_T&=2(2\,724\,814)+2\,731\,628+4K+2K+1\\
    &=8\,186\,999.
\end{align*}
The final $1$ in $A_T$ pays for zero as $1-1$. Each directional run costs two event updates, including unused exterior updates; each singleton costs one accumulation. The four sweeps and two kind-wise merges are retained in full. These formulas give 13,656,984 gates and agree with independent expansion of the new finite compiler.

\section{Finite geometric ownership}\label{ptr:sc:sec:geometry}
Put $F_0=400(R+1)$. A macro's vertical fundamental interval is $[50,V+50)$. Partition it into half-open height-400 slabs:
\begin{align*}
 \text{header: } &[50,450),\\
 \text{program }r: &[400r+50,400r+450),\quad 1\le r\le R,\\
 \text{footer: } &[F_0+50,F_0+450).
\end{align*}
This partition assigns every ordinate exactly once. Profiles $H[x],B[x],F[x]$ encode the black indicators in a header, a baseline program slab and a footer respectively. Their digit 0 is at the lower endpoint of their slab. Thus each is a 400-digit nonnegative ternary integer; the letter $F$ here denotes the footer profile, not the final polynomial.

\subsection{Bulk and the common seam}
The baseline bulk places DELAY in all 958 active slots, current LEFT\_TURN and RIGHT\_TURN routes, the previous LEFT\_TURN tail, and the vertical return wire. All components are clipped to the owned slab. The prior left-turn tail contributes 163 colored cells, 82 black, at local ordinates 50 through 76. The current left turn contributes 401 colored cells, 201 black, at 305 through 449. The current right turn contributes 684 colored cells, 341 black. The vertical wire's black column is $x=S-600+175$, and its white column is one unit to the left.

Every prior DELAY or pair-operation tail at local $y=450,\ldots,459$ agrees with the next round's incoming interface: 43 colored cells, 23 black, per slot. Those cells are owned by the following canonical slab. They already occur in its initial interface and add no second contribution. The union has no color conflict. In this baseline bulk the retained components are support-disjoint, giving 5,386,966 colored cells and 2,695,878 black cells.

\subsection{Header ownership and the staggered marker exception}
The header contains NOT and COPY in active slots, except that NOT is omitted at slot 481; the header turns; the upper corner and horizontal wire; the part of the vertical wire in the slab; and predecessor-footer tails. Those tails are translated by $S/2$ in $x$ and $-400$ in local $y$.

Ordinary predecessor COPY and RIGHT\_MARKER tails supply the common 23-black interface. The predecessor LEFT\_MARKER is different. Its slot 1 moves under the half-shift to slot 481, exactly where the current NOT is omitted. Its tail spans local $y=50,\ldots,259$ and contains 1,620 black cells. Relative to NOT, it adds 99 and removes 16 black cells. It overlaps current COPY in 43 colored cells, 23 black, at $y=250,\ldots,259$, with agreeing colors. The outer slots 0 and 959 receive the common tails even though they have no current header tile. Thus summing raw template black counts would be wrong; taking the compatible set union is necessary.

\subsection{Footer and wraparound routes}
The footer includes its NOT/COPY/marker placements, the common interface from the preceding program round, the preceding left-turn tail, the last 20 black cells of the vertical wire, a 170-black-cell bottom wire and a 10-black-cell east-to-north corner. It has no current turn maps. The LEFT\_MARKER tail beyond local $y=449$ is assigned to the next macro's header. All horizontal coordinates, including the top wire crossing the horizontal boundary, are reduced modulo $S$ before duplicate cells are unioned.

For precision, the finite component lists can be reconstructed from the following rules, where $k$ denotes a storage slot. In the header, use NOT at $1\le k\le958$, $k\ne481$, and COPY shifted vertically by 200 in all active slots. In the footer use NOT at $1\le k\le958$, $k\ne921$, and shifted COPY at $0\le k<960$ except $k=1,921$; place LEFT\_MARKER at 1 and RIGHT\_MARKER at 921. Insert all prior-slot DELAY tails for the footer. The header receives all predecessor-footer tails, using LEFT\_MARKER at 1, RIGHT\_MARKER at 921 and shifted COPY elsewhere. Both header and bulk have turns; both bulk and footer have the previous left-turn tail. The supplied finite checker spells out the wire intervals and corner coordinates directly from the pinned atlas text.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrrrr@{}}\toprule
Profile&Raw colored&Union colored&Raw black&Union black&Conflicts\\\midrule
Header&5,941,771&5,859,426&2,979,510&2,935,465&0\\
Bulk&5,386,966&5,386,966&2,695,878&2,695,878&0\\
Footer&5,944,375&5,862,030&2,980,815&2,936,770&0\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}\end{center}
These are exhaustive finite-union counts, not random samples. A separate geometric projector checks all 576,000 columns of each profile against independently assembled neighboring macro placements. It also checks the last program band. Synthetic height $R=2$ suffices for this local ownership check: each finite motif crosses at most a neighboring slab, the long return wire has a translation-invariant interior intersection with each slab, the header/footer seam types are separately included, and the actual $R$ only repeats the interior. This local reduction does not simulate the actual machine or assert any ant behavior.

\section{Signed operation corrections and the bulk identity}\label{ptr:sc:sec:correction}
Let $D_2$ be the black union of DELAY at horizontal offsets 0 and 600. For a pair kind $a$, let $A_a$ be its black set, using the specified NAND union when $a=\sNAND$. In particular NAND is the union of the finite NAND macro with COPY shifted by $(0,200)$ and $(600,200)$. Its internal overlaps agree; a naive black total would count 46 shared cells twice. Its exact union has 13,197 colored cells and 6,614 black cells.

For each horizontal offset $d$, define the signed 400-digit profile
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:sc:eq:q}
 Q_{a,d}=\sum_{b=0}^{399}\left(\ind_{A_a}(d,50+b)-\ind_{D_2}(d,50+b)\right)3^b.
\end{equation}
It is built from the disjoint plus mask $A_a\setminus D_2$ and minus mask $D_2\setminus A_a$. Both DELAYs are cell-disjoint; together they have 5,672 black cells.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrrr@{}}\toprule
Kind&Plus cells&Minus cells&Net cells&Nonzero columns\\\midrule
DUP&1,520&1,884&$-364$&763\\
NAND&3,447&2,505&942&1,159\\
MOVE\_LEFT&1,424&1,814&$-390$&678\\
MOVE\_RIGHT&1,495&2,001&$-506$&772\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}\end{center}
Every correction point has $0\le d<1200$ and $56\le y\le449$; NAND is supported even more narrowly in $57\le y\le297$. The shared incoming/outgoing seam has zero correction. Corrections miss margin routes and the actual outside neighboring DELAY slots at offsets $-600$ and $1200$. Successive program slabs have disjoint correction supports even when their selected pair changes.

\begin{lemma}[Exact replacement]\label{ptr:sc:lem:replacement}
In an owned program slab with operation kind $a$ at pair index $s$, the exact black-indicator profile is the baseline $B$ plus the translate of $Q_a$ by $600(s+1)$. There is no additional ownership subtraction or overlap correction.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
Within the selected pair's support, subtraction of $D_2$ removes exactly the baseline black indicators, while addition of $A_a$ inserts exactly the replacement indicators. Their intersection contributes zero to the difference. Outside the pair, every nonzero correction is disjoint from the other retained components. At the following or previous slab boundary, the common compatible seam has zero difference and has already been assigned to its unique slab. Hence the pointwise indicator after replacement is exactly 0 or 1 and is the actual colored-union black indicator. The statement holds also at horizontal period boundaries because translation and set union were taken modulo $S$.
\end{proof}
Now define
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:sc:eq:bulk}
 G=\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}Z^r,\qquad
 C[x]=\sum_{\substack{a,s,d\\x\equiv600(s+1)+d\pmod S}}Q_{a,d}T_{a,s},
 \qquad U[x]=B[x]G+C[x].
\end{equation}
\begin{proposition}[Exact program column]\label{ptr:sc:prop:bulk}
$U[x]$ is the exact ternary column of the $R$ consecutive owned program slabs, with the first program slab aligned to digit zero.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The baseline repeated $R$ times is $\sum_r B[x]Z^r=B[x]G$. For a fixed row $r$, exactly its prescribed kind and pair contributes $Z^r$ in~\eqref{ptr:sc:eq:occurrence}. Distributing the finite sums in~\eqref{ptr:sc:eq:bulk} therefore inserts the unique translated correction from Lemma~\ref{ptr:sc:lem:replacement} in every row. All coordinates and exponents align with that slab's 400-digit origin. This is an identity of integers obtained from a pointwise indicator identity. It does not assume that an arbitrary signed redundant ternary expansion has no carries. The resulting expansion has Boolean digits because it has been identified with the actual compatible black set.
\end{proof}

\section{Division free sums and the rotated periodic cut}\label{ptr:sc:sec:cut}
\subsection{The geometric sum is paid arithmetic}
Construct simultaneously
\[
 (P_n,G_n)=\left(Z^n,\sum_{k=0}^{n-1}Z^k\right),\qquad (P_1,G_1)=(Z,1).
\]
A doubling step is
\[
 P_{2n}=P_n^2,\qquad G_{2n}=G_n+P_nG_n;
\]
an ensuing odd step is
\[
 P_{2n+1}=P_{2n}Z,\qquad G_{2n+1}=G_{2n}+P_{2n}.
\]
\begin{lemma}\label{ptr:sc:lem:geometric}
The binary grammar above constructs the stated pair for every fixed $n\ge1$ using only addition and multiplication.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The invariant holds at 1. Split the exponent range $0,\ldots,2n-1$ into $0,\ldots,n-1$ and its translate by $n$ to obtain the doubling sum. Appending the term at exponent $2n$ gives the odd sum. The power identities follow by multiplication. Induction along the bits of $n$ proves the result. No quotient such as $(Z^n-1)/(Z-1)$ is evaluated.
\end{proof}
For $n=R$ there are 29 doublings and 16 odd steps, costing 74 multiplications and 45 additions. Five separate binary chains build
\[
 3^{375},\quad3^{V-425},\quad3^{V-400},\quad3^{V-25},\quad3^{u-25},
\]
with respective multiplication counts $14,49,45,52,49$. Thus the geometric-sum-and-shifts stage costs exactly $283M+45A$.

\subsection{Why the cut is at physical ordinate 75}
Reports 42 and 44 use the rotation $X=y-75$ and $Y=288650-x$~\cite{ptr:sc:report42,ptr:sc:report44}. The fixed coefficient \codem{tile:j} is
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:sc:eq:oldtile}
 \sum_{i=0}^{u-1}c(288650-j,i+75)3^i,
\end{equation}
where $c$ is the frozen periodic black indicator. Our natural profile origin is 50, so the rotation cuts 25 rows into a header. Split that header directly from its bits:
\[
 H[x]=H_{\rm low}[x]+3^{25}H_{\rm high}[x],
\]
where the low part has 25 digits and the high part 375. Both are separately constructed by Horner evaluation. No division or floor operation is in the arithmetic source.

Define
\begin{align}
 \op{Full}[x]&=H[x]+Z U[x]+3^{V-400}F[x],\label{ptr:sc:eq:full}\\
 \op{Cut}[x]&=H_{\rm high}[x]+3^{375}U[x]+3^{V-425}F[x],\label{ptr:sc:eq:cut}\\
 \op{Tile}[x]&=\op{Cut}[x]+3^{V-25}\op{Full}[x-S/2]
                   +3^{u-25}H_{\rm low}[x].\label{ptr:sc:eq:tile}
\end{align}
\begin{proposition}[Exact cut and wrap]\label{ptr:sc:prop:tile}
For every $x$ modulo $S$,
\[
 \op{Tile}[x]=\sum_{i=0}^{u-1}c(x,i+75)3^i.
\]
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The header starts at 50, the first program slab at 450 and the footer at $F_0+50$. Relative to origin 50 their exponents are $0,400,F_0=V-400$, proving~\eqref{ptr:sc:eq:full}. Removing the first 25 header rows and using origin 75 gives exponents $0,375,V-425$, proving~\eqref{ptr:sc:eq:cut}.

The first region in~\eqref{ptr:sc:eq:tile} has length $V-25$. At its end starts the complete next macro, with horizontal half-shift; at fixed global $x$ its local coordinate is $x-S/2$. That region has length $V$ and therefore starts at exponent $V-25$. After it come the 25 wrapped rows of the next header. Two half-shifts equal one full horizontal period, so their local coordinate is again $x$ and their first exponent is $(V-25)+V=u-25$. The three regions are disjoint, consecutive, and total $(V-25)+V+25=u$ rows. Their boundary ownership is already fixed by Section~\ref{ptr:sc:sec:geometry}. Concatenating their true Boolean columns gives the claimed equality.
\end{proof}
This proof accounts for the stagger and the cut simultaneously. Treating the period as two unshifted full macros, or replacing the exceptional predecessor marker by an ordinary tail, would give incorrect coefficients.

\section{Every old coefficient label}\label{ptr:sc:sec:labels}
For $0\le j<S$, put $x_j=(288650-j)\bmod S$. Bind
\[
 \codem{tile:j}\longmapsto\op{Tile}[x_j],\qquad
 \codem{first:j}\longmapsto\ind_{\text{header black}}(x_j,75).
\]
The first alias has the old value by Proposition~\ref{ptr:sc:prop:tile}; the second is precisely digit 25 of the fixed header and aliases the paid zero or literal 1. No bit extraction is an arithmetic operation on a variable or a giant integer; the compile-time finite header bit chooses one of two available wires.

For the 584 anchor labels, define $\delta(i,j)$ as the pinned patch's new-minus-old color at
\[
 (x,y)=(289200-j,i-144),\quad 0\le i\le220,
\]
or zero when no patch entry occurs. The patch has 2,806 entries, of which 1,401 are $+1$ and 1,405 are $-1$, with no duplicate coordinate. Construct
\[
 \codem{anchor:j}=\sum_{i=0}^{220}\delta(i,j)3^i
\]
by the unchanged 221-symbol signed Horner chain. The symbol $-1$ is the paid wire $(1-1)-1$. All $220M+220A$ per anchor row are retained, even when a symbol is zero.

The remaining named constants are exactly
\begin{align*}
 \codem{Cu}&=3^u,&\codem{Cx}&=3^u-1,\\
 \codem{Cbase}&=3^{u-219},&\codem{C198}&=3^{198},\\
 \codem{EndpointK}&=3^{481225262775},&\codem{EndpointD}&=3^u-2.
\end{align*}
The four independent binary chains have lengths 47, 50, 10 and 55. All ordinary main numerals are the eight labels $0,1,2,3,4,6,8,9$. Zero and 2 are already paid; 1 and 3 are the only literal leaves. Compute $4=2+2$, $6=3+3$, $8=4+4$, $9=3\times3$. The subtractions giving \codem{Cx} and \codem{EndpointD} are paid. The complete alias inventory is
\[
 S+S+584+6+8=1\,152\,598.
\]
An internal $-1$ wire is used for signed profiles and anchors; the main source has no \codem{C:-1} port. Equal-valued labels may alias the same expression without identifying their names. These identities establish the value of every port, not merely a sample.

\section{The exact gate ledger}\label{ptr:sc:sec:ledger}
A nonzero signed profile of width $w$ is formed from its $w$ fixed digits by the complete Horner recursion $a\leftarrow3a+d$. It costs $(w-1)M+(w-1)A$ irrespective of leading zero digits. Identical profiles of identical width share that chain; the all-zero profile aliases the already paid zero. Plus and minus masks are disjoint, so each digit selects one of the existing $-1,0,1$ expressions.

The cache has 736 nonzero width-400 profiles, 21 width-25 profiles and 81 width-375 profiles, so
\[
 736\cdot399+21\cdot24+81\cdot374=324\,462
\]
operations of each type build all small profiles. Two further additions/subtractions construct $-1$ and 2. The cache counts include operation corrections as well as $H,B,F$. For clarity, the footer's serialized dictionary has a zero mask available but the actual footer uses 246 distinct masks, all nonzero; dictionary inventory and used-mask inventory are different counts.

For corrections, every nonzero horizontal column of every kind is multiplied by every one of its $K=957$ occurrence constants and accumulated, even if an occurrence value is zero. Thus this stage costs
\[
 957(763+1159+678+772)=3\,227\,004
\]
of each type. For each $x$, forming $U$ costs $1M+1A$, forming Full costs $2M+2A$, forming Cut costs $2M+2A$, and the final Tile costs $2M+2A$. Hence the period join costs $7S=4\,032\,000$ of each type. Aliasing the tile/first labels adds no arithmetic.

\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrr@{}}\toprule
Stage&$M$&$A$&Total\\\midrule
Occurrence constants&5,469,985&8,186,999&13,656,984\\
Small profiles and basic literals&324,462&324,464&648,926\\
Geometric sum and five shifts&283&45&328\\
Operation corrections&3,227,004&3,227,004&6,454,008\\
Bulk, boundaries and period join&4,032,000&4,032,000&8,064,000\\
584 anchor rows&128,480&128,480&256,960\\
Remaining named constants&163&5&168\\\midrule
Prefix total&13,182,377&15,898,997&29,081,374\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}\end{center}
This verifies the prefix count in Theorem~\ref{ptr:sc:thm:prefix}. The saving relative to the old strict prefix is exactly
\[
 554\,386\,261\,678\,823\,757
\]
operations, a reduction by a factor of approximately $19\,063\,276\,092$. This comparison is only between the two specified grammars. It is an arithmetic operation-count reduction, not a numerical runtime or bit-complexity speedup: the enormous fixed integers retain exactly the same values and are represented by a shorter source, not materialized or made small. The arithmetic programs differ, while the coefficient identities prove equality of their resulting polynomial. No claim concerns the best possible source, other complete certificates, or operation-record priority.

\section{Strict binding to the variable source}\label{ptr:sc:sec:splice}
Let $N_P=29\,081\,374$, and give prefix gates consecutive identifiers $0,\ldots,N_P-1$. In this source an integer operand denotes an earlier gate identifier, not an integer literal. Only the tokens \codem{one} and \codem{three} are constant leaves. Let $L[k]$ be the prefix expression or literal leaf assigned to old coefficient key $k$.

For an old main operand $t$ define a fixed substitution $\sigma$ by four disjoint cases:
\begin{enumerate}
\item An old integer gate reference $r$ becomes $N_P+r$.
\item A fixed-coefficient operand \codem{C:k} becomes $L[k]$.
\item A declared raw or positive-witness coordinate name is preserved.
\item Every other operand is rejected.
\end{enumerate}
Append to $P$ every old gate $[r,\text{op},a,b]$ as
\[
 [N_P+r,\text{op},\sigma(a),\sigma(b)].
\]
Preserve the raw/positive declarations. Transform the operands of residual-pair metadata and of the final equation by the same rule. No \codem{C:} operand survives. The alias map is a total binding, not an additional input declaration or equation. In particular $L[0]$ is prefix gate 0, which computes $1-1$; the final zero side is paid.

\begin{proposition}[Pointwise identity of the splice]\label{ptr:sc:prop:splice}
The transformed source is topological and has exactly the old polynomial value for every assignment of all raw and witness coordinates.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The prefix is topological. An old main gate $r$ referenced only an earlier gate $q<r$, an old prescribed coefficient or a previously declared coordinate. After substitution, $N_P+q<N_P+r$; every prefix gate reference is less than $N_P$; and the declarations remain before their first main use. They may follow the input-independent prefix without changing their meaning. Thus topology is preserved.

Section~\ref{ptr:sc:sec:labels} proves equality of each substituted coefficient value. Induct on $r$: inputs and coefficients agree, and applying the unchanged opcode to equal operand values gives equality of the translated gate and the old gate. This proves equality of all residual operands and of the final polynomial, not only equivalence of their zero sets. Intermediate expressions are permitted to be signed; no positivity is imposed on prefix or main gate values.
\end{proof}
The two-input main source has exactly $1\,153\,586M+1\,153\,871A$; the one-input source has $1\,153\,590M+1\,153\,877A$. Adding these counts to the prefix, with no binding arithmetic, gives Theorem~\ref{ptr:sc:thm:prefix}. The only final equation is respectively gate $31\,388\,830=\text{gate }0$ or gate $31\,388\,840=\text{gate }0$. The 285 or 286 stored residual pairs are metadata used by the paid final sum of squares, not extra final equations.

The witness domains remain exactly those of Report 44: five positive history parameters, 61 positive history witnesses, four positive initialization-geometry witnesses, 392 positive recoder witnesses and three positive endpoint witnesses. This totals 465. The one-input version adds the two positive side-word codes for 467. No fixed coefficient becomes a variable. Both raw inputs, or the single paired input, range over $\Zp$.

Every prefix wire has variable degree zero. Pointwise polynomial equality therefore preserves exact degree, rather than just a syntactic upper bound. More explicitly, the inherited main source has exactly two maximal residuals with highest homogeneous term $-W^{1\,152\,000}$; all other residuals have smaller degree. The sum of their squares has highest homogeneous part
\[
 2W^{2\,304\,000}.
\]
It cannot cancel over $\Z$. The pairing residual has degree two and does not affect it. Thus the exact degree remains 2,304,000. No geometric equation is substituted into $W$ when calculating this polynomial degree.

\section{Evidence and reproducibility}\label{ptr:sc:sec:evidence}
The mathematical proof has three different layers. The general algebraic identities are proved above over integers or polynomial rings. Exact finite-map checks establish the concrete supports, overlaps and coefficient inputs to those identities. Stream checks establish that the supplied arithmetic grammar has the reported operation count, legal leaves, topology and binding. These layers should not be conflated: a stream hash identifies bytes, and a modular comparison tests values modulo its modulus; neither alone proves equality of astronomical integer coefficients.

\subsection{Prefix identity and independent checks}
The complete new prefix has been generated into a checked streaming sink, rather than retained as a large text file. Its canonical gate serialization has SHA-256
\begin{quote}\small\codem{6a01d7c1d8ee6b268d49c921b3263b578c152e49c62810d5aa877a4103cb9759}.
\end{quote}
\needspace{4\baselineskip}
Its ordered output-alias serialization has SHA-256
\begin{quote}\small\codem{87c890e3fe7bb138378d09df71125cc5783219b99d171ed426ed9791b0cdcc3e}.
\end{quote}
The independent checker verifies all 29,081,374 gates, the allowed $1/3$ leaves, topology, stage counts and the full 1,152,598-label inventory. Its all-label evaluation modulo 1,000,003 is supplementary regression evidence. Exact coefficient equality rests on the event, finite-union and cut proofs. Independent finite geometric projections agree in every profile column with zero color conflicts, and exact symbolic run tests supplement the general proof of Lemma~\ref{ptr:sc:lem:event}.

\subsection{What execution does and does not establish}
The coefficient compiler executes newly authored arithmetic lowering and finite JSON-set analysis only. It does not execute an upstream physical ant program, color recipe, row decoder, saved row schedule or saved upstream arithmetic schedule. The fixed compressed nodes are compilation data. For the main splice, the authenticated Report 44-owned arithmetic generator may be reused after its import/read closure has been inspected; that narrow reuse is distinct from running the forbidden upstream physical or color programs. The package documents this boundary and retains text-only specifications where appropriate.

The old astronomical Horner prefix is not generated, numerically evaluated or represented as a freshly verified stream. Equality with it is mathematical: its Horner grammar evaluates the same prescribed coefficients proved above. The new prefix's much shorter source can be generated without materializing those coefficient integers. Running a finite checker does not prove the arbitrary-integer Pell or history theorem, nor the literal machine/ant simulation. Those remain the same inherited dependencies as in Report 44.

The accompanying release separates the frozen scientific payload from manuscript/PDF construction and final-release quality checks. Source and data pins authenticate the frozen candidate; archive manifests authenticate delivered files. PDF rebuilding uses local pdfLaTeX with shell escape disabled. Reproduction writes outputs to external new directories and does not mutate the frozen source. Detailed commands, expected hashes and interpretation of each receipt are in the release README.

\section{Further questions}\label{ptr:sc:sec:further}
Several concrete improvements remain open within this fixed problem. Can the repeated copy/interchange description be compiled at a coarser level than 5.46 million runs while still exposing every paid arithmetic operation? Can occurrence polynomials or shifted correction products be shared more aggressively without losing a transparent exact ledger? Can a joint addition chain replace the independent fixed-shift and endpoint powers? These are possible refinements, not savings already claimed here.

A different question is a lower bound for constructing this particular labeled coefficient family in the literal $1/3$ language. No such lower bound is provided. The shortest source, the effect of allowing other literal sets, and comparisons with other complete Diophantine presentations require separately specified cost models. Reducing fixed-coefficient construction also does not reduce the positive witness dimension or prove a stronger machine-input universality statement. Those belong to different mathematical questions.

The present conclusion is exact and limited: the complete fixed coefficient family of Report 44 admits the explicit 29,081,374-operation prefix, and its literal splice preserves the existing polynomial, domains, equation and degree while giving the strict totals in Theorem~\ref{ptr:sc:thm:prefix}.

\phantomsection\label{ptr:fu:sec:intro}
\section*{Report 48: fusion of the background polynomials}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Report 48: fusion of the background polynomials}
\noindent\wtag\ \emph{Exact Fusion of the Ant Background Polynomials. A
smaller literal arithmetic source for the same polynomial}, Research
Report~48, dated 4 October 2026, author line ``Mathematical research
report''; batch-88 manuscript~10, archive
\path{Exact_Fusion_of_the_Ant_Background_Polynomials_Package.zip}. It pins no
ProveIt commit of its own and inherits Report~44's pins; its frozen reference
copy of Report~47's archive is byte-identical to batch-88 manuscript~09 and
is not shipped twice. Numbered 48.$k$; labels \texttt{ptr:fu:}; files
\texttt{06-ant-fusion-}. Its counts have since been lowered by the
programme (the remark after Theorem~\ref{ptr:pc:thm:complete}).

\begin{quote}\small\noindent\textbf{Abstract (Report 48).} We fuse the fixed-coefficient construction of Research Report 47 with the two dense spatial Horner expressions in the initializer of Research Report 44. A three-band cyclic-index identity factors the operation corrections into twelve short spatial factors and twenty-four rotated occurrence factors. Exact repeated-block grouping compresses the remaining fixed spatial profiles, with division-free, fully paid geometric weights. The resulting literal-$1/3$ sources have 14,658,934 arithmetic operations for two positive raw inputs and 14,658,944 for one. They compute exactly the same complete integer polynomial, on every assignment of the same coordinates, as the frozen predecessors. The positive witness counts remain 465 and 467, with one equation and exact variable degree 2,304,000. We prove the new polynomial identities, audit the two precise source replacements and every retained consumer, give the complete arithmetic ledger, and distinguish algebraic proof, finite checks and source reconstruction. No quotient-ring identity, nonzero assumption on the spatial variable, global optimality or operation-record claim is used.
\end{quote}

\section{Result and comparison}\label{ptr:fu:sec:result}
Reports 44 and 47 specify a fixed Diophantine polynomial source arising from a periodic-ant realization of the pinned U15 machine~\cite{ptr:fu:r44,ptr:fu:r47}. Report 47 reduces the cost of constructing the fixed coefficients. The present refinement avoids first constructing all spatial coefficients separately and then evaluating two dense polynomials from them. Instead, it constructs those two polynomials directly from the same exact coefficient identities.

An arithmetic gate is a binary addition, subtraction or multiplication of already available expressions. We write $M$ for multiplication count and $A$ for the combined addition/subtraction count. The only constant leaves are 1 and 3. Fixed loop indices, mask bits and exponents are compilation data; they do not supply free integer leaves. Aliasing an available expression is free, while every emitted arithmetic operation, including operations on zero or one, is paid. Gates are expressions, not extra quantified witnesses.

\Needspace{12\baselineskip}
\begin{theorem}[Exact fused sources]\label{ptr:fu:thm:main}
There are explicit topological literal-$1/3$ arithmetic sources with the following counts.
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrrr@{}}\toprule
Raw positive inputs & $M$ & $A$ & Total & Positive witnesses\\\midrule
Two &5,971,120&8,687,814&14,658,934&465\\
One &5,971,124&8,687,820&14,658,944&467\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Each has one final equation and computes the same complete final integer polynomial as its corresponding frozen Report 44 and Report 47 source, for every integer assignment of the declared coordinates. Its exact total variable degree is $2,304,000$. All positive domains, names and witness order are unchanged.
\end{theorem}
The new totals improve Report 47's $31,388,831$ and $31,388,841$ by exactly $16,729,897$ operations each, or approximately $53.2989\%$ for two inputs. Report 47 remains valid for its displayed grammar. The comparison is between these specified sources; it is neither a lower bound nor a priority or global operation-record claim.

{\small\wtag\ Two later programme packets lower these counts further, to
14,620,779 / 14,620,789 and then 14,620,711 / 14,620,721, by sharing inside
this source; the remark after Theorem~\ref{ptr:pc:thm:complete} lists every
count of the polynomial.\par}

The equality is stronger than equality of zero sets. It allows substitution into every old consumer without altering any residual polynomial or the final sum of squares. It is also independent of restrictions that particular solutions impose on the spatial coordinate $W$: the new identities hold as ordinary polynomial identities, including at $W=0,1,-1$.

\section{Inherited inputs and the scope of the new proof}\label{ptr:fu:sec:inputs}
We use distinct symbols for the horizontal period and the physical macro height:
\begin{align*}
 S&=576\,000=600\cdot960,& K&=957,& R&=601\,547\,591,\\
 L&=400(R+2)=240\,619\,037\,200,& u&=2L,& Z&=3^{400},\\
 c&=288\,650=600\cdot481+50.&&&&
\end{align*}
The old arithmetic initializer calls $S$ its \code{V}; the Report 47 profile construction calls $L$ its \code{V}. These are different dimensions. All spatial Horner polynomials here have $S$ coefficients, not $L$ coefficients.

For each operation kind
\[
 a\in\{\op{DUP},\op{NAND},\op{MOVE\_LEFT},\op{MOVE\_RIGHT}\}
\]
and each pair index $0\le s<K$, Report 47 constructs
\[
 T_{a,s}=\sum_{\substack{0\le r<R\\\text{row }r\text{ has kind }a\text{ at pair }s}}Z^r.
\]
The signed 400-digit ternary profile $Q_{a,d}$, $0\le d<1200$, describes the operation's exact correction to the two baseline DELAY slots. Its physical translation is $600(s+1)+d$. The unsigned profiles $H[x],B[x],F[x]$, indexed modulo $S$, encode the owned header, bulk and footer slabs. In this notation $F[x]$ is a footer coefficient, not the final Diophantine polynomial.

The masks define $H_{\rm low}$ as digits 0 through 24 of $H$, $H_{\rm high}$ as the remaining digits shifted down by 25, and $\op{first}[x]$ as digit 25. Thus
\[
 H[x]=H_{\rm low}[x]+3^{25}H_{\rm high}[x].
\]
Taking bits of the finite compile-time masks merely selects the prescribed digits; the arithmetic source does not divide an enormous coefficient or extract bits of a variable.

The inherited finite ownership theorem gives, with all $x$ indices reduced modulo $S$,
\begin{align}
 C[x]&=\sum_{\substack{a,s,d\\x\equiv600(s+1)+d\pmod S}} Q_{a,d}T_{a,s},\label{ptr:fu:eq:C}\\
 G_Z&=\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}Z^r,& U[x]&=G_Z B[x]+C[x],\label{ptr:fu:eq:U}\\
 \op{Full}[x]&=H[x]+ZU[x]+3^{L-400}F[x],\label{ptr:fu:eq:Full}\\
 \op{Cut}[x]&=H_{\rm high}[x]+3^{375}U[x]+3^{L-425}F[x],\label{ptr:fu:eq:Cut}\\
 \op{Tile}[x]&=\op{Cut}[x]+3^{L-25}\op{Full}[x-S/2]
                 +3^{u-25}H_{\rm low}[x].\label{ptr:fu:eq:Tile}
\end{align}
The three vertical portions in~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:Tile} have lengths $L-25,L,25$. Their total is $u$, their cut begins at physical ordinate 75, and the middle macro has the horizontal half-shift. Report 47 proves the exact finite colored unions, signed corrections and boundary ownership underlying these identities. In particular, they are true coefficient identities, not an assumption that arbitrary signed ternary digits never carry.

The old labels are
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:fu:eq:labels}
 \code{tile:j}=\op{Tile}[(c-j)\bmod S],\qquad
 \code{first:j}=\op{first}[(c-j)\bmod S],\quad 0\le j<S.
\end{equation}
This report takes that coefficient theorem and the occurrence-compiler theorem as inherited inputs. It proves the new spatial identities and literal-source substitution in full; it does not repeat the physical template or machine-simulation construction.

\paragraph{Inherited recognition statement.}
\wtag\ Report~48 recalls here the recognition theorem of Report~44 in its own
notation, $\mathcal H(x,y)$ for $H(x,y)$ and $\mathcal P_2$ for $F_2$:
$\mathcal H(x,y)\iff\exists z\in\Zp^{465}\;\mathcal P_2(x,y,z)=0$, relative to
the unchanged history, Pell, recoder, initializer, endpoint and
literal-simulation theorems of Report~44, with the same sentinel decoding;
the one-input source quantifies the two side codes, retains the paid pairing
relation $2N=(x+y-2)(x+y-1)+2y$ and has 467 positive witnesses. This is
Theorem~\ref{ptr:pc:thm:complete}, printed only there. The recall's own
remarks are kept: ``This is the same raw-pair recognition statement, not a
new assertion about empty-right machine inputs or stronger universality. The
accepting ant observable represents halting; the ant itself continues
moving.''

\section{The ordinary polynomial cyclic index identity}\label{ptr:fu:sec:cyclic}
For any fixed $S$-array $A$, any $p\in\{0,S/2\}$ and an unrestricted indeterminate $W$, define
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:fu:eq:E}
 \E_p(A;W)=\sum_{j=0}^{S-1}A[(c-j-p)\bmod S]W^j.
\end{equation}
Permuting the fixed array indices gives the equivalent expression
\[
 \E_p(A;W)=\sum_{x=0}^{S-1}A[x]W^{(c-p-x)\bmod S}.
\]
The modulo operation chooses a fixed coefficient position. It does not reduce a polynomial modulo $W^S-1$, and it implies no relation $W^S=1$.

Partition the operation offsets into three bands
\[
 I_0=\{0,\ldots,50\},\quad I_1=\{51,\ldots,650\},\quad
 I_2=\{651,\ldots,1199\}.
\]
Their lengths are $51,600,549$; all three factors are padded to width 600 in the emitted grammar. For $d\in I_b$ define
\[
 \ell_b(d)=50+600b-d,\qquad
 k_{b,p}(s)=(480-b-s-p/600)\bmod960.
\]
Both $p/600=0,480$ are fixed integers.

\begin{lemma}[Exact exponent separation]\label{ptr:fu:lem:exp}
For every $0\le s<957$, $d\in I_b$ and $p\in\{0,S/2\}$,
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:fu:eq:exp}
 (c-p-600(s+1)-d)\bmod S=600k_{b,p}(s)+\ell_b(d).
\end{equation}
Both sides are ordinary integers in $\{0,\ldots,S-1\}$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The three bands give respectively $0\le\ell_0\le50$, $0\le\ell_1\le599$, and $51\le\ell_2\le599$. Hence the right side is nonnegative and at most $600\cdot959+599=S-1$. Before reducing $k$, its value gives
\[
 600(480-b-s-p/600)+50+600b-d
 =c-p-600(s+1)-d.
\]
Reducing $k$ changes this by a multiple of $600\cdot960=S$. The unique least nonnegative representative proves~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:exp}. This includes both band boundaries and all spatial wrap boundaries.
\end{proof}

Define the short and rotated factors by
\begin{align}
 \calQ_{a,b}(W)&=\sum_{d\in I_b}Q_{a,d}W^{\ell_b(d)},\label{ptr:fu:eq:Qfactor}\\
 \calL_{a,b,p}(Y)&=\sum_{s=0}^{956}T_{a,s}Y^{k_{b,p}(s)}.\label{ptr:fu:eq:Lfactor}
\end{align}
The first has degree at most 599. The second has degree at most 959, and the map $s\mapsto k_{b,p}(s)$ is injective, since two distinct $s$ values differ by less than 960. Exactly three of its 960 coefficient positions therefore contain the paid zero expression.

\begin{theorem}[Three-band correction factorization]\label{ptr:fu:thm:corr}
Treat every coefficient as an independent symbol. In the ordinary polynomial ring
\[\Z[\{Q_{a,d}\},\{T_{a,s}\}][W]\]
we have
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:fu:eq:correction}
 \E_p(C;W)=\sum_a\sum_{b=0}^2
       \calQ_{a,b}(W)\calL_{a,b,p}(W^{600}).
\end{equation}
Consequently the identity holds after substituting the actual inherited integer profiles and occurrence constants.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Expand~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:C} in the second expression for~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:E}. Each term $Q_{a,d}T_{a,s}$ has exponent
$(c-p-600(s+1)-d)\bmod S$. Apply Lemma~\ref{ptr:fu:lem:exp}, then use
$W^{600k+\ell}=W^\ell(W^{600})^k$ and finite distributivity separately on each band. This yields~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:correction}. If several terms contribute to the same coefficient, their sum is retained on both sides; no disjointness assumption on the correction supports is required. Every exponent already lies in the ordinary spatial range, so no reduction of a product polynomial is involved.
\end{proof}

\paragraph{Paid grammar.}
Construct $Y=W^{600}$ once by a binary chain of 12 multiplications. Evaluate twelve full length-600 Horner polynomials for~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:Qfactor}, sharing them between phases. Evaluate twenty-four full length-960 Horner polynomials for~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:Lfactor}. Multiply each by its short factor, and sum twelve terms per phase. Thus
\begin{align*}
 M_{\rm short}=A_{\rm short}&=12\cdot599=7\,188,\\
 M_{\rm long}&=24\cdot959+24=23\,040,\\
 A_{\rm long}&=24\cdot959+2\cdot11=23\,038.
\end{align*}
Leading and internal zero coefficients remain in these dense Horner recurrences. The improvement comes from the proved factorization, not from retroactively deleting paid operations.

\section{Exact repeated block compression}\label{ptr:fu:sec:blocks}
For each $A\in\{H,B,F,H_{\rm low},H_{\rm high},\op{first}\}$ and phase $p$, partition the $S$ spatial coefficient positions into 960 consecutive blocks of width 600. Let
\[
 a_{k,r}=A[(c-600k-r-p)\bmod S],\quad 0\le k<960,\quad0\le r<600.
\]
Group $k$ values only when their complete 600-tuples agree. Write $J_g$ for one group of block positions and $b_g[r]$ for its common tuple. The groups must form a disjoint cover of $\{0,\ldots,959\}$.

\begin{proposition}[Exact block identity]\label{ptr:fu:prop:block}
With the grouping above,
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:fu:eq:block}
 \E_p(A;W)=\sum_g\left(\sum_{r=0}^{599}b_g[r]W^r\right)
                         \left(\sum_{k\in J_g}(W^{600})^k\right).
\end{equation}
The half-phase rotates each group's block positions by 480 modulo 960 and leaves its tuple unchanged.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Write each $j$ uniquely as $600k+r$. Substitute $a_{k,r}=b_g[r]$ for the unique group containing $k$, and distribute the finite sum. For the second assertion, the phase-$S/2$ tuple at $k$ is the phase-zero tuple at $(k+480)\bmod960$. Thus a phase-zero group $J_g$ becomes $\{(k-480)\bmod960:k\in J_g\}$. This is an exact permutation of fixed coefficient positions, not a transformation assuming a value of $W$.
\end{proof}

The distinct tuple counts, verified in both phases, are
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrrrrr@{}}\toprule
Profile&$H$&$B$&$F$&$H_{\rm low}$&$H_{\rm high}$&first\\\midrule
Distinct blocks&5&4&6&3&5&4\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Appendix~\ref{ptr:fu:app:groups} gives every phase-zero position group. The receipt records every position in both phases and a digest of each complete mask tuple. Verification compares actual entries, not merely digests or a few sample columns.

\subsection{Division free geometric weights}
For a maximal consecutive run $\{k,\ldots,k+n-1\}\subseteq J_g$, its weight is
\[
 Y^kG_n(Y),\qquad G_n(Y)=\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}Y^i.
\]
Construct $P_n(Y)=Y^n$ together with $G_n$ by the following finite binary recursion:
\begin{align}
 P_1&=Y,&G_1&=1,\notag\\
 P_{2n}&=P_n^2,&G_{2n}&=G_n+P_nG_n,\label{ptr:fu:eq:geom}\\
 P_{2n+1}&=P_{2n}Y,&G_{2n+1}&=G_{2n}+P_{2n}.\notag
\end{align}
\begin{lemma}[Paid geometric pair]\label{ptr:fu:lem:geom}
Recursion~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:geom} computes $(Y^n,\sum_{i=0}^{n-1}Y^i)$ in $\Z[Y]$ for every integer $n\ge1$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
The statement holds at $n=1$. Multiplication by $Y^n$ shifts the exponents of $G_n$ from $0,\ldots,n-1$ to $n,\ldots,2n-1$, proving the even sum and power. The odd step adds the next monomial $Y^{2n}$ and multiplies the power by $Y$. Induction along the binary digits proves the claim.
\end{proof}
{\small\wtag\ This is Report~47's Lemma~\ref{ptr:sc:lem:geometric}, with $Y$
for $Z$, proved by the same induction along the binary digits. Report~48
states it in $\Z[Y]$ for an indeterminate $Y$, which its identities at
$W=0,\pm1$ need, so the statement and its short proof are kept here as a
marked second statement.\par}
Each even step costs $2M+1A$ and each following odd extension $1M+1A$. The power output remains paid even when only the sum is consumed. Independent powers $Y^k$ use ordinary binary chains. At $k=0$, the power aliases literal 1; at $n=1$, the sum aliases literal 1. The source omits a run product only through the explicitly specified branches $k=0$ or $n=1$. It retains every group product in~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:block}.

No expression divides by $Y-1$, $W$ or any other expression. Hence no special case or limiting argument is needed at $W=0,1,-1$. In particular, when $W=0$, $Y^0=1$ and the finite polynomials evaluate normally; when $W=\pm1$, $Y=1$ and $G_n(Y)=n$ by its finite sum.

\subsection{Sharing and the closed block ledger}
One fusion object has a single fixed $W$ and $Y=W^{600}$. It shares identical coefficient-wire tuples as whole spatial Horner blocks; identical position tuples share their weight polynomial. Powers and geometric sums use explicit caches scoped to that same object. The eight actual profile requests, in source order, are
\[
 (H,S/2),\ (B,0),\ (B,S/2),\ (F,0),\ (F,S/2),\
 (H_{\rm low},0),\ (H_{\rm high},0),\ (\op{first},0).
\]
They use 37 groups in total, 26 distinct spatial coefficient-wire tuples and 18 distinct position tuples. The complete closed ledger is
\begin{center}\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrr@{}}\toprule
Fixed-block work&$M$&$A$\\\midrule
26 full width-600 spatial Horner blocks&15,574&15,574\\
37 group products and 29 group-sum additions&37&29\\
Cached $Y$ powers&119&0\\
Cached geometric pairs&168&104\\
13 run products and 7 run-sum additions&13&7\\\midrule
Total&15,911&15,714\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The cached exponents are $0,1,2,3,39,40,479,480,481,482,483,519,520,959$; the cached geometric lengths are $1,2,36,440,477,478,479,480,919,957$. They are part of the deterministic grammar. Every requested small profile is already in the paid prefix cache, so no hidden profile construction is appended through a stale prefix object during fusion.

\section{Assembly of the two initializer polynomials}\label{ptr:fu:sec:assembly}
Set
\begin{align}
 \mathcal U_p&=G_Z\E_p(B;W)+\E_p(C;W),\label{ptr:fu:eq:Up}\\
 \mathcal H_{1/2}&=\E_{S/2}(H;W)+Z\mathcal U_{S/2}
                          +3^{L-400}\E_{S/2}(F;W),\label{ptr:fu:eq:fullp}\\
 \mathcal C_0&=\E_0(H_{\rm high};W)+3^{375}\mathcal U_0
                          +3^{L-425}\E_0(F;W),\label{ptr:fu:eq:cutp}\\
 \calT(W)&=\mathcal C_0+3^{L-25}\mathcal H_{1/2}
                          +3^{u-25}\E_0(H_{\rm low};W),\label{ptr:fu:eq:tilep}\\
 \calF(W)&=\E_0(\op{first};W).\label{ptr:fu:eq:firstp}
\end{align}
\begin{proposition}[Exact dense outputs]\label{ptr:fu:prop:dense}
The fused expressions satisfy
\[
 \calT(W)=\sum_{j=0}^{S-1}\code{tile:j}\,W^j,\qquad
 \calF(W)=\sum_{j=0}^{S-1}\code{first:j}\,W^j
\]
as ordinary integer polynomials.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The operator $\E_p$ is linear in its coefficient array. Apply it to~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:U}--\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:Tile}, using Theorem~\ref{ptr:fu:thm:corr} and Proposition~\ref{ptr:fu:prop:block} for the computed spatial expressions. The middle array $x\mapsto\op{Full}[x-S/2]$ becomes $\E_{S/2}(\op{Full};W)$ directly from~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:E}. This gives~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:Up}--\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:tilep}. Equation~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:labels} then identifies the old dense coefficients, and the first polynomial follows immediately from its definition.
\end{proof}
After the profile and correction polynomials are available, the two bulk assemblies cost $2M+2A$ together, the full and cut expressions each cost $2M+2A$, and the final tile assembly costs $2M+2A$. The total is $8M+8A$. The first polynomial needs no further gate beyond its fixed-block construction.

\section{The retained literal constant prefix}\label{ptr:fu:sec:prefix}
Fusion removes the need for the 1,152,000 individual tile and first coefficient ports, but it does not introduce free fixed constants. The prefix still constructs every required occurrence, profile, shift, anchor and small numeral.

\paragraph{Occurrence constants.}
The authenticated Report 47 compiler is retained unchanged. It lowers the fixed compressed description into signed run events without executing the ant or Boolean tape. A directional run of length $n$, beginning at time $t$ and slot $s_0$ in direction $d\in\{-1,1\}$, contributes events $Z^t$ at $s_0$ and $-Z^{t+n}$ at $s_0+dn$. Sweeping by $v(s)=Zv(s-d)+E(s)$ gives the exact occurrence powers. Linearity combines the runs. The inherited finite lowering and its counts remain $5,469,985M+8,186,999A=13,656,984$ gates. This includes the paid zero $1-1$, all event updates, cached powers, four sweeps and two kind-wise merges. The arbitrary row count $R$ is encoded by the fixed description and its algebraic lowering, not by an executed physical schedule.

\paragraph{Small profiles.}
A nonzero signed width-$w$ mask is built by the complete ternary Horner chain from its $w$ fixed digits. It costs $(w-1)M+(w-1)A$, including leading zeros. Disjoint plus and minus masks select digits from the existing $-1,0,1$ expressions. The zero mask aliases paid zero; identical masks of the same width share a chain. The entire original inventory is retained, even if a profile is not subsequently needed:
\[
 736\cdot399+21\cdot24+81\cdot374=324\,462
\]
of each operation type. The two extra additions/subtractions construct $-1=(1-1)-1$ and $2=1+1$. The 838 nonzero cache keys have widths 400, 25 and 375 in counts 736, 21 and 81. The independent cache check proves that all subsequent profile requests are contained in this inventory.

\paragraph{Geometric constant and shifts.}
The prefix constructs $G_Z=\sum_{r=0}^{R-1}Z^r$ by Lemma~\ref{ptr:fu:lem:geom}, with $Y=Z$, and the five fixed powers
\[
 3^{375},\quad3^{L-425},\quad3^{L-400},\quad3^{L-25},\quad3^{u-25}
\]
by paid binary chains. Together they cost $283M+45A$. An exponent is a finite chain specification, not an exponentiation gate.

\paragraph{All anchors and remaining ports.}
All 584 anchor rows remain. Each is a signed 221-symbol ternary Horner expression and costs $220M+220A$, totaling $128,480M+128,480A$. Their later spatial Horner evaluation also remains in the old main source. The six named constants are
\begin{align*}
 \code{Cu}&=3^u,&\code{Cx}&=3^u-1,&\code{Cbase}&=3^{u-219},\\
 \code{C198}&=3^{198},&\code{EndpointK}&=3^{481225262775},&
 \code{EndpointD}&=3^u-2.
\end{align*}
The four independent power chains cost $47,50,10,55$ multiplications. Together with $9=3\times3$, they give 163 multiplications. Constructing $4=2+2$, $6=3+3$, $8=4+4$ and the two named subtractions costs five additions/subtractions. The eight ordinary numeral ports are $0,1,2,3,4,6,8,9$; zero and 2 are already paid, and 1 and 3 are the only constant leaves. There is no main \code{C:-1} port. Hence exactly
\[
 584+6+8=598
\]
old constant ports remain, each bound to a paid expression or a permitted literal. The complete input-independent prefix costs $14,563,366$ gates.

\section{Every consumer and the complete source substitution}\label{ptr:fu:sec:splice}
The change is localized to two exact old-main gate intervals. Old gate identifiers are zero-based, and the intervals below are half-open:
\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{@{}lrrr@{}}\toprule
Dense expression&Start&End&Removed gates\\\midrule
Tile Horner&1,264&1,153,262&1,151,998\\
First Horner&1,153,262&2,305,260&1,151,998\\\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Each expression starts with coefficient $S-1$ and performs
\[
 a\leftarrow aW+\code{kind:j},\quad j=S-2,S-3,\ldots,0,
\]
using two records per step. Every multiplication and addition in each interval is checked against this exact recurrence. The total removed is
\[
 4(S-1)=2\,303\,996.
\]
Only each interval's final output is mapped to its fused replacement. Every other removed output is marked unavailable, and any reference to one from outside its interval causes rejection.

\subsection{Initializer consumers remain unchanged}
To make the scope explicit, let $X,h,k,P$ denote the unchanged positive initializer geometry witnesses, and let $A_*,B_*,T_*$ denote its unchanged recoder outputs. Write $W_p$ for the old coordinate named \code{Wp}, and let $Q_*$ be the old geometry coordinate named \code{Q}. The following old definitions and residual pairs remain, with their original source order and arithmetic:
\begin{align*}
 h_x&=X+1,& W_p-1&=\code{Cx}\,h_x,\\
 g&=W^S,& h-1&=(g-1)k,\\
 Q_*&=h^2,& h_y&=k(h+1),\\
 h&=P(A_*g),&\op{InitialHead}&=\code{Cu}\,h.
\end{align*}
The recoder's internal source and residuals are unchanged. The only new operands are the two proved outputs in
\begin{equation}\label{ptr:fu:eq:bg}
 \op{bg}=h_y\bigl(h_x\calT(W)+W_p\calF(W)\bigr).
\end{equation}
In particular $W_p$ is its existing coordinate, not an inferred free power, and neither multiplier $h_x$ nor $h_y$ is dropped.

Let $\mathcal A(W)=\sum_{j=0}^{583}\code{anchor:j}\,W^j$ be the retained full anchor Horner polynomial. Every subsequent initializer expression is retained:
\begin{align*}
 \op{anch}&=(W^{S-550}A_*)\mathcal A(W),\\
 \op{middle}&=W^{24000}T_*+B_*,\\
 \op{tail}&=W^{263945}(W^{264000}+1)\op{middle},\\
 \op{markerTail}&=W^{23945}+\op{tail},\\
 \op{removed}&=\code{C198}(W+1)\op{markerTail},\\
 \delta&=\code{Cbase}\,P(\op{anch}-\op{removed}),\\
 \op{InitialMemoryPlus}&=\op{bg}+\delta+1.
\end{align*}
These equations summarize the original arithmetic, whose exact parenthesization and individual gates remain unchanged. The five displayed spatial powers are still paid in the old main. The history module, endpoint module, optional one-input pairing module, residual metadata and finalizer are also retained.

\subsection{Topological binding and polynomial equality}
The input-independent prefix is generated first. The old main is then regenerated by the authenticated owned arithmetic generator, whose complete old canonical hash must match the frozen target for each arity. Its declarations are preserved. Fusion is inserted just before old gate 1264, after the referenced $W$ coordinate is available.

Each retained old gate is mapped to a new gate with the same opcode. Its operands bind to an earlier retained gate, an unchanged declared coordinate, one of the 598 paid constant aliases, or one of the two proved fused outputs. There is no residual \code{C:} operand in the resulting source. The profile-cache closure described in Section~\ref{ptr:fu:sec:prefix} ensures the fused construction cannot silently append a late constant to the already copied prefix state.

\begin{proof}[Proof of Theorem~\ref{ptr:fu:thm:main}, equality and invariants]
All prefix aliases have the prescribed old integer values by the inherited occurrence and profile identities and the explicit paid constant chains. Proposition~\ref{ptr:fu:prop:dense} supplies exactly the old polynomial at each removed interval's final output. Induct on retained old gates: if all mapped operands have the old polynomial values, applying the unchanged opcode gives the same polynomial. The mapping is topological by construction and no removed interior value is referenced. The same induction verifies~\eqref{ptr:fu:eq:bg}, all later initializer consumers, all residual operands and every finalizer gate.

For $m=285$ in the two-input version, or $m=286$ in the one-input version, the retained finalizer computes each residual difference $r_i=a_i-b_i$, squares it, and sums all squares. Thus it retains $mM+(2m-1)A$, namely $285M+569A$ or $286M+571A$. Residual pairs are metadata, not extra asserted equations. The single final equation has that sum on the left and paid gate $0=1-1$ on the right. Coordinate declarations and positive domains are identical, so no new witness is introduced.

The old polynomial's exact degree is $4S=2,304,000$: its only degree-$2S$ residuals have leading homogeneous term $-W^{2S}$, giving leading final part $2W^{4S}$, as established in Report 44. Equality of the complete polynomial preserves that exact degree. We do not infer it from syntactic intermediate degrees of the factored source, which can ignore cancellations. The operation counts completing the theorem are derived in Section~\ref{ptr:fu:sec:ledger}.
\end{proof}

\section{Complete arithmetic ledger}\label{ptr:fu:sec:ledger}
Every stage count below is both a count of the specified grammar and a count reproduced by actual source generation. No giant coefficient integer is numerically instantiated.
\begingroup\small
\begin{longtable}{@{}lrrr@{}}\toprule
Stage&$M$&$A$&Total\\\midrule\endhead
Occurrence constants&5,469,985&8,186,999&13,656,984\\
Small profiles and basic literals&324,462&324,464&648,926\\
$G_Z$ and five fixed shifts&283&45&328\\
All 584 anchor rows&128,480&128,480&256,960\\
Remaining named constants&163&5&168\\\midrule
Retained constant prefix&5,923,373&8,639,993&14,563,366\\\midrule
$W^{600}$&12&0&12\\
Twelve short correction factors&7,188&7,188&14,376\\
Twenty-four long factors and products&23,040&23,038&46,078\\
Fixed blocks and geometric weights&15,911&15,714&31,625\\
Fused tile assembly&8&8&16\\\midrule
Fused spatial subtotal&46,159&45,948&92,107\\\midrule
Retained old main for two inputs&1,588&1,873&3,461\\\midrule
Complete two-input source&5,971,120&8,687,814&14,658,934\\
Complete one-input source&5,971,124&8,687,820&14,658,944\\\bottomrule
\end{longtable}\endgroup
The two-input old main had $2,307,457$ gates. Removing precisely $2,303,996$ leaves 3,461. The one-input pairing adds $3M+4A$ and its added residual costs another $1M+2A$ in the finalizer. Therefore the one-input remainder has $1,592M+1,879A=3,471$ gates, exactly ten more overall.

If the actually used outputs of the constant prefix are instead supplied as prescribed fixed coefficients, the remaining body has $92,107+3,461=95,568$ gates for two inputs and $95,578$ for one. This is a separate free-coefficient model: its allowed constants include the profile and occurrence values needed by the fused body, not just the 598 surviving old named ports. The main theorem and headline totals pay for all of those constants from literal 1 and 3.

The old Report 47 prefix's columnwise correction and tile-assembly stages cost respectively $6,454,008$ and $8,064,000$. These disappear, as do the two old spatial Horner intervals. They are replaced by the $92,107$ fused gates, giving the independent subtraction check
\[
 6\,454\,008+8\,064\,000+2\,303\,996-92\,107
 =16\,729\,897.
\]
All other prefix and main work remains paid. This is a unit-cost arithmetic-source comparison. It is not a bit-complexity or numerical runtime estimate: integer values can remain enormous, and a short straight-line program is not an efficient fully expanded numerical representation.

\section{Independent evidence and reproducibility}\label{ptr:fu:sec:evidence}
The evidence has four separate roles.
\begin{enumerate}
\item The generic proofs in Sections~\ref{ptr:fu:sec:cyclic}--\ref{ptr:fu:sec:assembly} establish ordinary polynomial identities for all coefficient substitutions and all $W$.
\item Exact finite-data checks identify the concrete masks, block partitions and profile-cache closure to which those identities apply.
\item Independent source reconstruction checks actual gates, topology, leaves, metadata, splice bindings, counts and hashes.
\item Modular differential cases provide regression tests. They supplement, rather than replace, the algebraic and exhaustive evidence.
\end{enumerate}
The independent audit~\cite{ptr:fu:audit} reports PASS. It verifies all 9,187,200 formal correction-term identities across four kinds, two phases, 957 pair positions and 1200 offsets, including zero columns. It checks all 6,912,000 fixed profile entries across the six arrays in both phases. Every block partition is disjoint and exhaustive, and the half-phase is verified as the exact 480-block rotation.

All 18 actual weight polynomials are evaluated as exact sparse polynomials in $\Z[W]$ and compared coefficientwise to $\sum_{k\in J}W^{600k}$. All ten geometric pairs are checked exactly. The independent adapter uses the two fixed intervals of Section~\ref{ptr:fu:sec:splice}, rather than the candidate's interval recognizer, validates every removed Horner record, rejects removed-interior uses, and reproduces both complete source streams. It verifies all 598 surviving ports, witness names and order, the 285/286 residual metadata records, paid zero and the sole final equation. All 95 frozen Report 47 files were unchanged in the audit's before/after hash inventory.

The author's supplementary checker verifies 2,296,800 exponent identities without repeating identical arithmetic for each kind, and the same 6,912,000 array entries. Seven dense modular comparisons use 3,828 independent synthetic occurrence values. The cases are $(q,W)=(1,000,003,0),(1,000,003,1),(1,000,003,-1),(1,000,003,2),(1,000,033,17),(2,1),(3,2)$. These are deliberately small finite-field evaluations, not full integer coefficient evaluations or a proof for all occurrence constants.

\paragraph{Canonical complete-source identities.}
Arithmetic records use the canonical tab-separated format identifier/opcode/operand/operand with one newline per gate. Metadata is compact JSONL. The source generator streams and hashes the actual complete source, without retaining its roughly 14.7 million records. An integer operand is an earlier gate identifier, never an extra integer literal. The two complete source hashes are
\paragraph{Two inputs.} \path{62cf59e79cd5d3baf61b19ed50ccc8965e8fb4814bbdc33a6d7fad417d379667}
\paragraph{One input.} \path{722340689ce84f505af7d010db21d6b48178c5d6008b28966e9836aeca75b25b}

Their final equations are respectively gate 14,658,933 = gate 0 and gate 14,658,943 = gate 0. The unchanged old-main target hashes and the input pins are included in the machine-readable receipts. A hash identifies the source bytes and supports reproducibility; it is not itself a mathematical proof.

\paragraph{Execution boundary and limits.}
The packet executes only inspected, authenticated owned arithmetic/profile code and new finite symbolic checks. It does not run upstream ant, physical-program, color-recipe or saved upstream schedule code. The compressed physical-program JSON is treated as fixed compilation data, not executed as a tape computation, and its saved row schedule is not used. The huge old literal coefficient prefix is not regenerated. Its equality with the new construction rests on the inherited coefficient theorem and the new identities. The checks do not re-prove arbitrary-integer Pell/history results or literal machine/ant dynamics; those inherited assumptions are unchanged.

\paragraph{Reproducible package.}
The accompanying ZIP contains the LaTeX source and PDF, exact frozen science, independent audit sources and receipts, a frozen Report 47 reference archive, provenance and release manifests, and isolated replay/build instructions. The release verifier authenticates the externally supplied manifest hash before running copied scientific code, uses fresh external output directories and disabled bytecode, and checks that original bytes, modes and modification times are preserved. PDF rebuilding disables shell escape and fixes the source epoch. The README supplies the exact offline commands and current release identities. Rebuilding the PDF requires a compatible local TeX Live installation; science replay requires Python 3. No package download or network execution is part of reproduction.

\section{Further questions suggested by this construction}\label{ptr:fu:sec:questions}
The result leaves concrete opportunities, none of which are included in the present count.
\begin{enumerate}
\item \textbf{Fuse the occurrence compiler more deeply.} The retained occurrence construction uses 13,656,984 gates, about 93\% of the new complete source. A direct bivariate or block-valued run construction might avoid materializing every $T_{a,s}$. It would need an exact ordinary-polynomial invariant at the cyclic boundaries and a paid source ledger.
\item \textbf{Share rotated factors algebraically.} The twenty-four long Horner expressions are exact and simple but do not exhaust possible sharing. Rotation formulas using prefixes and suffixes might lower their cost; division by $W$ and reduction modulo $W^S-1$ would not be valid substitutes for an ordinary-polynomial proof.
\item \textbf{Refine the paid profile and anchor grammar.} This source deliberately retains the complete old profile cache and all anchor rows. A narrower cache, shared addition chains or a proved anchor factorization may help, provided every remaining consumer and constant port is still paid and independently checked.
\item \textbf{Evaluate alternative band widths and block decompositions.} The present 600-column split matches physical slots. Other exact decompositions may trade short-factor cost against occurrence-factor length or cache reuse. Any improvement must include the new powers and geometric weights rather than treating them as free.
\item \textbf{Separate arithmetic size from execution cost.} Source-generation memory, validation time and bit complexity of numerical evaluation merit distinct analysis. A lower gate count alone does not settle any of them or establish a lower bound on the best possible source.
\end{enumerate}
The stopping point of this report is the independently reproduced 14,658,934/14,658,944-gate construction and its exact polynomial identity. The frozen earlier reports remain available as independently valid constructions and as the provenance of the inherited mathematical assumptions.

and 44 (\verb|\nolinkurl|) is \verb|\codeu|; \verb|\code| of Report~47
(an \verb|\mbox|) is \verb|\codem|; Report~47's \verb|\DUP|, \verb|\NAND|,
\verb|\ML|, \verb|\MR| are \verb|\sDUP|, \verb|\sNAND|, \verb|\sML|,
\verb|\sMR|. No mathematical symbol was renamed.
\item \emph{Duplicates.} The recognition theorem is printed once
(Theorem~\ref{ptr:pc:thm:complete}). The recalls of it in Report~47's
Section~\ref{ptr:sc:sec:result} and Report~48's
Section~\ref{ptr:fu:sec:inputs} are replaced by marked pointers that keep the
recalls' own remarks verbatim. Report~48's Lemma~\ref{ptr:fu:lem:geom}
(Report~47's Lemma~\ref{ptr:sc:lem:geometric} with $Y$ for $Z$, stated in
$\Z[Y]$) is kept as a marked second statement; Report~40's
Section~\ref{ptr:la:sec:lower} is marked as a summary re-proof of Part~I. No
other statement, proof, example, remark, non-claim or question of the five
manuscripts was removed or changed.
\item \emph{Notes.} The notes marked \wtag\ in Parts I--IV, this section and
the preface to the four Parts, and the \wtag\ annotations on bibliography
entries that name sibling reports.
\end{itemize}

\paragraph{Where the merge chose.} (1)~\emph{Host.} The five reports were
written into this report, not into a new one, because Report~40 answers
Part~I's question~3 nearly verbatim and the other four continue Report~40
alone; a new report \emph{periodic-langton-ant-certificates} was the
alternative considered. (2)~\emph{Parts.} Report~40 alone is Part~II;
Reports 42 and~44 form Part~III because~44 composes~42; Reports 47 and~48
form Part~IV because both refine the same polynomial's literal source. (3)~\emph{The
one theorem.} The recognition theorem is printed with Report~44, which
proves it; the smaller sources of Reports 47 and~48 and the programme's later
counts are listed beside it. (4)~\emph{Order.} The new Parts follow Part~I's
appendices and bibliography, so that no number of Part~I moves. (5)~\emph{Files.}
Byte copies of repository files, the five manuscripts, READMEs, PDFs and
checksum manifests, a Mathlib file and its licence, in-batch duplicates and
27 large regenerable files are not shipped; the report README lists every
one, the commands that rebuild the 27, and how to retrieve the archives.
